Algebraic Realisation of the Zamolodchikov Metric in Narain Theories
E.H Saidi, R. Sammani

Abstract
We revisit Narain conformal field theories from an algebraic perspective based on finite dimensional Lie algebras 𝐠 \mathbf{g} and representations ℛ 𝐠 \mathcal{R}_{\mathbf{g}} , and show how the root and weight lattices can encode the momenta and subsequently the partition functions of Narain theories. In this framework, we construct a realisation of the Zamolodchikov metric of the moduli space ℳ 𝐠 \mathcal{M}_{\mathbf{g}} in terms of Lie algebraic data namely the Cartan matrix K g and its inverse K − 1 𝐠 {}_{\mathbf{g}}^{-1} . Properties regarding the ensemble averaging of these CFTs and their holographic dual are also derived. Additionally, we discuss possible generalisations to NCFTs having dis-symmetric central charges ( c L , c R ) = ( s , r ) (\mathrm{c}_{L},\mathrm{c}_{R})=(\mathrm{s},\mathrm{r}) with s > r s>r and highlight further features of the partition function Z ( r , r ) 𝐠 {}_{\mathbf{g}}^{(r,r)} .



1
Introduction

Quantum gravity studies revealed surprising links between disparate
areas of quantum matter. One such connection suggests interrelations between
ensemble averaging
[
1
]
, code-based ensembles
[
2
,
3
]
, holography and Swampland program
[
4
,
5
,
6
]
with
regard to the no global symmetry conjecture
[
7
,
8
]
in quantum
gravity
[
9
]
. What makes this particularly fascinating is the concept
of ensemble averaging, firstly discovered for 2D Jackiw-Teitelboim (JT)
gravity
[
10
]
then extended to 3D
[
1
,
11
,
12
]
. It has been shown
that pure gravity in 2D is dual to random matrix theory
[
13
]
-
[
16
]

therefore stretching the usual one-to-one AdS/CFT correspondence to an AdS
2
dual to an ensemble average instead of one traditional CFT
1
. Adopting an up-down perspective and viewing JT gravity as the
dimensional reduction of 3D gravity
[
17
,
18
]
, one might wonder
whether this applies to AdS
3

[
19
]
-
[
21
]
as well.

Indeed three dimensional Chern-Simons (CS) U(1) gravity is dual to an
average over Narain conformal field theories (NCFT
2
) on compact torii

[
1
,
12
,
22
,
23
]
. The average is computed over Narain moduli
space parameterised with even integer lattices with a natural distance
measure given by the Zamolodchikov metric
[
24
]
. The density of states
is provided by the Siegel-Weil theorem
[
25
]
-
[
27
]
. Using spectral decomposition, the partition function uncovers two pieces:
one is moduli-dependent and another moduli-independent corresponding to the
average
[
28
]
-
[
30
]
.

From the bulk perspective, the average is equivalent to the sum over three
dimensional topologies. Since the theory is only defined perturbatively as
it is only checked on torus boundaries, the sum is carried out over
handlebodies
[
11
,
31
,
32
]
. Moreover, adopting diagonal boundary
conditions, the U(1) gravity can be reinterpreted as the asymptotic symmetry
of an AdS
3
gravity coupled to U(1) gauge fields
[
33
]
-
[
39
]
and the expected Brown-Henneaux boundary gravitons of ordinary
3d gravity
[
40
]
emerge as composites of the asymptotic symmetry.
Furthermore, by promoting the sl
(2,\mathbb{R})
algebra of AdS
3
to higher dimensional gauge symmetries, one can again recuperate the U(1)
algebra of the bulk theory dual to the ensemble average from higher spin
gravity
[
33
,
41
,
42
,
44
,
45
]
.

Generalisations of this theory can be drawn in several ways:
\mathbf{(a)}

by adding flavours to the partition function
[
31
]
. This follows from
considering fugacities, chemical potentials, conjugate to the U(1) charges.
\mathbf{(b)}
By constructing orbifolds of Narain CFTs
[
46
]
. The
process of orbifolding consists of architecting new conformal field theories
from established ones by gauging discrete symmetries of the original theory.
There are several incentives for considering orbifolds of Narain CFT but the
most compelling one in the context of ensemble averaging is that it allows
to gauge the global ensemble symmetries
[
46
]
which might serve in the
restoration of a healthy quantum gravity theory forbidding the presence of
global symmetries. And
\mathbf{(c)}
by extending Narain lattices to
general non-unimodular even or odd integer lattices
[
9
,
12
,
46
]
.

In the present investigation, we shall discuss both unorbifolded standard
and generalised Narain CFT and offer a systematic classification based on
finite dimensional Lie algebras
\mathbf{g}
and representations
\mathcal{R}_{\mathbf{g}}
. Building upon this, we give explicit realisations of the Zamolodchikov metric of the moduli space of these CFTs and show that it can be written in terms of the Cartan matrix and its inverse. We first consider Narain conformal field
theories (NCFT
2
) on compact torii
[
1
,
9
]
and their gravity dual
with symmetry
U(1)^{\mathrm{c}_{L}}\times U(1)^{\mathrm{c}_{R}}

[
33
,
47
]
. These CFT
2
s are given by strings compactified on
\mathbb{T}^{r}

with radii
\left\{R_{1},...,R_{r}\right\}
and underlying symmetry
U(1)_{L}^{\mathrm{r}}\times U(1)_{R}^{\mathrm{r}}.
There are two natural
parametrisations of these CFTs given by two real quadratic forms:
\mathcal{H}^{\left(r,r\right)}
and
\mathfrak{Q}^{\left(r,r\right)},
each
unveils an interesting structure:

\left(\mathbf{i}\right)
A local quadratic form
\mathcal{H}^{\left(r,r\right)}
which is a real function of variables
\left(\mathbf{x}\right)
coordinating the moduli space
\mathcal{M}_{\left(r,r\right)}

of the CFT (
{{}\mathchoice{\mbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle\mathchar 626$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\textstyle\mathchar 626$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle\mathchar 626$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle\mathchar 626$}}}_{\mathbf{x}}\mathcal{H}^{\left(r,r\right)}\neq 0
).
This is a positive function that can be imagined in terms of scaling
dimensions
\mathcal{H}=h+\bar{h}
of generic primary field operators
\Phi_{h,\bar{h}}
with conformal weight
(h,\bar{h})=(p_{L}^{2}/2,p_{R}^{2}/2)

and quantized momenta
p_{L/R}
. This
\mathcal{H}^{\left(r,r\right)}

expands like
\sum_{\text{{a}}=1}^{2r}
u
{}^{\text{{a}}}\mathbf{H}_{\text{{ab}}}
v
{}^{\text{{b}}}
with
characteristic matrix
\mathbf{H}_{\text{{ab}}}\left(\mathbf{x}\right)
capturing the local target space data of the two dimensional CFTs
and subsequently averaged data on effective field properties in the 3D bulk
dual.

\left(\mathbf{ii}\right)
A global real quadratic form
\mathfrak{Q}^{\left(r,r\right)}
that is independent of the moduli (
{{}\mathchoice{\mbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle\mathchar 626$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\textstyle\mathchar 626$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle\mathchar 626$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle\mathchar 626$}}}_{\mathbf{x}}\mathfrak{Q}^{\left(r,r\right)}=0
); thus capturing ”topological” data
of the bulk dual of the boundary CFT. This
\mathfrak{Q}^{\left(r,r\right)}
can be thought of in terms of generic conformal spins
h-\bar{h}
; and
quite similarly to
\mathcal{H}^{\left(r,r\right)}
, it has the typical
expansion
[
12
]

\mathfrak{Q}^{\left(r,r\right)}\left(\text{{u},{v}}\right)=\sum_{\text{{a}}=1}^{2r}\text{{u}}^{\text{{a}}}\mathbf{Q}_{\text{{ab}}}\text{{v}}^{\text{{b}}}\qquad,\qquad\mathfrak{Q}^{\left(r,r\right)}\left(\text{{u},{v}}\right)\in\mathbb{Z}

(1)

with discrete variables
u
{}^{\text{{a}}}=\left(n^{i},w_{a}\right)
having
r+r
integral components and characteristic
matrix
\mathbf{Q}_{\text{{ab}}}
. Here, the integer vector
\boldsymbol{n}:=\left(n_{1},...,n_{r}\right)
labels the Kaluza-Klein (KK)
excitations of the compactified string while the integral
\boldsymbol{w}:=\left(w_{1},...,w_{r}\right)
indexes the winding modes. The global
\mathfrak{Q}^{\left(r,r\right)}
is then characterised by the
2r\times 2r

square matrix
\mathbf{Q}_{\text{{ab}}}
splitting in 4 bloc
sub-matrices as

\mathbf{Q}_{\text{{ab}}}=\left(\begin{array}[]{cc}Q_{ij}&q_{i}^{\text{ }b}\\
\tilde{q}_{\text{ }j}^{a}&\tilde{Q}^{ab}\end{array}\right)

(2)

It also allows to define
an even integer lattice
\Lambda_{Q^{\left(r,r\right)}}
with quadratic
form
\mathfrak{Q}^{\left(r,r\right)}\left(\text{{u},{u}}\right)

\in 2\mathbb{Z}
as in eq(
1
).

Integrating over the moduli
\left(\mathbf{x}\right)
defines an
ensemble-averaged free CFT which is conjectured to be holographically dual
to an exotic three dimensional gravity with partition function
Z_{\text{{bulk}}}=Z_{\text{{cft}}}.
As we have stated, this gravity is believed to be given by a generalised Chern-Simons theory with
U\left(1\right)^{r}\times\tilde{U}\left(1\right)^{r}
gauge symmetry; its field
action
\mathcal{S}_{{\small CS}}
is expressed in terms of the 1-form CS
potentials
\mathcal{A}^{\text{{a}}}=\mathcal{A}_{{{}\mathchoice{\mbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle\mathchar 278$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\textstyle\mathchar 278$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle\mathchar 278$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle\mathchar 278$}}}}^{\text{{a}}}d
x
{}^{{{}\mathchoice{\mbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle\mathchar 278$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\textstyle\mathchar 278$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle\mathchar 278$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle\mathchar 278$}}}}
and their 2-form field strength curvatures

\mathcal{F}^{\text{{a}}}=d\mathcal{A}^{\text{{a}}};
it reads
approximately as follows
[
1
,
12
]

\mathcal{S}_{{\small CS}}=\frac{1}{8{{}\mathchoice{\mbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle\mathchar 281$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\textstyle\mathchar 281$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle\mathchar 281$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle\mathchar 281$}}}}\int_{\text{{Y}}_{3}}\mathfrak{Q}_{{\small CS}}^{\left(r,r\right)}\qquad,\qquad\mathfrak{Q}_{{\small CS}}^{\left(r,r\right)}=\sum_{\text{{a}}=1}^{2r}\mathcal{A}^{\text{{a}}}\mathbf{Q}_{\text{{ab}}}\mathcal{F}^{\text{{b}}}

(3)

with real threefold
Y
3
being a handlebody whose boundary is a
compact 2D surface; here a 2-torus
S_{t}^{1}\times S_{{{}\mathchoice{\mbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle\mathchar 283$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\textstyle\mathchar 283$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle\mathchar 283$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle\mathchar 283$}}}}^{1}
world
sheet. In this topological field action, the global coupling
\mathbf{Q}_{\text{{ab}}}
is the same matrix as in eq(
1
) and the quadratic
monomials
\mathcal{A}^{\text{{a}}}\mathcal{F}^{\text{{b}}}

refers to the 3-form
\mathcal{A}^{\text{{a}}}\wedge\mathcal{F}^{\text{{b}}}
.

In this paper, we give a classification of a family of two dimensional Narain conformal
field theories with central charges
\mathrm{c}_{L}=\mathrm{c}_{R}=r
and
their ensemble average in terms of finite dimensional Lie algebras
\mathbf{g}
. The various classes of these conformal field theories are labeled like

\text{NCFT}_{2}^{\mathbf{g}}\qquad,\qquad\mathbf{g}=\mathtt{A_{r},}\text{ }\mathtt{B_{r},}\text{ }\mathtt{C_{r},}\text{ }\mathtt{D_{r},}\text{ }\mathtt{E_{6,7,8},}\text{ }\mathtt{F}_{4}\mathtt{,}\text{ }\mathtt{G}_{2}

(4)

with
\mathbf{g}
including both simply and non simply laced finite Lie
algebras. Moreover, we investigate the genus-one partition function of these NCFT
{}_{2}^{\mathbf{g}}
s termed below as Z
{}_{\mathbf{g}}^{(r,r)}\left[{{}\mathchoice{\mbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle\mathchar 284$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\textstyle\mathchar 284$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle\mathchar 284$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle\mathchar 284$}}},\bar{{{}\mathchoice{\mbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle\mathchar 284$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\textstyle\mathchar 284$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle\mathchar 284$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle\mathchar 284$}}}};\mathbf{x}\right].
It factorises like
\left|{{}\mathchoice{\mbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle\mathchar 273$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\textstyle\mathchar 273$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle\mathchar 273$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle\mathchar 273$}}}\left({{}\mathchoice{\mbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle\mathchar 284$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\textstyle\mathchar 284$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle\mathchar 284$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle\mathchar 284$}}}\right)\right|^{-2r}\Theta_{\mathbf{g}}^{(r,r)}\left({{}\mathchoice{\mbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle\mathchar 284$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\textstyle\mathchar 284$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle\mathchar 284$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle\mathchar 284$}}},\bar{{{}\mathchoice{\mbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle\mathchar 284$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\textstyle\mathchar 284$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle\mathchar 284$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle\mathchar 284$}}}},\mathbf{x}\right)
with the usual Dedekin
{{}\mathchoice{\mbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle\mathchar 273$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\textstyle\mathchar 273$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle\mathchar 273$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle\mathchar 273$}}}\left({{}\mathchoice{\mbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle\mathchar 284$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\textstyle\mathchar 284$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle\mathchar 284$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle\mathchar 284$}}}\right)
and
the Siegel-Narain theta function
\Theta_{\mathbf{g}}^{(r,r)}
shown in
this study to be given by the following

\Theta_{\mathbf{g}}^{(r,r)}\left({{}\mathchoice{\mbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle\mathchar 284$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\textstyle\mathchar 284$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle\mathchar (5)

where the vectors
\mathbf{{{}\mathchoice{\mbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle\mathchar 268$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\textstyle\mathchar 268$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle\mathchar 268$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle\mathchar 268$}}}}=\sum_{i=1}^{r}n^{i}\mathbf{{{}\mathchoice{\mbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle\mathchar 268$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\textstyle\mathchar 268$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle\mathchar 268$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle\mathchar 268$}}}}_{i}

and
\textstyle\mathchar 287

=\sum_{j=1}^{r}w_{j}\mathbf{{{}\mathchoice{\mbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle\mathchar 287$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\textstyle\mathchar 287$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle\mathchar 287$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle\mathchar 287$}}}}^{j}

over the moduli space which is given by

kills the dependence on the moduli
\mathbf{x}
;
but manifests itself by some constants c

bearing the imprint

\mathcal{H}_{\mathbf{g}}^{\left(r,r\right)}

like
\mathcal{Z}_{\mathbf{g}}^{(r,r)}\left({{}\mathchoice{\mbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle\mathchar 284$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\textstyle\mathchar 284$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle\mathchar 284$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle\mathchar 284$}}},\bar{{{}\mathchoice{\mbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle\mathchar 284$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\textstyle\mathchar 284$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle\mathchar 284$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle\mathchar 284$}}}}\right)=\left|{{}\mathchoice{\mbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle\mathchar 273$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\textstyle\mathchar 273$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle\mathchar 273$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle\mathchar 273$}}}\left({{}\mathchoice{\mbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle\mathchar 284$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\textstyle\mathchar 284$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle\mathchar 284$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle\mathchar 284$}}}\right)\right|^{-2{\small r}}F_{\mathbf{g}}^{(r,r)}

>

. In this regard, we show in appendix C that the

) to give explicit realisations of the Zamolodchikov
