{}_{\mathbf{g}}^{2}
of the moduli space of these CFTs namely

\mathcal{M}_{\mathbf{g}}^{(r,r)}=\mathcal{O}(r,r;\mathbb{Z})\backslash\mathcal{O}(r,r;\mathbb{R})/[\mathcal{O}(r;\mathbb{R})\times\mathcal{O}(r;\mathbb{R})]

(6)

In particular, we show that this metric can be presented like ds
{}_{\mathbf{g}}^{2}=\sum_{i,k=1}^{r}\left(d{{}\mathchoice{\mbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle\mathchar 278$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\textstyle\mathchar 278$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle\mathchar 278$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle\mathchar 278$}}}_{\mathbf{g}}\right)_{i}^{k}\left(d{{}\mathchoice{\mbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle\mathchar 278$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\textstyle\mathchar 278$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle\mathchar 278$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle\mathchar 278$}}}_{\mathbf{g}}\right)_{k}^{i}
with
d{{}\mathchoice{\mbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle\mathchar 278$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\textstyle\mathchar 278$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle\mathchar 278$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle\mathchar 278$}}}_{\mathbf{g}}
realised in terms of
the Cartan matrix K
g
and K
{}_{\mathbf{g}}^{-1}
in addition to
the coordinates
\mathbf{x}
of
\mathcal{M}_{\mathbf{g}}^{(r,r)}
. As well,
we comment on the extension of our proposal to the so-called generalised
NCFTs whose moduli space
\mathcal{O}(s,r;\mathbb{Z})\backslash\mathcal{O}(s,r;\mathbb{R})/[\mathcal{O}(s;\mathbb{R})\times\mathcal{O}(r;\mathbb{R})]

with left central charge
\mathrm{c}_{L}
greater than the right
\mathrm{c}_{R}
(
s>r
).

The structure of the paper is as follows: In
section 2
, we describe
the set up of our proposal for the NCFT
{}^{\mathbf{su}_{2}}
and give its
basic properties including the partition function
Z_{\mathbf{su}_{2}}^{(1,1)}\left({{}\mathchoice{\mbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle\mathchar 284$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\textstyle\mathchar 284$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle\mathchar 284$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle\mathchar 284$}}}_{1},{{}\mathchoice{\mbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle\mathchar 284$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\textstyle\mathchar 284$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle\mathchar 284$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle\mathchar 284$}}}_{2},x\right)
and its ensemble
averaging. In
section 3
, we develop the proposal for NCFT
g
for rank r=2 Lie algebras which are given by su(3), so(5), sp(4) and G
2
with a particular focus on the simply laced case for explicit
calculations. In
section 4
, we provide results for NCFT
g
classified by finite dimensional Lie algebras
g
. As
illustrations, we describe leading members of the classes of Lie algebras.
In
section 5
, we conclude our study by summarising our main findings and comment on the so-called generalised Narain-CFTs. Then, we give three appendices:
Appendix A
concerns Narain
CFTs and gravitational dual.
Appendix B
regards Root
\Lambda_{r}^{\mathbf{g}}
and weight
\Lambda_{w}^{\mathbf{g}}
lattices of Lie algebras

\mathbf{g}
and their discriminant
\Lambda_{w}^{\mathbf{g}}/\Lambda_{r}^{\mathbf{g}}
.
Appendix C
deals with ensemble averaging, giving
details on the derivation of results presented in the main text and
exhibiting the footprint of
\mathbf{g}
and representations
\mathcal{R}_{\mathbf{g}}
in the averaged partition function
\mathcal{Z}_{\mathbf{g}}^{(r,r)}\left({{}\mathchoice{\mbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle\mathchar 284$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\textstyle\mathchar 284$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle\mathchar 284$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle\mathchar 284$}}},\bar{{{}\mathchoice{\mbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle\mathchar 284$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\textstyle\mathchar 284$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle\mathchar 284$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle\mathchar 284$}}}}\right)
.

2
Narain CFT
2
and Lie algebras

In this section, we establish the foundation for the classification of
standard Narain CFT
2
’s by studying the basic su(2) conformal model with
central charge
(\mathrm{c}_{L},\mathrm{c}_{R})=(\mathrm{1},\mathrm{1})

corresponding to the level
k=\tilde{k}=1
in

\mathrm{c}_{L}\left(k\right)=\frac{3k}{k+2}\qquad,\qquad\mathrm{c}_{R}(\tilde{k})=\frac{3\tilde{k}}{\tilde{k}+2}

(7)

We leverage this construction later to propose a classification of Narain
sigma-models with target r-torus
\mathbb{T}^{r}
leading to a CFT
2

with central charges
(\mathrm{c}_{L},\mathrm{c}_{R})=(\mathrm{r},\mathrm{r})
as described in (
4
). This classification will be comprehensively
addressed in the forthcoming sections 3 and 4.

2.1
Standard Narain CFT
{}_{2}^{\mathtt{su}_{2}}

The standard NCFT
{}_{2}^{\mathtt{su}_{2}}
is a subset of conformal field
theories; it concerns
\left(\mathbf{i}\right)
the compactification of a
closed string field
X=(X_{L}+X_{R})/\sqrt{2}
on circle
\mathbb{S}^{1}\simeq U\left(1\right)
with radius R (one 2D free boson), and
\left(\mathbf{ii}\right)
has quantized left/right momenta
\left(p_{L},p_{R}\right)
labeled by two integers n and w as in eq(
14
).
The moduli space of this conformal theory is one dimensional;

\mathcal{M}_{1}\simeq\mathcal{O}\left(1,1,\mathbb{R}\right)/\mathcal{O}\left(1,1,\mathbb{Z}\right)\qquad\dim\mathcal{M}_{1}=1

(8)

it is parameterized by
R
taking values in the range
0<R<\infty;
its
metric
ds_{\mathcal{M}_{1}}^{2}
is given by
\left(R^{-1}dR\right)^{2}

[
1
]
. By using the T-duality property
R\rightarrow 1/R
, the range of
the radius can be reduced to
1<R<\infty
indicating that
vol(\mathcal{M}_{1})
has infinite volume;
vol(\mathcal{M}_{1})\sim\int_{1}^{\infty}d\left(\log R\right)
behaving like
\left.\log R\right|_{1}^{\infty}
. For later use, we will think about the metric
ds_{\mathcal{M}_{1}}^{2}
like

ds_{\mathcal{M}_{1}}^{2}=\left(\mathcal{K}^{-1}d\mathcal{K}\right)^{2}=\left(-\mathcal{K}d\mathcal{K}^{-1}\right)^{2}

(9)

with
\mathcal{K}=2R^{2}
to be commented upon the presentation of our
proposal. Notice that under T-duality (
R\rightarrow 1/R
), we have the
mappings

\mathbb{S}^{1}

\quad\leftrightarrow\quad

\mathbb{\tilde{S}}^{1}

U\left(1\right)

\quad\leftrightarrow\quad

\tilde{U}\left(1\right)

(10)

which expand for left and right sectors
\mathbb{S}_{L/R}^{1}
as follows;
see also appendix C for further details,

\mathbb{S}_{L/R}^{1}

\quad\sim\quad

\mathbb{S}^{1}\pm\mathbb{\tilde{S}}^{1}

U\left(1\right)_{L/R}

\quad\sim\quad

U\left(1\right)\pm\tilde{U}\left(1\right)

(11)

In standard formulation of NCFT
{}_{2}^{\mathtt{su}_{2}}
, the momentum vector

\boldsymbol{p}
of the compactified string field on a circle is a real 2D
vector
\left(\mathfrak{p}^{\text{{\@text@baccent{m}}}}\right)\in\mathbb{R}^{1,1}
splitting like
\mathfrak{p}^{\text{{\@text@baccent{m}}}}=\left(p_{L},p_{R}\right)
. Its quantized values form a lattice
{\large\Lambda}_{\mathcal{M}_{1}}
contained in
\mathbb{R}^{1,1}
with pseudo-metric
{{}\mathchoice{\mbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle\mathchar 273$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\textstyle\mathchar 273$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle\mathchar 273$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle\mathchar 273$}}}_{\text{{\@text@baccent{m}\@text@baccent{n}}}}=diag(+,-).
This lattice is defined by the
condition on the spin
\left(p_{L}^{2}-p_{R}^{2}\right)/2\in\mathbb{Z}

that we write as follows

{\large\Lambda}_{\mathcal{M}_{1}}=\left\{\mathfrak{p}^{\text{{\@text@baccent{m}}}}\in\mathbb{R}^{1,1}\quad;\quad\mathfrak{p}^{\text{{\@text@baccent{m}}}}{{}\mathchoice{\mbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle\mathchar 273$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\textstyle\mathchar 273$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle\mathchar 273$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle\mathchar 273$}}}_{\text{{\@text@baccent{m}\@text@baccent{n}}}}\mathfrak{p}^{\text{{\@text@baccent{n}}}}\in 2\mathbb{Z}\right\}

(12)

where

\mathfrak{Q}_{\mathcal{M}_{1}}:=\mathfrak{p}^{\text{{\@text@baccent{m}}}}{{}\mathchoice{\mbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle\mathchar 273$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\textstyle\mathchar 273$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle\mathchar 273$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle\mathchar 273$}}}_{\text{{\@text@baccent{m}\@text@baccent{n}}}}\mathfrak{p}^{\text{{\@text@baccent{n}}}}=p_{L}^{2}-p_{R}^{2}

(13)

The values of the quantized
p_{L}
and
p_{R}
are labeled by two integers:
n and w; they are given by
[
12
,
48
,
58
]

\begin{tabular}[]{lllll}$p_{L}$&$=$&$\frac{n}{2R}+wR$&$\in$&$\mathbb{R}$\\
$p_{R}$&$=$&$\frac{n}{2R}-wR$&$\in$&$\mathbb{R}$\end{tabular}\qquad,\qquad\begin{tabular}[]{lll}$p_{L}$&$:=$&$\left(p_{L}\right)_{n,w}$\\
$p_{R}$&$:=$&$\left(p_{R}\right)_{n,w}$\end{tabular}

(14)

the integer
n
describes the Kaluza-Klein (KK) modes of the compactified
string and the parameter
w
defines the winding number. By setting
\mathfrak{p}^{\text{{\@text@baccent{m}}}}=E_{\text{.{a}}}^{\text{{\@text@baccent{m}}}}m^{\text{{a}}}
and
m^{\text{{a}}}=(n,w),
we have

E_{\text{ {a}}}^{\text{{\@text@baccent{m}}}}=\left(\begin{array}[]{cc}\frac{1}{2R}&R\\
\frac{1}{2R}&-R\end{array}\right)

(15)

with modulus independent determinant
\det E_{\text{ {a}}}^{\text{{\@text@baccent{m}}}}=-1
and global quadratic form as

\mathfrak{Q}_{\mathcal{M}_{1}}=m^{\text{{a}}}\mathbf{Q}_{\text{{ab}}}m^{\text{{b}}}\qquad,\qquad\mathbf{Q}_{\text{{ab}}}=E_{\text{{a}}}^{\text{ {\@text@baccent{m}}}}{{}\mathchoice{\mbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle\mathchar 273$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\textstyle\mathchar 273$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle\mathchar 273$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle\mathchar 273$}}}_{\text{{\@text@baccent{m}\@text@baccent{n}}}}E_{\text{ {b}}}^{\text{{\@text@baccent{n}}}}

(16)

The matrix
\mathbf{Q}_{\text{{ab}}}
is invariant under
\mathcal{O}(1,1,\mathbb{R})/\left[\mathcal{O}(1)\times\mathcal{O}(1)\right]
and the
integral form
m^{\text{{a}}}\mathbf{Q}_{\text{{ab}}}m^{\text{{b}}}
is unchanging under
\mathcal{O}(1,1,\mathbb{Z}).

Using eq(
14
), we calculate the useful relations

\begin{tabular}[]{lll}$p_{L}^{2}$&$=$&$\frac{n^{2}}{4R^{2}}+nw+w^{2}R^{2}$\\
$p_{R}^{2}$&$=$&$\frac{n^{2}}{4R^{2}}-nw+w^{2}R^{2}$\end{tabular}\qquad\Rightarrow\qquad\begin{tabular}[]{lll}$p_{L}^{2}+p_{R}^{2}$&$=$&$\frac{n^{2}}{2R^{2}}+2w^{2}R^{2}$\\
$p_{L}^{2}-p_{R}^{2}$&$=$&$2nw$\end{tabular}

(17)

showing that
\mathfrak{Q}_{\mathcal{M}_{1}}=p_{L}^{2}-p_{R}^{2}
is given
by the
coupling
of KK modes {n} and the winding {w}. Moreover, in
terms of
\mathfrak{p}^{\text{{\@text@baccent{m}}}}=(p_{L},p_{R}),
the global
quadratic form
\mathfrak{Q}_{\mathcal{M}_{1}}:=\mathfrak{Q}\left(\mathfrak{p},\mathfrak{p}\right)
can be formulated like

\mathfrak{Q}_{\mathcal{M}_{1}}=\mathfrak{p}^{\text{{\@text@baccent{m}}}}{{}\mathchoice{\mbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle\mathchar 273$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\textstyle\mathchar 273$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle\mathchar 273$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle\mathchar 273$}}}_{\text{{\@text@baccent{m}\@text@baccent{n}}}}\mathfrak{p}^{\text{{\@text@baccent{n}}}}=2nw

(18)

In addition to
\mathfrak{Q}_{\mathcal{M}_{1}},
we also have a twin
quadratic form
\mathcal{H}_{\mathcal{M}_{1}}:=\mathcal{H}\left(\mathfrak{p},\mathfrak{p}\right)
given by

\mathcal{H}_{\mathcal{M}_{1}}:=p_{L}^{2}+p_{R}^{2}\geq 0

(19)

it is invariant under
\mathcal{O}\left(1,\mathbb{R}\right)_{L}\times\mathcal{O}\left(1,\mathbb{R}\right)_{R}
with action
p_{L}\rightarrow\pm p_{L}
and
p_{R}\rightarrow\pm p_{R}
. These
\mathfrak{Q}_{\mathcal{M}_{1}}
and
\mathcal{H}_{\mathcal{M}_{1}}
constitute basic ingredients
required for the derivation of the properties of NCFT
{}_{2}^{\mathbf{su}_{2}};
in particular its partition function
Z\left({{}\mathchoice{\mbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle\mathchar 284$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\textstyle\mathchar 284$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle\mathchar 284$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle\mathchar 284$}}},\bar{{{}\mathchoice{\mbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle\mathchar 284$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\textstyle\mathchar 284$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle\mathchar 284$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle\mathchar 284$}}}},R\right)
, its average
\left\langle Z\left({{}\mathchoice{\mbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle\mathchar 284$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\textstyle\mathchar 284$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle\mathchar 284$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle\mathchar 284$}}},\bar{{{}\mathchoice{\mbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle\mathchar 284$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\textstyle\mathchar 284$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle\mathchar 284$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle\mathchar 284$}}}},R\right)\right\rangle_{\mathcal{M}_{1}}
and its holographic dual Z
grav
.

Equipped with these tools, we proceed to present our proposal for NCFT
{}_{2}^{\mathbf{su}_{2}};
to be extended later for higher dimensional Lie algebras
beyond su(2).

2.2
Refined Narain CFT
{}_{2}^{\mathtt{su}_{2}}

In pursuit of classifying Narain CFT
2
’s and their generalisations first considered in
[
10
,
12
]
, we give below a refined parametrisation of (
14
) by involving both the simple root
\textstyle\mathchar 267
(root lattice) and the fundamental weight
\textstyle\mathchar 277
vector (weight lattice) of the su(2) Lie algebra. The Lie group SU(2)
associated with this algebra is imagined here in terms of the 3-sphere

SU(2)\simeq\mathbb{S}^{3}\qquad,\qquad\mathbb{S}^{3}\simeq\mathbb{S}^{1}\times\mathbb{S}^{2}\qquad,\qquad\mathbb{S}^{1}\simeq U(1)

(20)

Below, the fiber
\mathbb{S}^{1}
(with radius R) will be regarded as
Narain’s circle of string compactification; as for the base
\mathbb{S}^{2},

it will be used for geometric interpretations.

2.2.1
NCFT
{}_{2}^{\mathtt{su}_{2}}
at the point
R=1/2

The refined parametrisation we propose for the NCFT
{}_{2}^{\mathtt{su}_{2}}

description can be introduced in two steps, starting from a special point of

\mathcal{M}_{1}
as follows:

\left(\mathbf{1}\right)

Consider first the particular case of a
circle
\mathbb{S}^{1}
with radius
R=1/2
; this choice corresponds to
sitting at a particular point in the moduli space
\left]0,\infty\right[

that can be split like

\mathcal{M}_{1}\simeq\left[0,1\right]\cup\left]1,\infty\right[

(21)

which we designate as
\mathcal{M}_{\mathtt{su}_{2}}.
By substituting into (
14
), we get the local values

\begin{tabular}[]{lll}$p_{L}$&$=$&$n+\frac{1}{2}w$\\
$p_{R}$&$=$&$n-\frac{1}{2}w$\end{tabular}\qquad\Rightarrow\qquad\begin{tabular}[]{lll}$p_{L}^{2}+p_{R}^{2}$&$=$&$2n^{2}+\frac{1}{2}w^{2}$\\
$p_{L}^{2}-p_{R}^{2}$&$=$&$2nw$\end{tabular}

(22)

with
\mathfrak{p}^{\text{{\@text@baccent{m}}}}=E_{\text{.{a}}}^{\text{{\@text@baccent{m}}}}m^{\text{{a}}}
and Zweibein as follows

E_{\text{ {a}}}^{\text{{\@text@baccent{m}}}}=\left(\begin{array}[]{cc}1&+\frac{1}{2}\\
1&-\frac{1}{2}\end{array}\right)\qquad,\qquad\mathbf{Q}_{\text{{ab}}}=E_{\text{{a}}}^{\text{ {\@text@baccent{m}}}}{{}\mathchoice{\mbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle\mathchar 273$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\textstyle\mathchar 273$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle\mathchar 273$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle\mathchar 273$}}}_{\text{{\@text@baccent{m}\@text@baccent{n}}}}E_{\text{
{b}}}^{\text{{\@text@baccent{n}}}}=\left(\begin{array}[]{cc}0&1\\
1&0\end{array}\right)

(23)

The generic dependence on the modulus R in (
15
) will be restored
later on.

\left(\mathbf{2}\right)

Think about the above left
p_{L}
and
the right
p_{R}
components of momentum (
22
) in terms of the vectors
\boldsymbol{p}_{L}
and
\boldsymbol{p}_{R}
valued in the weight lattice of
su(2) and given by the following linear combinations

\boldsymbol{p}_{L}

=

\frac{1}{\sqrt{2}}\left(n\mathbf{{{}\mathchoice{\mbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle\mathchar 267$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\textstyle\mathchar 267$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle\mathchar 267$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle\mathchar 267$}}}}+w\mathbf{{{}\mathchoice{\mbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle\mathchar 277$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\textstyle\mathchar 277$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle\mathchar 277$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle\mathchar 277$}}}}\right)

\boldsymbol{p}_{R}

=

\frac{1}{\sqrt{2}}\left(n\mathbf{{{}\mathchoice{\mbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle\mathchar 267$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\textstyle\mathchar 267$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle\mathchar 267$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle\mathchar 267$}}}}-w\mathbf{{{}\mathchoice{\mbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle\mathchar 277$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\textstyle\mathchar 277$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle\mathchar 277$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle\mathchar 277$}}}}\right)

(24)

Here, the
\textstyle\mathchar 267
and the
\textstyle\mathchar 277
are respectively
the simple root and the fundamental weight of su(2). From (
24
), we
calculate

\boldsymbol{p}_{L}^{2}

=

\frac{1}{2}\left(n^{2}\mathbf{{{}\mathchoice{\mbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle\mathchar 267$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\textstyle\mathchar 267$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle\mathchar 267$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle\mathchar 267$}}}}^{2}+w^{2}\mathbf{{{}\mathchoice{\mbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle\mathchar 277$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\textstyle\mathchar 277$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle\mathchar 277$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle\mathchar 277$}}}}^{2}+2nw\mathbf{{{}\mathchoice{\mbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle\mathchar 267$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\textstyle\mathchar 267$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle\mathchar 267$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle\mathchar 267$}}}.{{}\mathchoice{\mbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle\mathchar 277$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\textstyle\mathchar 277$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle\mathchar 277$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle\mathchar 277$}}}}\right)

\boldsymbol{p}_{R}^{2}

=

\frac{1}{2}\left(n^{2}\mathbf{{{}\mathchoice{\mbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle\mathchar 267$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\textstyle\mathchar 267$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle\mathchar 267$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle\mathchar 267$}}}}^{2}+w^{2}\mathbf{{{}\mathchoice{\mbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle\mathchar 277$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\textstyle\mathchar 277$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle\mathchar 277$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle\mathchar 277$}}}}^{2}-2nw\mathbf{{{}\mathchoice{\mbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle\mathchar 267$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\textstyle\mathchar 267$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle\mathchar 267$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle\mathchar 267$}}}.{{}\mathchoice{\mbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle\mathchar 277$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\textstyle\mathchar 277$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle\mathchar 277$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle\mathchar 277$}}}}\right)

(25)

and then

\boldsymbol{p}_{L}^{2}+\boldsymbol{p}_{R}^{2}

=

n^{2}\mathbf{{{}\mathchoice{\mbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle\mathchar 267$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\textstyle\mathchar 267$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle\mathchar 267$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle\mathchar 267$}}}}^{2}+w^{2}\mathbf{{{}\mathchoice{\mbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle\mathchar 277$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\textstyle\mathchar 277$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle\mathchar 277$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle\mathchar 277$}}}}^{2}

:=

\mathcal{H}_{\mathtt{su}_{2}}

\boldsymbol{p}_{L}^{2}-\boldsymbol{p}_{R}^{2}

=

2nw\mathbf{{{}\mathchoice{\mbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle\mathchar 267$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\textstyle\mathchar 267$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle\mathchar 267$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle\mathchar 267$}}}.{{}\mathchoice{\mbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle\mathchar 277$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\textstyle\mathchar 277$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle\mathchar 277$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle\mathchar 277$}}}}

:=

\mathfrak{Q}_{\mathtt{su}_{2}}

(26)

By substituting
\mathbf{{{}\mathchoice{\mbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle\mathchar 267$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\textstyle\mathchar 267$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle\mathchar 267$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle\mathchar 267$}}}}^{2}=2
and
\mathbf{{{}\mathchoice{\mbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle\mathchar 277$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\textstyle\mathchar 277$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle\mathchar 277$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle\mathchar 277$}}}}^{2}=1/2
as
well as the usual duality
\mathbf{{{}\mathchoice{\mbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle\mathchar 267$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\textstyle\mathchar 267$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle\mathchar 267$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle\mathchar 267$}}}.{{}\mathchoice{\mbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle\mathchar 277$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\textstyle\mathchar 277$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle\mathchar 277$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle\mathchar 277$}}}}=1
, we recover
precisely the relations (
22
).

A) Two comments

\left(\mathbf{i}\right)

From eq(
26
), we observe that the
root/weight duality relation
\mathbf{{{}\mathchoice{\mbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle\mathchar 267$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\textstyle\mathchar 267$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle\mathchar 267$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle\mathchar 267$}}}.{{}\mathchoice{\mbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle\mathchar 277$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\textstyle\mathchar 277$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle\mathchar 277$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle\mathchar 277$}}}}=1
in the su(2)
algebra captures indeed the
coupling
between the KK and the winding
modes given by

\mathfrak{Q}_{\mathtt{su}_{2}}=2\left(n\mathbf{{{}\mathchoice{\mbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle\mathchar 267$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\textstyle\mathchar 267$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle\mathchar 267$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle\mathchar 267$}}}}\right)\mathbf{.}\left(w\mathbf{{{}\mathchoice{\mbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle\mathchar 277$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\textstyle\mathchar 277$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle\mathchar 277$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle\mathchar 277$}}}}\right)

(27)

For later generalisation, notice that the root/weight duality in finite Lie
algebras reads in general like
\mathbf{{{}\mathchoice{\mbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle\mathchar 267$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\textstyle\mathchar 267$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle\mathchar 267$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle\mathchar 267$}}}}_{i}^{\vee}\mathbf{.{{}\mathchoice{\mbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle\mathchar 277$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\textstyle\mathchar 277$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle\mathchar 277$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle\mathchar 277$}}}}^{j}={{}\mathchoice{\mbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle\mathchar 270$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\textstyle\mathchar 270$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle\mathchar 270$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle\mathchar 270$}}}_{i}^{j}
where the simple co-root
\mathbf{{{}\mathchoice{\mbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle\mathchar 267$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\textstyle\mathchar 267$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle\mathchar 267$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle\mathchar 267$}}}}_{i}^{\vee}
is defined as
2\mathbf{{{}\mathchoice{\mbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle\mathchar 267$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\textstyle\mathchar 267$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle\mathchar 267$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle\mathchar 267$}}}}_{i}/\mathbf{{{}\mathchoice{\mbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle\mathchar 267$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\textstyle\mathchar 267$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle\mathchar 267$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle\mathchar 267$}}}}_{i}^{2}.
For
simply laced Lie algebra such as su(r+1), we have
\mathbf{{{}\mathchoice{\mbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle\mathchar 267$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\textstyle\mathchar 267$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle\mathchar 267$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle\mathchar 267$}}}}_{i}^{2}=2
implying
\mathbf{{{}\mathchoice{\mbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle\mathchar 267$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\textstyle\mathchar 267$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle\mathchar 267$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle\mathchar 267$}}}}_{i}^{\vee}=\mathbf{{{}\mathchoice{\mbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle\mathchar 267$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\textstyle\mathchar 267$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle\mathchar 267$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle\mathchar 267$}}}}_{i}.

This property also applies for the so(2r) and exceptional E
r
Lie
algebras but does not hold for non simply laced B
r
, C
r
, F
4

and G
2
.

\left(\mathbf{ii}\right)

In our proposal, the zweibein
E_{\text{
{a}}}^{\text{{\@text@baccent{m}}}}
given by eq(
23
) reads formally
as follows

\boldsymbol{E}_{\text{ {a}}}^{\text{{\@text@baccent{m}}}}=\frac{1}{\sqrt{2}}\left(\begin{array}[]{cc}\mathbf{{{}\mathchoice{\mbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle\mathchar 267$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\textstyle\mathchar 267$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle\mathchar 267$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle\mathchar 267$}}}}&\mathbf{{{}\mathchoice{\mbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle\mathchar 277$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\textstyle\mathchar 277$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle\mathchar 277$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle\mathchar 277$}}}}\\
\mathbf{{{}\mathchoice{\mbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle\mathchar 267$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\textstyle\mathchar 267$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle\mathchar 267$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle\mathchar 267$}}}}&-\mathbf{{{}\mathchoice{\mbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle\mathchar 277$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\textstyle\mathchar 277$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle\mathchar 277$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle\mathchar 277$}}}}\end{array}\right)

(28)

with

\mathbf{Q}_{\text{{ab}}}=\boldsymbol{E}_{\text{{a}}}^{\text{
{\@text@baccent{m}}}}{{}\mathchoice{\mbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle\mathchar 273$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\textstyle\mathchar 273$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle\mathchar 273$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle\mathchar 273$}}}_{\text{{\@text@baccent{m}\@text@baccent{n}}}}\boldsymbol{E}_{\text{
{b}}}^{\text{{\@text@baccent{n}}}}

(29)

invariant under the
\mathcal{O}(1,1)
action on the zweibein
\boldsymbol{E}_{\text{ {b}}}^{\text{{\@text@baccent{n}}}}
via orthogonal matrix
transformations
\mathcal{O}_{\text{ {\@text@baccent{k}}}}^{\text{{\@text@baccent{n}}}}
which act as
\mathcal{O}_{\text{ {\@text@baccent{k}}}}^{\text{{\@text@baccent{n}}}}\boldsymbol{E}_{\text{ {b}}}^{\text{{\@text@baccent{k}}}}
and preserve
{{}\mathchoice{\mbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle\mathchar 273$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\textstyle\mathchar 273$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle\mathchar 273$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle\mathchar 273$}}}_{\text{{\@text@baccent{m}\@text@baccent{n}}}};
that is (
\mathcal{O}^{T})_{\text{{\@text@baccent{m}}}}^{\text{ {\@text@baccent{p}}}}{{}\mathchoice{\mbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle\mathchar 273$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\textstyle\mathchar 273$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle\mathchar 273$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle\mathchar 273$}}}_{\text{{\@text@baccent{p}\@text@baccent{q}}}}\mathcal{O}_{\text{ {\@text@baccent{n}}}}^{\text{{\@text@baccent{q}}}}={{}\mathchoice{\mbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle\mathchar 273$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\textstyle\mathchar 273$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle\mathchar 273$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle\mathchar 273$}}}_{\text{{\@text@baccent{m}\@text@baccent{n}}}}.

B) Quadratic forms and su(2) Lie algebra

Focussing on eq(
26
), we learn that
\mathfrak{Q}_{L}^{\mathtt{su}_{2}}:=\boldsymbol{p}_{L}^{2}
and
\mathfrak{Q}_{R}^{\mathtt{su}_{2}}:=\boldsymbol{p}_{R}^{2}
are quadratic forms that can be also presented
like
\mathfrak{Q}_{L/R}^{\mathtt{su}_{2}}=\boldsymbol{m}^{T}.\boldsymbol{Q}_{L/R}^{\mathtt{su}_{2}}.\boldsymbol{m}
where
\boldsymbol{m}^{T}=\left(n,w\right)
; they are fully defined by the 2
\times
2 matrices
\boldsymbol{Q}_{L/R}^{\mathtt{su}_{2}}
given by

\boldsymbol{Q}_{L}^{\mathtt{su}_{2}}=\frac{1}{2}\left(\begin{array}[]{cc}\mathbf{{{}\mathchoice{\mbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle\mathchar 267$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\textstyle\mathchar 267$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle\mathchar 267$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle\mathchar 267$}}}.{{}\mathchoice{\mbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle\mathchar 267$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\textstyle\mathchar 267$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle\mathchar 267$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle\mathchar 267$}}}}&\mathbf{{{}\mathchoice{\mbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle\mathchar 267$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\textstyle\mathchar 267$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle\mathchar 267$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle\mathchar 267$}}}.{{}\mathchoice{\mbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle\mathchar 277$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\textstyle\mathchar 277$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle\mathchar 277$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle\mathchar 277$}}}}\\
\mathbf{{{}\mathchoice{\mbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle\mathchar 277$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\textstyle\mathchar 277$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle\mathchar 277$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle\mathchar 277$}}}.{{}\mathchoice{\mbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle\mathchar 267$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\textstyle\mathchar 267$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle\mathchar 267$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle\mathchar 267$}}}}&\mathbf{{{}\mathchoice{\mbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle\mathchar 277$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\textstyle\mathchar 277$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle\mathchar 277$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle\mathchar 277$}}}.{{}\mathchoice{\mbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle\mathchar 277$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\textstyle\mathchar 277$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle\mathchar 277$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle\mathchar 277$}}}}\end{array}\right)\qquad,\qquad\boldsymbol{Q}_{R}^{\mathtt{su}_{2}}=\frac{1}{2}\left(\begin{array}[]{cc}\mathbf{{{}\mathchoice{\mbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle\mathchar 267$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\textstyle\mathchar 267$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle\mathchar 267$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle\mathchar 267$}}}.{{}\mathchoice{\mbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle\mathchar 267$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\textstyle\mathchar 267$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle\mathchar 267$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle\mathchar 267$}}}}&-\mathbf{{{}\mathchoice{\mbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle\mathchar 267$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\textstyle\mathchar 267$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle\mathchar 267$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle\mathchar 267$}}}.{{}\mathchoice{\mbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle\mathchar 277$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\textstyle\mathchar 277$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle\mathchar 277$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle\mathchar 277$}}}}\\
-\mathbf{{{}\mathchoice{\mbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle\mathchar 277$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\textstyle\mathchar 277$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle\mathchar 277$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle\mathchar 277$}}}.{{}\mathchoice{\mbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle\mathchar 267$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\textstyle\mathchar 267$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle\mathchar 267$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle\mathchar 267$}}}}&\mathbf{{{}\mathchoice{\mbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle\mathchar 277$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\textstyle\mathchar 277$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle\mathchar 277$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle\mathchar 277$}}}.{{}\mathchoice{\mbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle\mathchar 277$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\textstyle\mathchar 277$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle\mathchar 277$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle\mathchar 277$}}}}\end{array}\right)

(30)

By setting

\mathcal{H}_{\mathtt{su}_{2}}

=

\mathfrak{Q}_{L}^{\mathtt{su}_{2}}+\mathfrak{Q}_{R}^{\mathtt{su}_{2}}

\mathfrak{Q}_{\mathtt{su}_{2}}

=

\mathfrak{Q}_{L}^{\mathtt{su}_{2}}-\mathfrak{Q}_{R}^{\mathtt{su}_{2}}

(31)

and in bold the matrices

\mathbf{H}_{\mathtt{su}_{2}}

=

\boldsymbol{Q}_{L}^{\mathtt{su}_{2}}+\boldsymbol{Q}_{R}^{\mathtt{su}_{2}}

\mathbf{Q}_{\mathtt{su}_{2}}

=

\boldsymbol{Q}_{L}^{\mathtt{su}_{2}}-\boldsymbol{Q}_{R}^{\mathtt{su}_{2}}

(32)

we end up with two interesting matrices

\boldsymbol{H}_{\mathtt{su}_{2}}=\left(\begin{array}[]{cc}\mathbf{{{}\mathchoice{\mbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle\mathchar 267$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\textstyle\mathchar 267$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle\mathchar 267$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle\mathchar 267$}}}}^{2}&\mathbf{0}\\
\mathbf{0}&\mathbf{{{}\mathchoice{\mbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle\mathchar 277$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\textstyle\mathchar 277$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle\mathchar 277$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle\mathchar 277$}}}}^{2}\end{array}\right)\qquad,\qquad\mathbf{Q}_{\mathtt{su}_{2}}=\left(\begin{array}[]{cc}0&\mathbf{{{}\mathchoice{\mbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle\mathchar 267$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\textstyle\mathchar 267$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle\mathchar 267$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle\mathchar 267$}}}.{{}\mathchoice{\mbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle\mathchar 277$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\textstyle\mathchar 277$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle\mathchar 277$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle\mathchar 277$}}}}\\
\mathbf{{{}\mathchoice{\mbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle\mathchar 277$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\textstyle\mathchar 277$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle\mathchar 277$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle\mathchar 277$}}}.{{}\mathchoice{\mbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle\mathchar 267$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\textstyle\mathchar 267$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle\mathchar 267$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle\mathchar 267$}}}}&0\end{array}\right)

(33)

where
\boldsymbol{H}_{\mathtt{su}_{2}}
is diagonal and
\mathbf{Q}_{\mathtt{su}_{2}}
off diagonal. The form of the matrix
\mathbf{Q}_{\mathtt{su}_{2}}
is particularly insightful:
\left(\mathbf{i}\right)
it has
vanishing diagonal entries
\mathbf{Q}_{ii}^{\mathtt{su}_{2}}=0
(even
integer); and as such it captures only the intersection between the cycles
\textstyle\mathchar 267
and
\textstyle\mathchar 277
.
\left(\mathbf{ii}\right)

Because
\mathbf{{{}\mathchoice{\mbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle\mathchar 267$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\textstyle\mathchar 267$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle\mathchar 267$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle\mathchar 267$}}}.{{}\mathchoice{\mbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle\mathchar 277$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\textstyle\mathchar 277$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle\mathchar 277$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle\mathchar 277$}}}}=1
, it results that

\left|\det\mathbf{Q}_{\mathtt{su}_{2}}\right|=\left|-\left(\mathbf{{{}\mathchoice{\mbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle\mathchar 267$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\textstyle\mathchar 267$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle\mathchar 267$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle\mathchar 267$}}}.{{}\mathchoice{\mbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle\mathchar 277$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\textstyle\mathchar 277$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle\mathchar 277$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle\mathchar 277$}}}}\right)^{2}\right|=1

(34)

showing that
\mathbf{Q}_{\mathtt{su}_{2}}
describes an
even unimodular lattice. As anticipated by eq(
18
), we indeed have
\mathfrak{Q}_{\mathtt{su}_{2}}=\mathfrak{p}^{\text{{\@text@baccent{m}}}}{{}\mathchoice{\mbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle\mathchar 273$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\textstyle\mathchar 273$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle\mathchar 273$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle\mathchar 273$}}}_{\text{{\@text@baccent{m}\@text@baccent{n}}}}\mathfrak{p}^{\text{{\@text@baccent{n}}}}=2nw
which
in our proposal reads as follows

\mathfrak{Q}_{\mathtt{su}_{2}}=\boldsymbol{m}^{T}.\boldsymbol{Q}_{\mathtt{su}_{2}}.\boldsymbol{m}\qquad,\qquad\mathfrak{Q}_{\mathtt{su}_{2}}=\mathfrak{Q}_{\mathtt{su}_{2}}\left(\boldsymbol{m},\boldsymbol{m}\right)

(35)

indicating that the lattice (
12
) is isomorphic to
{\large\Lambda}_{\mathtt{su}_{2}}
with metric
{\large g}_{\text{{ab}}}

given by
\boldsymbol{Q}_{\text{{ab}}}^{{}_{\mathtt{su}_{{\small 2}}}}

up to an SL(2,
\mathbb{Z}
) transformation. Explicitly, we have

{\large\Lambda}_{\mathtt{su}_{2}}=\left\{\boldsymbol{m}^{\text{{a}}}\in\mathbb{Z}^{2}\quad;\quad\mathfrak{Q}_{\mathtt{su}_{2}}=\boldsymbol{m}^{\text{{a}}}{\large g}_{\text{{ab}}}\boldsymbol{m}^{\text{{b}}}\in 2\mathbb{Z}\right\}

(36)

with

{\large g}_{\text{{ab}}}=\boldsymbol{Q}_{\text{{ab}}}^{{}_{\mathtt{su}_{{\small 2}}}}

(37)

In addition to these relationships, and due to the property
\mathbf{{{}\mathchoice{\mbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle\mathchar 267$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\textstyle\mathchar 267$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle\mathchar 267$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle\mathchar 267$}}}}^{2}=2,
the fundamental weight can be expressed like
\mathbf{{{}\mathchoice{\mbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle\mathchar 277$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\textstyle\mathchar 277$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle\mathchar 277$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle\mathchar 277$}}}}=\frac{1}{2}\mathbf{{{}\mathchoice{\mbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle\mathchar 267$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\textstyle\mathchar 267$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle\mathchar 267$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle\mathchar 267$}}}}
implying a length
\mathbf{{{}\mathchoice{\mbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle\mathchar 277$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\textstyle\mathchar 277$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle\mathchar 277$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle\mathchar 277$}}}}^{2}=1/2;

which is simply the inverse of
\mathbf{{{}\mathchoice{\mbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle\mathchar 267$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\textstyle\mathchar 267$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle\mathchar 267$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle\mathchar 267$}}}}^{2}\mathbf{.}
Substituting

\mathbf{{{}\mathchoice{\mbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle\mathchar 267$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\textstyle\mathchar 267$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle\mathchar 267$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle\mathchar 267$}}}}=2\mathbf{{{}\mathchoice{\mbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle\mathchar 277$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\textstyle\mathchar 277$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle\mathchar 277$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle\mathchar 277$}}}}
in (
24
), we find

\begin{tabular}[]{lll}$\boldsymbol{p}_{L}$&$=$&$\frac{1}{\sqrt{2}}\left(2n+w\right)\mathbf{{{}\mathchoice{\mbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle\mathchar 277$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\textstyle\mathchar 277$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle\mathchar 277$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle\mathchar 277$}}}}$\\
$\boldsymbol{p}_{R}$&$=$&$\frac{1}{\sqrt{2}}\left(2n-w\right)\mathbf{{{}\mathchoice{\mbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle\mathchar 277$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\textstyle\mathchar 277$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle\mathchar 277$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle\mathchar 277$}}}}$\end{tabular}\qquad,\qquad\begin{tabular}[]{lll}$\boldsymbol{p}_{L}.\mathbf{{{}\mathchoice{\mbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle\mathchar 267$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\textstyle\mathchar 267$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle\mathchar 267$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle\mathchar 267$}}}}$&$=$&$\frac{1}{\sqrt{2}}\left(2n+w\right)$\\
$\boldsymbol{p}_{R}.\mathbf{{{}\mathchoice{\mbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle\mathchar 267$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\textstyle\mathchar 267$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle\mathchar 267$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle\mathchar 267$}}}}$&$=$&$\frac{1}{\sqrt{2}}\left(2n-w\right)$\end{tabular}

(38)

we deduce that
\left(\mathbf{i}\right)
the
\boldsymbol{p}_{L}
and the
\boldsymbol{p}_{R}
determine left/right shifts of the KK momentum
\boldsymbol{p}_{(n)}=2n\mathbf{{{}\mathchoice{\mbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle\mathchar 277$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\textstyle\mathchar 277$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle\mathchar 277$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle\mathchar 277$}}}}
by the amounts
\pm w\mathbf{{{}\mathchoice{\mbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle\mathchar 277$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\textstyle\mathchar 277$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle\mathchar 277$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle\mathchar 277$}}}}
arising from windings; and
\left(\mathbf{ii}\right)
with
regard to the factorisation
\mathfrak{Q}_{\mathtt{su}_{2}}=\mathfrak{p}^{\text{{\@text@baccent{m}}}}{{}\mathchoice{\mbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle\mathchar 273$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\textstyle\mathchar 273$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle\mathchar 273$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle\mathchar 273$}}}_{\text{{\@text@baccent{m}\@text@baccent{n}}}}\mathfrak{p}^{\text{{\@text@baccent{n}}}}
of eq(
18
), we have

\mathfrak{p}^{\text{{\@text@baccent{m}}}}=\left(\begin{array}[]{c}\frac{1}{\sqrt{2}}\boldsymbol{p}_{L}.\mathbf{{{}\mathchoice{\mbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle\mathchar 267$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\textstyle\mathchar 267$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle\mathchar 267$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle\mathchar 267$}}}}\\
\frac{1}{\sqrt{2}}\boldsymbol{p}_{R}.\mathbf{{{}\mathchoice{\mbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle\mathchar 267$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\textstyle\mathchar 267$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle\mathchar 267$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle\mathchar 267$}}}}\end{array}\right)=\left(\begin{array}[]{c}\frac{2}{\sqrt{2}}\boldsymbol{p}_{L}.\mathbf{{{}\mathchoice{\mbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle\mathchar 277$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\textstyle\mathchar 277$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle\mathchar 277$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle\mathchar 277$}}}}\\
\frac{2}{\sqrt{2}}\boldsymbol{p}_{R}.\mathbf{{{}\mathchoice{\mbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle\mathchar 277$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\textstyle\mathchar 277$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle\mathchar 277$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle\mathchar 277$}}}}\end{array}\right)

(39)

2.2.2
NCFT
{}_{2}^{\mathtt{su}_{2}}
at generic values of radius R

In the refined proposal (
24
), we set
R=1/2
and used
\left(\mathbf{a}\right)
the simple root
\textstyle\mathchar 267
of su(2) with length
\mathbf{{{}\mathchoice{\mbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle\mathchar 267$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\textstyle\mathchar 267$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle\mathchar 267$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle\mathchar 267$}}}}^{2}=2;
and
\left(\mathbf{b}\right)
the associated fundamental
weight
\textstyle\mathchar 277
with length
\mathbf{{{}\mathchoice{\mbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle\mathchar 277$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\textstyle\mathchar 277$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle\mathchar 277$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle\mathchar 277$}}}}^{2}=1/2.
For
generic values of R, imagined in what follows in terms of the positive
variable
x=1/\left(2R\right)
, we consider the scaled vector
\mathbf{{{}\mathchoice{\mbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle\mathchar 268$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\textstyle\mathchar 268$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle\mathchar 268$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle\mathchar 268$}}}}=x\mathbf{{{}\mathchoice{\mbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle\mathchar 267$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\textstyle\mathchar 267$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle\mathchar 267$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle\mathchar 267$}}}}
instead of
\mathbf{{{}\mathchoice{\mbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle\mathchar 267$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\textstyle\mathchar 267$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle\mathchar 267$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle\mathchar 267$}}};}
and the scaled
weight
\mathbf{{{}\mathchoice{\mbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle\mathchar 287$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\textstyle\mathchar 287$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle\mathchar 287$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle\mathchar 287$}}}}=

\mathbf{{{}\mathchoice{\mbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle\mathchar 277$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\textstyle\mathchar 277$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle\mathchar 277$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle\mathchar 277$}}}}/x
satisfying the duality
relation
\mathbf{{{}\mathchoice{\mbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle\mathchar 268$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\textstyle\mathchar 268$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle\mathchar 268$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle\mathchar 268$}}}}.\mathbf{{{}\mathchoice{\mbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle\mathchar 287$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\textstyle\mathchar 287$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle\mathchar 287$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle\mathchar 287$}}}}=1
with

\mathbf{{{}\mathchoice{\mbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle\mathchar 268$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\textstyle\mathchar 268$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle\mathchar 268$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle\mathchar 268$}}}}\left(x\right)

=

\frac{1}{(2R)}\mathbf{{{}\mathchoice{\mbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle\mathchar 267$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\textstyle\mathchar 267$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle\mathchar 267$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle\mathchar 267$}}}}

\mathbf{:}=

x\mathbf{{{}\mathchoice{\mbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle\mathchar 267$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\textstyle\mathchar 267$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle\mathchar 267$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle\mathchar 267$}}}}

\mathbf{{{}\mathchoice{\mbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle\mathchar 287$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\textstyle\mathchar 287$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle\mathchar 287$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle\mathchar 287$}}}}\left(x\right)

=

\left(2R\right)\mathbf{{{}\mathchoice{\mbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle\mathchar 277$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\textstyle\mathchar 277$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle\mathchar 277$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle\mathchar 277$}}}}

\mathbf{:}=

\frac{1}{x}\mathbf{{{}\mathchoice{\mbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle\mathchar 277$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\textstyle\mathchar 277$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle\mathchar 277$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle\mathchar 277$}}}}

(40)

and the lengths

\mathbf{{{}\mathchoice{\mbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle\mathchar 268$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\textstyle\mathchar 268$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle\mathchar 268$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle\mathchar 268$}}}}^{2}\left(x\right)=2x^{2}\qquad,\qquad\mathbf{{{}\mathchoice{\mbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle\mathchar 287$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\textstyle\mathchar 287$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle\mathchar 287$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle\mathchar 287$}}}}^{2}\left(x\right)=\frac{1}{2x^{2}}

(41)

With these scaled cycles, the proposal (
24
) extends as follows

\boldsymbol{p}_{L}\left(x\right)

=

\frac{1}{\sqrt{2}}\left(n\mathbf{{{}\mathchoice{\mbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle\mathchar 268$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\textstyle\mathchar 268$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle\mathchar 268$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle\mathchar 268$}}}}+w\mathbf{{{}\mathchoice{\mbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle\mathchar 287$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\textstyle\mathchar 287$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle\mathchar 287$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle\mathchar 287$}}}}\right)

\boldsymbol{p}_{R}\left(x\right)

=

\frac{1}{\sqrt{2}}\left(n\mathbf{{{}\mathchoice{\mbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle\mathchar 268$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\textstyle\mathchar 268$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle\mathchar 268$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle\mathchar 268$}}}}-w\mathbf{{{}\mathchoice{\mbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle\mathchar 287$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\textstyle\mathchar 287$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle\mathchar 287$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle\mathchar 287$}}}}\right)

(42)

They are function of the modulus
x.
For this generic case, eqs(
33
)
get mapped into , :

\boldsymbol{H}_{\mathtt{su}_{2}}\left(x\right)

=

\left(\begin{array}[]{cc}x^{2}\mathbf{{{}\mathchoice{\mbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle\mathchar 267$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\textstyle\mathchar 267$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle\mathchar 267$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle\mathchar 267$}}}}^{2}&\mathbf{0}\\
\mathbf{0}&\frac{1}{x^{2}}\mathbf{{{}\mathchoice{\mbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle\mathchar 277$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\textstyle\mathchar 277$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle\mathchar 277$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle\mathchar 277$}}}}^{2}\end{array}\right)

=

\left(\begin{array}[]{cc}2x^{2}&\mathbf{0}\\
\mathbf{0}&\frac{1}{2x^{2}}\end{array}\right)

\mathbf{Q}_{\mathtt{su}_{2}}\left(x\right)

=

\left(\begin{array}[]{cc}0&\mathbf{{{}\mathchoice{\mbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle\mathchar 268$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\textstyle\mathchar 268$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle\mathchar 268$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle\mathchar 268$}}}.{{}\mathchoice{\mbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle\mathchar 287$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\textstyle\mathchar 287$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle\mathchar 287$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle\mathchar 287$}}}}\\
\mathbf{{{}\mathchoice{\mbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle\mathchar 287$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\textstyle\mathchar 287$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle\mathchar 287$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle\mathchar 287$}}}.{{}\mathchoice{\mbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle\mathchar 268$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\textstyle\mathchar 268$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle\mathchar 268$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle\mathchar 268$}}}}&0\end{array}\right)

=

\left(\begin{array}[]{cc}0&\mathbf{{{}\mathchoice{\mbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle\mathchar 267$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\textstyle\mathchar 267$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle\mathchar 267$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle\mathchar 267$}}}.{{}\mathchoice{\mbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle\mathchar 277$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\textstyle\mathchar 277$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle\mathchar 277$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle\mathchar 277$}}}}\\
\mathbf{{{}\mathchoice{\mbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle\mathchar 277$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\textstyle\mathchar 277$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle\mathchar 277$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle\mathchar 277$}}}.{{}\mathchoice{\mbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle\mathchar 267$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\textstyle\mathchar 267$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle\mathchar 267$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle\mathchar 267$}}}}&0\end{array}\right)

(43)

from which we learn that
\left(\mathbf{i}\right)
the
\boldsymbol{H}_{\mathtt{su}_{2}}
is a function of the modulus x with
\det\boldsymbol{H}_{\mathtt{su}_{2}}=1
and
\mathbf{Q}_{\mathtt{su}_{2}}^{-1}\boldsymbol{H}_{\mathtt{su}_{2}}\mathbf{Q}_{\mathtt{su}_{2}}=\boldsymbol{H}_{\mathtt{su}_{2}}^{-1}
, while
\left(\mathbf{ii}\right)
the
\mathbf{Q}_{\mathtt{su}_{2}}
is x- independent and unimodular

\left|\det\mathbf{Q}_{\mathtt{su}_{2}}\right|=\left|-\left(\mathbf{{{}\mathchoice{\mbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle\mathchar 268$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\textstyle\mathchar 268$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle\mathchar 268$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle\mathchar 268$}}}.{{}\mathchoice{\mbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle\mathchar 287$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\textstyle\mathchar 287$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle\mathchar 287$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle\mathchar 287$}}}}\right)^{2}\right|=\left|-\left(\mathbf{{{}\mathchoice{\mbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle\mathchar 267$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\textstyle\mathchar 267$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle\mathchar 267$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle\mathchar 267$}}}.{{}\mathchoice{\mbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle\mathchar 277$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\textstyle\mathchar 277$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle\mathchar 277$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle\mathchar 277$}}}}\right)^{2}\right|=1

(44)

In what follows, we will often perform calculations at the point
x=1
in
the moduli space of the Narain CFT
{}_{2}^{(\mathtt{su}_{2})}
. The
implementation of the modulus
x
is straightforward and will be applied
when needed for interpretations.

2.3
Results for NCFT
{}_{2}^{\mathtt{su}_{2}}

To summarize the above description within the framework of our proposal (
24
) and (
42
), we collect below characteristic results on NCFT
{}_{2}^{\mathtt{su}_{2}}
in the following list of points:

\left(\mathbf{1}\right)

Masses as volume of cycles
: The
positive quadratic form
\mathcal{H}_{\mathtt{su}_{2}}=\boldsymbol{p}_{L}^{2}+\boldsymbol{p}_{R}^{2}
is given by the sum of the self
intersections of

\left(\mathbf{i}\right)
the SU(2)
{}_{\mathbf{{{}\mathchoice{\mbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle\mathchar 268$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\textstyle\mathchar 268$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle\mathchar 268$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle\mathchar 268$}}}}}
- cycle
\mathcal{C}_{\mathbf{{{}\mathchoice{\mbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle\mathchar 268$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\textstyle\mathchar 268$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle\mathchar 268$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle\mathchar 268$}}}}}
generated by the scaled simple root
\mathbf{{{}\mathchoice{\mbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle\mathchar 268$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\textstyle\mathchar 268$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle\mathchar 268$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle\mathchar 268$}}}}=x\mathbf{{{}\mathchoice{\mbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle\mathchar 267$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\textstyle\mathchar 267$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle\mathchar 267$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle\mathchar 267$}}}};
and

\left(\mathbf{ii}\right)
the SU(2)
{}_{\mathbf{{{}\mathchoice{\mbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle\mathchar 287$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\textstyle\mathchar 287$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle\mathchar 287$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle\mathchar 287$}}}}}
- cycle
\mathcal{C}_{\mathbf{{{}\mathchoice{\mbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle\mathchar 287$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\textstyle\mathchar 287$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle\mathchar 287$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle\mathchar 287$}}}}}
spanned by the scaled fundamental weight
\mathbf{{{}\mathchoice{\mbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle\mathchar 287$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\textstyle\mathchar 287$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle\mathchar 287$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle\mathchar 287$}}}}=x^{-1}\mathbf{{{}\mathchoice{\mbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle\mathchar 277$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\textstyle\mathchar 277$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle\mathchar 277$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle\mathchar 277$}}}}.

We have

\mathcal{C}_{\mathbf{{{}\mathchoice{\mbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle\mathchar 268$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\textstyle\mathchar 268$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle\mathchar 268$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle\mathchar 268$}}}}}.\mathcal{C}_{\mathbf{{{}\mathchoice{\mbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle\mathchar 268$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\textstyle\mathchar 268$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle\mathchar 268$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle\mathchar 268$}}}}}

=

\mathbf{{{}\mathchoice{\mbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle\mathchar 268$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\textstyle\mathchar 268$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle\mathchar 268$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle\mathchar 268$}}}.{{}\mathchoice{\mbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle\mathchar 268$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\textstyle\mathchar 268$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle\mathchar 268$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle\mathchar 268$}}}}

=

x^{2}\mathbf{{{}\mathchoice{\mbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle\mathchar 267$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\textstyle\mathchar 267$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle\mathchar 267$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle\mathchar 267$}}}}\mathbf{.}\mathbf{{{}\mathchoice{\mbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle\mathchar 267$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\textstyle\mathchar 267$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle\mathchar 267$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle\mathchar 267$}}}}

=

x^{2}\mathcal{C}_{\mathbf{{{}\mathchoice{\mbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle\mathchar 267$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\textstyle\mathchar 267$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle\mathchar 267$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle\mathchar 267$}}}}}.\mathcal{C}_{\mathbf{{{}\mathchoice{\mbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle\mathchar 267$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\textstyle\mathchar 267$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle\mathchar 267$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle\mathchar 267$}}}}}

\mathcal{C}_{\mathbf{{{}\mathchoice{\mbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle\mathchar 287$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\textstyle\mathchar 287$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle\mathchar 287$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle\mathchar 287$}}}}}.\mathcal{C}_{\mathbf{{{}\mathchoice{\mbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle\mathchar 287$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\textstyle\mathchar 287$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle\mathchar 287$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle\mathchar 287$}}}}}

=

\mathbf{{{}\mathchoice{\mbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle\mathchar 287$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\textstyle\mathchar 287$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle\mathchar 287$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle\mathchar 287$}}}.{{}\mathchoice{\mbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle\mathchar 287$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\textstyle\mathchar 287$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle\mathchar 287$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle\mathchar 287$}}}}

=

x^{-2}\mathbf{{{}\mathchoice{\mbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle\mathchar 277$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\textstyle\mathchar 277$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle\mathchar 277$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle\mathchar 277$}}}}\mathbf{.}\mathbf{{{}\mathchoice{\mbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle\mathchar 277$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\textstyle\mathchar 277$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle\mathchar 277$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle\mathchar 277$}}}}

=

x^{-2}\mathcal{C}_{\mathbf{{{}\mathchoice{\mbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle\mathchar 277$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\textstyle\mathchar 277$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle\mathchar 277$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle\mathchar 277$}}}}}.\mathcal{C}_{\mathbf{{{}\mathchoice{\mbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle\mathchar 277$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\textstyle\mathchar 277$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle\mathchar 277$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle\mathchar 277$}}}}}

(45)

implying

Area\left(\mathcal{C}_{\mathbf{{{}\mathchoice{\mbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle\mathchar 268$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\textstyle\mathchar 268$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle\mathchar 268$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle\mathchar 268$}}}}}\right)=2x^{2}\qquad,\qquad Area\left(\mathcal{C}_{\mathbf{{{}\mathchoice{\mbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle\mathchar 287$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\textstyle\mathchar 287$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle\mathchar 287$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle\mathchar 287$}}}}}\right)=\frac{1}{2x^{2}}

(46)

By thinking of
\boldsymbol{p}_{L/R}^{2}
in terms of squared masses
\mathbf{{{}\mathchoice{\mbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle\mathchar 278$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\textstyle\mathchar 278$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle\mathchar 278$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle\mathchar 278$}}}}_{L/R}^{2},
the
\mathcal{H}_{su_{2}}
is, physically
speaking, nothing but the total squared mass
\mathbf{{{}\mathchoice{\mbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle\mathchar 278$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\textstyle\mathchar 278$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle\mathchar 278$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle\mathchar 278$}}}}_{tot}^{2}=\mathbf{{{}\mathchoice{\mbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle\mathchar 278$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\textstyle\mathchar 278$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle\mathchar 278$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle\mathchar 278$}}}}_{L}^{2}+\mathbf{{{}\mathchoice{\mbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle\mathchar 278$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\textstyle\mathchar 278$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle\mathchar 278$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle\mathchar 278$}}}}_{R}^{2}
of the KK and the winding
modes. Substituting
\boldsymbol{p}_{L/R}^{2}
by their values, we obtain

\mathbf{{{}\mathchoice{\mbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle\mathchar 278$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\textstyle\mathchar 278$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle\mathchar 278$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle\mathchar 278$}}}}_{tot}^{2}=2x^{2}+\frac{1}{2x^{2}}\qquad\Leftrightarrow\qquad\mathbf{{{}\mathchoice{\mbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle\mathchar 278$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\textstyle\mathchar 278$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle\mathchar 278$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle\mathchar 278$}}}}_{tot}^{2}=\mathcal{C}_{\mathbf{{{}\mathchoice{\mbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle\mathchar 268$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\textstyle\mathchar 268$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle\mathchar 268$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle\mathchar 268$}}}}}^{2}+\mathcal{C}_{\mathbf{{{}\mathchoice{\mbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle\mathchar 287$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\textstyle\mathchar 287$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle\mathchar 287$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle\mathchar 287$}}}}}^{2}

(47)

\left(\mathbf{2}\right)

Gap energy as intersection of
cycles
: The relative quantity
\mathfrak{Q}_{\mathtt{su}_{2}}=\boldsymbol{p}_{L}^{2}-\boldsymbol{p}_{R}^{2}
, which is equal to
2\left(n\mathbf{{{}\mathchoice{\mbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle\mathchar 268$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\textstyle\mathchar 268$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle\mathchar 268$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle\mathchar 268$}}}}\right).\left(w\mathbf{{{}\mathchoice{\mbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle\mathchar 287$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\textstyle\mathchar 287$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle\mathchar 287$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle\mathchar 287$}}}}\right)
, is given by the intersection
between the cycle
n\mathcal{C}_{\mathbf{{{}\mathchoice{\mbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle\mathchar 268$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\textstyle\mathchar 268$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle\mathchar 268$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle\mathchar 268$}}}}}
and the cycle
w\mathcal{C}_{\mathbf{{{}\mathchoice{\mbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle\mathchar 287$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\textstyle\mathchar 287$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle\mathchar 287$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle\mathchar 287$}}}}}.
We have

\mathcal{C}_{\mathbf{{{}\mathchoice{\mbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle\mathchar 268$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\textstyle\mathchar 268$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle\mathchar 268$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle\mathchar 268$}}}}}.\mathcal{C}_{\mathbf{{{}\mathchoice{\mbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle\mathchar 287$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\textstyle\mathchar 287$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle\mathchar 287$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle\mathchar 287$}}}}}=\mathbf{{{}\mathchoice{\mbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle\mathchar 268$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\textstyle\mathchar 268$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle\mathchar 268$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle\mathchar 268$}}}.{{}\mathchoice{\mbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle\mathchar 287$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\textstyle\mathchar 287$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle\mathchar 287$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle\mathchar 287$}}}}=\mathbf{{{}\mathchoice{\mbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle\mathchar 267$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\textstyle\mathchar 267$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle\mathchar 267$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle\mathchar 267$}}}.{{}\mathchoice{\mbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle\mathchar 277$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\textstyle\mathchar 277$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle\mathchar 277$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle\mathchar 277$}}}}=\mathcal{C}_{\mathbf{{{}\mathchoice{\mbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle\mathchar 267$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\textstyle\mathchar 267$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle\mathchar 267$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle\mathchar 267$}}}}}.\mathcal{C}_{\mathbf{{{}\mathchoice{\mbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle\mathchar 277$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\textstyle\mathchar 277$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle\mathchar 277$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle\mathchar 277$}}}}}=1

(48)

Physically, this represents the gap mass
\mathbf{{{}\mathchoice{\mbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle\mathchar 278$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\textstyle\mathchar 278$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle\mathchar 278$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle\mathchar 278$}}}}_{L}^{2}-\mathbf{{{}\mathchoice{\mbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle\mathchar 278$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\textstyle\mathchar 278$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle\mathchar 278$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle\mathchar 278$}}}}_{R}^{2}
between the left and the right moving particle states. We denote
this gap by
{{}\mathchoice{\mbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle\mathchar 271$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\textstyle\mathchar 271$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle\mathchar 271$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle\mathchar 271$}}}_{gap}^{\mathtt{su}_{2}}
following the terminology
used in the physics of topological insulators and semiconductors
[
50
,
51
]
; it is given by
{{}\mathchoice{\mbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle\mathchar 271$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\textstyle\mathchar 271$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle\mathchar 271$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle\mathchar 271$}}}_{gap}^{\mathtt{su}_{2}}=2n\times w.
Notice that a vanishing gap
{{}\mathchoice{\mbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle\mathchar 271$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\textstyle\mathchar 271$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle\mathchar 271$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle\mathchar 271$}}}_{gap}^{\mathtt{su}_{2}}=0

corresponds to the equality

\mathbf{{{}\mathchoice{\mbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle\mathchar 278$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\textstyle\mathchar 278$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle\mathchar 278$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle\mathchar 278$}}}}_{L}^{2}=\mathbf{{{}\mathchoice{\mbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle\mathchar 278$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\textstyle\mathchar 278$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle\mathchar 278$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle\mathchar 278$}}}}_{R}^{2}

(49)

and the constraint
n\times w=0
which can be satisfied either by the KK
zero mode
n=0
or the winding zero mode
w=0.
From the perspective of (
48
), we have two possibilities
\left(\mathbf{i}\right)
either the
contraction
\mathcal{C}_{\mathbf{{{}\mathchoice{\mbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle\mathchar 268$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\textstyle\mathchar 268$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle\mathchar 268$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle\mathchar 268$}}}}}\rightarrow 0
with the
decompactification
\mathcal{C}_{\mathbf{{{}\mathchoice{\mbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle\mathchar 287$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\textstyle\mathchar 287$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle\mathchar 287$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle\mathchar 287$}}}}}\rightarrow\infty;
or
\left(\mathbf{ii}\right)
the decompactification
\mathcal{C}_{\mathbf{{{}\mathchoice{\mbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle\mathchar 268$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\textstyle\mathchar 268$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle\mathchar 268$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle\mathchar 268$}}}}}\rightarrow\infty
with the contraction
\mathcal{C}_{\mathbf{{{}\mathchoice{\mbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle\mathchar 287$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\textstyle\mathchar 287$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle\mathchar 287$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle\mathchar 287$}}}}}\rightarrow 0

[
52
]
.

\left(\mathbf{3}\right)

Root

\Lambda_{\mathrm{r}}^{\mathtt{su}_{2}}

and weight

\Lambda_{\mathrm{w}}^{\mathtt{su}_{2}}

lattices
: By using the canonical
variables
\boldsymbol{p}_{1}=\left(\boldsymbol{p}_{L}+\boldsymbol{p}_{R}\right)/\sqrt{2}
and
\boldsymbol{p}_{2}=\left(\boldsymbol{p}_{L}-\boldsymbol{p}_{R}\right)/\sqrt{2}
in addition to substituting
\boldsymbol{p}_{L}
and
\boldsymbol{p}_{R}
by their values, we end up with
new momentum expressions that exhibit the dependencies on both the weight
vector
\textstyle\mathchar 277
and the simple root
\mathbf{{{}\mathchoice{\mbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle\mathchar 267$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\textstyle\mathchar 267$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle\mathchar 267$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle\mathchar 267$}}}},

\begin{tabular}[]{lll}$\boldsymbol{p}_{1}$&$=$&$n\mathbf{{{}\mathchoice{\mbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle\mathchar 267$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\textstyle\mathchar 267$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle\mathchar 267$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle\mathchar 267$}}}}$\\
$\boldsymbol{p}_{2}$&$=$&$w\mathbf{{{}\mathchoice{\mbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle\mathchar 277$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\textstyle\mathchar 277$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle\mathchar 277$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle\mathchar 277$}}}}$\end{tabular}\qquad,\qquad\begin{tabular}[]{lll}$n$&$=$&$\boldsymbol{p}_{1}.\mathbf{{{}\mathchoice{\mbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle\mathchar 277$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\textstyle\mathchar 277$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle\mathchar 277$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle\mathchar 277$}}}}$\\
$w$&$=$&$\boldsymbol{p}_{2}.\mathbf{{{}\mathchoice{\mbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle\mathchar 267$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\textstyle\mathchar 267$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle\mathchar 267$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle\mathchar 267$}}}}$\end{tabular}

(50)

indicating in turn that the free fields
X_{L}
and
X_{R}
can be similarly
promoted to vectors
\boldsymbol{X}_{L}
and
\boldsymbol{X}_{R}
with
components
U
and

along the
\textstyle\mathchar 267
and
\textstyle\mathchar 277

directions as follows

\begin{tabular}[]{lll}$\boldsymbol{X}_{L}$&$=$&$\frac{1}{\sqrt{2}}\left(U\mathbf{{{}\mathchoice{\mbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle\mathchar 267$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\textstyle\mathchar 267$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle\mathchar 267$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle\mathchar 267$}}}}+V\mathbf{{{}\mathchoice{\mbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle\mathchar 277$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\textstyle\mathchar 277$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle\mathchar 277$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle\mathchar 277$}}}}\right)$\\
$\boldsymbol{X}_{R}$&$=$&$\frac{1}{\sqrt{2}}\left(U\mathbf{{{}\mathchoice{\mbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle\mathchar 267$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\textstyle\mathchar 267$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle\mathchar 267$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle\mathchar 267$}}}}-V\mathbf{{{}\mathchoice{\mbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle\mathchar 277$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\textstyle\mathchar 277$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle\mathchar 277$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle\mathchar 277$}}}}\right)$\end{tabular}\qquad,\qquad\begin{tabular}[]{lll}$U\mathbf{{{}\mathchoice{\mbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle\mathchar 267$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\textstyle\mathchar 267$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle\mathchar 267$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle\mathchar 267$}}}}$&$=$&$\frac{1}{\sqrt{2}}\left(\boldsymbol{X}_{L}+\boldsymbol{X}_{R}\right)$\\
$V\mathbf{{{}\mathchoice{\mbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle\mathchar 277$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\textstyle\mathchar 277$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle\mathchar 277$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle\mathchar 277$}}}}$&$=$&$\frac{1}{\sqrt{2}}\left(\boldsymbol{X}_{L}-\boldsymbol{X}_{R}\right)$\end{tabular}

(51)

and metric

\mathcal{G}_{\text{{ab}}}=\left(\begin{array}[]{cc}2x^{2}&0\\
0&\frac{1}{2x^{2}}\end{array}\right)=\left(\begin{array}[]{cc}\frac{1}{2R^{2}}&0\\
0&2R^{2}\end{array}\right)

(52)

See appendix C for the full derivation of this metric and other
T-duality properties. Also, they show that
\boldsymbol{p}_{1}
labels
precisely the set
\Lambda_{\mathrm{r}}^{\mathtt{su}_{2}}
which is the
su(2) root lattice while
\boldsymbol{p}_{2}
parameterises
\Lambda_{\mathrm{w}}^{\mathtt{su}_{2}}
defining the weight lattice. By using (
39
), we also have

\mathfrak{p}^{\text{{\@text@baccent{m}}}}=\left(\begin{array}[]{c}n+\frac{1}{2}w\\
n-\frac{1}{2}w\end{array}\right)

(53)

with even integer
\mathfrak{Q}_{\mathtt{su}_{2}}=\mathfrak{p}^{\text{{\@text@baccent{m}}}}{{}\mathchoice{\mbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle\mathchar 273$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\textstyle\mathchar 273$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle\mathchar 273$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle\mathchar 273$}}}_{\text{{\@text@baccent{m}\@text@baccent{n}}}}\mathfrak{p}^{\text{{\@text@baccent{n}}}}
. From the relations (
50
) with
\mathbf{{{}\mathchoice{\mbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle\mathchar 267$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\textstyle\mathchar 267$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle\mathchar 267$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle\mathchar 267$}}}}=2\mathbf{{{}\mathchoice{\mbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle\mathchar 277$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\textstyle\mathchar 277$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle\mathchar 277$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle\mathchar 277$}}}}
, we learn that the weight lattice
\Lambda_{\mathrm{w}}^{\mathtt{su}_{2}},
with sites
\boldsymbol{p}_{2}=w\mathbf{{{}\mathchoice{\mbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle\mathchar 277$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\textstyle\mathchar 277$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle\mathchar 277$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle\mathchar 277$}}},}

can be decomposed into two isomorphic sublattices as

\boldsymbol{p}_{2}^{(even)}=\left(2w^{\prime}\right)\mathbf{{{}\mathchoice{\mbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle\mathchar 277$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\textstyle\mathchar 277$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle\mathchar 277$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle\mathchar 277$}}}}\qquad,\qquad\boldsymbol{p}_{2}^{(odd)}=\left(2w^{\prime}-1\right)\mathbf{{{}\mathchoice{\mbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle\mathchar 277$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\textstyle\mathchar 277$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle\mathchar 277$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle\mathchar 277$}}}}

(54)

with

integer. These sublattices are also isomorphic to the

\Lambda_{\mathrm{r}}^{\mathtt{su}_{2}}
. In consequence, the
discriminant
D_{\mathtt{su}_{2}}=\Lambda_{\mathrm{w}}^{\mathtt{su}_{2}}/\Lambda_{\mathrm{r}}^{\mathtt{su}_{2}}
is isomorphic to the group
\mathbb{Z}_{2}.

\left(\mathbf{5}\right)

Siegel-Narain

\Theta_{\mathrm{su}_{2}}
and
Partition function
Z_{\mathrm{su}_{2}}
: The

{}_{2}^{\mathbf{su}_{2}}

{{}\mathchoice{\mbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle\mathchar 273$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\textstyle\mathchar 273$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle\mathchar 273$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle\mathchar 273$}}}\left({{}\mathchoice{\mbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle\mathchar 284$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\textstyle\mathchar 284$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle\mathchar 284$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle\mathchar 284$}}}\right)=q^{1/24}\mathop{\displaystyle\prod}\nolimits_{n=1}^{\infty}\left(1-q^{n}\right)
with

as follows

with Siegel-Narain theta function

reads explicitly as

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\mathop{\displaystyle\sum}\limits_{\boldsymbol{m}\in\mathbb{Z}^{2}}q^{\frac{1}{2}\mathfrak{Q}_{L}\left(\boldsymbol{m}\right)}\bar{q}^{\frac{1}{2}\mathfrak{Q}_{R}\left(\boldsymbol{m}\right)}

(62)

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\mathop{\displaystyle\sum}\limits_{\boldsymbol{m}\in\mathbb{Z}^{2}}e^{i{{}\mathchoice{\mbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle\mathchar 281$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\textstyle\mathchar 281$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle\mathchar 281$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle\mathchar 281$}}}{{}\mathchoice{\mbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle\mathchar 284$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\textstyle\mathchar 284$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle\mathchar 284$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle\mathchar 284$}}}_{1}\mathfrak{Q}_{\mathtt{su}_{2}}\left(\boldsymbol{m}\right)}\times e^{-{{}\mathchoice{\mbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle\mathchar 281$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\textstyle\mathchar 281$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle\mathchar 281$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle\mathchar 281$}}}{{}\mathchoice{\mbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle\mathchar 284$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\textstyle\mathchar 284$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle\mathchar 284$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle\mathchar 284$}}}_{2}\mathcal{H}_{\mathtt{su}_{2}}\left(\boldsymbol{m}\right)}

it is known to obey the differential equation

\left[{{}\mathchoice{\mbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle\mathchar 284$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\textstyle\mathchar 284$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle\mathchar 284$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle\mathchar 284$}}}_{2}^{2}\left(\frac{\partial^{2}}{\partial{{}\mathchoice{\mbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle\mathchar 284$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\textstyle\mathchar 284$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle\mathchar 284$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle\mathchar 284$}}}_{1}^{2}}+\frac{\partial^{2}}{\partial{{}\mathchoice{\mbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle\mathchar 284$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\textstyle\mathchar 284$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle\mathchar 284$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle\mathchar 284$}}}_{2}^{2}}\right)+{{}\mathchoice{\mbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle\mathchar 284$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\textstyle\mathchar 284$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle\mathchar 284$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle\mathchar 284$}}}_{2}\frac{\partial}{\partial{{}\mathchoice{\mbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle\mathchar 284$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\textstyle\mathchar 284$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle\mathchar 284$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle\mathchar 284$}}}_{2}}-\frac{1}{4}\left(R\frac{\partial}{\partial R}\right)^{2}\right]\Theta_{\mathrm{su}_{2}}\left[{{}\mathchoice{\mbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle\mathchar 284$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\textstyle\mathchar 284$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle\mathchar 284$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle\mathchar 284$}}},\bar{{{}\mathchoice{\mbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle\mathchar 284$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\textstyle\mathchar 284$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle\mathchar 284$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle\mathchar 284$}}}};R\right]=0

\left[{{}\mathchoice{\mbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle\mathchar 284$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\textstyle\mathchar 284$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle\mathchar 284$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle\mathchar 284$}}}_{2}^{2}\left(\frac{\partial^{2}}{\partial{{}\mathchoice{\mbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle\mathchar 284$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\textstyle\mathchar 284$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle\mathchar 284$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle\mathchar 284$}}}_{1}^{2}}+\frac{\partial^{2}}{\partial{{}\mathchoice{\mbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle\mathchar 284$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\textstyle\mathchar 284$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle\mathchar 284$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle\mathchar 284$}}}_{2}^{2}}\right)+{{}\mathchoice{\mbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle\mathchar 284$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\textstyle\mathchar 284$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle\mathchar 284$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle\mathchar 284$}}}_{2}\frac{\partial}{\partial{{}\mathchoice{\mbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle\mathchar 284$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\textstyle\mathchar 284$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle\mathchar 284$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle\mathchar 284$}}}_{2}}\right]F_{\mathrm{su}_{2}}\left({{}\mathchoice{\mbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle\mathchar 284$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\textstyle\mathchar 284$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle\mathchar 284$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle\mathchar 284$}}},\bar{{{}\mathchoice{\mbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle\mathchar 284$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\textstyle\mathchar 284$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle\mathchar 284$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle\mathchar 284$}}}}\right)=0

with
\boldsymbol{h}=0
is modular invariant under

transformation
{{}\mathchoice{\mbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle\mathchar 284$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\textstyle\mathchar 284$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle\mathchar 284$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle\mathchar 284$}}}\rightarrow\left(a{{}\mathchoice{\mbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle\mathchar 284$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\textstyle\mathchar 284$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle\mathchar 284$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle\mathchar 284$}}}+b\right)/\left(c{{}\mathchoice{\mbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle\mathchar 284$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\textstyle\mathchar 284$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle\mathchar 284$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle\mathchar 284$}}}+d\right)
with
