It is important to note for later use that the T-invariance requires an

even integer
lattice
\Lambda_{d_{L},d_{R}}
while the invariance S
implies that
\Lambda_{d_{L},d_{R}}
must be also
self dual
. Notice
that even and self dual lattices exist if and only if
d_{L}-d_{R}=8l
for
integer
l
. In type II strings, we must have
d_{L}-d_{R}=0
and for
heterotic string we should have
d_{L}-d_{R}=16

[
48
]
.

3
Rank two Narain CFT
{}_{2}^{\mathbf{g}}

In this section, we initiate our study with NCFT
{}_{2}^{\mathtt{su}_{3}}
as
the first member of rank 2 theories which also include G
{}_{2},
SO(5) and
SP(4) having the isomorphism
SO(5)\simeq SP(4).
The general set up for the
rank two unitary NCFT
{}_{2}^{\mathtt{su}_{3}}
is quite similar to the
framework outlined in the previous section for NCFT
{}_{2}^{\mathtt{su}_{2}}
.
The main distinction lies in the presence of two scalar fields
\left(X_{1},X_{2}\right)
compactified on the 2-torus
\mathbb{S}^{1}\times\mathbb{S}^{1}
generating the Cartan subgroup

U(1)\times U(1)\subset SU(3)

(73)

As in the case of su(2), the string fields are split into left and right
moving components like
X^{a}=(X_{L}^{a}+X_{R}^{a})/\sqrt{2}.
Their 2D field action
\mathcal{I}\left[X_{1},X_{2};\mathcal{G}_{ab},\mathcal{B}_{ab}\right]
generally takes the form

\mathcal{I}\left[X;\mathcal{G},\mathcal{B}\right]=\frac{1}{4{{}\mathchoice{\mbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle\mathchar 281$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\textstyle\mathchar 281$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle\mathchar 281$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle\mathchar 281$}}}{{}\mathchoice{\mbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle\mathchar 267$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\textstyle\mathchar 267$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle\mathchar 267$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle\mathchar 267$}}}^{\prime}}\displaystyle\int d^{2}{{}\mathchoice{\mbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle\mathchar 283$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\textstyle\mathchar 283$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle\mathchar 283$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle\mathchar 283$}}}\mathop{\displaystyle\sum}\limits_{a,b=1}^{2}\left({{}\mathchoice{\mbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle\mathchar 270$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\textstyle\mathchar 270$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle\mathchar 270$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle\mathchar 270$}}}^{\mathrm{{{}\mathchoice{\mbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle\mathchar 267$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\textstyle\mathchar 267$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle\mathchar 267$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle\mathchar 267$}}}{{}\mathchoice{\mbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle\mathchar 268$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\textstyle\mathchar 268$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle\mathchar 268$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle\mathchar 268$}}}}}\partial_{\mathrm{{{}\mathchoice{\mbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle\mathchar 267$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\textstyle\mathchar 267$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle\mathchar 267$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle\mathchar 267$}}}}}X^{a}\mathcal{G}_{ab}\partial_{\mathrm{{{}\mathchoice{\mbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle\mathchar 268$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\textstyle\mathchar 268$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle\mathchar 268$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle\mathchar 268$}}}}}X^{b}+i{{}\mathchoice{\mbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle\mathchar 290$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\textstyle\mathchar 290$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle\mathchar 290$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle\mathchar 290$}}}^{\mathrm{{{}\mathchoice{\mbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle\mathchar 267$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\textstyle\mathchar 267$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle\mathchar 267$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle\mathchar 267$}}}{{}\mathchoice{\mbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle\mathchar 268$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\textstyle\mathchar 268$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle\mathchar 268$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle\mathchar 268$}}}}}\partial_{\mathrm{{{}\mathchoice{\mbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle\mathchar 267$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\textstyle\mathchar 267$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle\mathchar 267$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle\mathchar 267$}}}}}X^{a}\mathcal{B}_{ab}\partial_{\mathrm{{{}\mathchoice{\mbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle\mathchar 268$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\textstyle\mathchar 268$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle\mathchar 268$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle\mathchar 268$}}}}}X^{b}\right)

(74)

Here,
\mathcal{G}_{ab}
represents the constant symmetric target space
metric possessing three degrees of freedom, while
\mathcal{B}_{ab}={{}\mathchoice{\mbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle\mathchar 290$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\textstyle\mathchar 290$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle\mathchar 290$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle\mathchar 290$}}}_{ab}\mathcal{B}
denotes the constant antisymmetric
background field interpreted as a flux. These matrix variables
\left(\mathcal{G}_{ab},\mathcal{B}_{ab}\right)
define the coordinates of the
four dimensional moduli space

\mathcal{M}_{\mathrm{su}_{3}}=\mathcal{O}(2,2;\mathbb{Z})\backslash\mathcal{O}(2,2;\mathbb{R})/\mathcal{O}(2;\mathbb{R})\times\mathcal{O}(2;\mathbb{R})

(75)

of the standard Narain CFT
{}_{2}^{\mathtt{su}_{3}}.
Distances within this
moduli space are measured by the Zamolodchikov metric
[
1
]

ds_{\mathrm{su}_{3}}^{2}=\mathcal{G}^{ac}\mathcal{G}^{bd}\left(d\mathcal{G}_{ab}d\mathcal{G}_{cd}+d\mathcal{B}_{ab}d\mathcal{B}_{cd}\right)

(76)

In our proposal, this metric will be realised in terms of the Cartan matrix K
{}_{\mathrm{su}_{3}}
of su(3) and its inverse K
{}_{\mathrm{su}_{3}}^{-1}
. A
similar analysis to NCFT
{}_{2}^{\mathbf{su}_{3}}
holds also for SO(5) and G
2
where
\mathcal{B}_{ab}
assumes non zero values.

3.1
NCFT
{}_{2}^{su_{3}}
as an extension of NCFT
{}_{2}^{su_{2}}

The description of the Narain CFT
{}_{2}^{\mathtt{su}_{3}}
and its genus-one
partition function
Z_{\mathrm{su}_{3}}
along the lines of our proposal can
be constructed by starting from eq(
24
) and extending it to the su(3)
symmetry. This su(3) Lie algebra features two simple roots
\left(\mathbf{{{}\mathchoice{\mbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle\mathchar 267$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\textstyle\mathchar 267$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle\mathchar 267$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle\mathchar 267$}}}}_{1},\mathbf{{{}\mathchoice{\mbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle\mathchar 267$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\textstyle\mathchar 267$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle\mathchar 267$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle\mathchar 267$}}}}_{2}\right)
and two fundamental weights
\left(\mathbf{{{}\mathchoice{\mbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle\mathchar 277$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\textstyle\mathchar 277$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle\mathchar 277$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle\mathchar 277$}}}}_{1},\mathbf{{{}\mathchoice{\mbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle\mathchar 277$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\textstyle\mathchar 277$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle\mathchar 277$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle\mathchar 277$}}}}_{2}\right)
with
intersection matrices

\mathbf{{{}\mathchoice{\mbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle\mathchar 267$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\textstyle\mathchar 267$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle\mathchar 267$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle\mathchar 267$}}}}_{i}.\mathbf{{{}\mathchoice{\mbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle\mathchar 267$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\textstyle\mathchar 267$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle\mathchar 267$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle\mathchar 267$}}}}_{j}=K_{ij}\qquad,\qquad\mathbf{{{}\mathchoice{\mbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle\mathchar 277$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\textstyle\mathchar 277$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle\mathchar 277$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle\mathchar 277$}}}}^{i}.\mathbf{{{}\mathchoice{\mbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle\mathchar 277$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\textstyle\mathchar 277$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle\mathchar 277$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle\mathchar 277$}}}}^{j}=\tilde{K}^{ij}\qquad,\qquad\mathbf{{{}\mathchoice{\mbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle\mathchar 267$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\textstyle\mathchar 267$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle\mathchar 267$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle\mathchar 267$}}}}_{i}.\mathbf{{{}\mathchoice{\mbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle\mathchar 277$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\textstyle\mathchar 277$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle\mathchar 277$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle\mathchar 277$}}}}^{j}={{}\mathchoice{\mbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle\mathchar 270$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\textstyle\mathchar 270$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle\mathchar 270$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle\mathchar 270$}}}_{i}^{j}

(77)

reading explicitly as

K_{ij}=\left(\begin{array}[]{cc}2&-1\\
-1&2\end{array}\right)\qquad,\qquad\tilde{K}^{ij}=\frac{1}{3}\left(\begin{array}[]{cc}2&1\\
1&2\end{array}\right)

(78)

showing in turn that
\mathbf{{{}\mathchoice{\mbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle\mathchar 267$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\textstyle\mathchar 267$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle\mathchar 267$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle\mathchar 267$}}}}_{i}^{2}=2
and
\mathbf{{{}\mathchoice{\mbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle\mathchar 277$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\textstyle\mathchar 277$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle\mathchar 277$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle\mathchar 277$}}}}_{i}^{2}=2/3
as well as
\left|K_{ij}\right|\geq 1
and
\left|\tilde{K}^{ij}\right|<1.
In this NCFT
{}_{2}^{su_{3}}
with
central charge
c_{L/R}=2
, the length
\mathbf{{{}\mathchoice{\mbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle\mathchar 277$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\textstyle\mathchar 277$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle\mathchar 277$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle\mathchar 277$}}}}_{i}^{2}
differs
from the inverse
1/\mathbf{{{}\mathchoice{\mbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle\mathchar 267$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\textstyle\mathchar 267$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle\mathchar 267$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle\mathchar 267$}}}}_{i}^{2}
although it remains confined
to the interval
\left[1/2,1\right];
that is
1/2\leq\mathbf{{{}\mathchoice{\mbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle\mathchar 277$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\textstyle\mathchar 277$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle\mathchar 277$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle\mathchar 277$}}}}_{i}^{2}<1.
Furthermore, the duality relation
\mathbf{{{}\mathchoice{\mbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle\mathchar 267$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\textstyle\mathchar 267$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle\mathchar 267$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle\mathchar 267$}}}}_{i}.\mathbf{{{}\mathchoice{\mbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle\mathchar 277$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\textstyle\mathchar 277$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle\mathchar 277$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle\mathchar 277$}}}}_{j}={{}\mathchoice{\mbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle\mathchar 270$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\textstyle\mathchar 270$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle\mathchar 270$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle\mathchar 270$}}}_{ij}
allows to express the simple roots
\mathbf{{{}\mathchoice{\mbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle\mathchar 267$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\textstyle\mathchar 267$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle\mathchar 267$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle\mathchar 267$}}}}_{i}
in terms of the fundamental weights
\mathbf{{{}\mathchoice{\mbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle\mathchar 277$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\textstyle\mathchar 277$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle\mathchar 277$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle\mathchar 277$}}}}_{j}

and vice versa as follows,

\begin{tabular}[]{lll}$\mathbf{{{}\mathchoice{\mbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle\mathchar 267$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\textstyle\mathchar 267$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle\mathchar 267$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle\mathchar 267$}}}}_{1}$&$=$&$2\mathbf{{{}\mathchoice{\mbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle\mathchar 277$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\textstyle\mathchar 277$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle\mathchar 277$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle\mathchar 277$}}}}_{1}-\mathbf{{{}\mathchoice{\mbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle\mathchar 277$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\textstyle\mathchar 277$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle\mathchar 277$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle\mathchar 277$}}}}_{2}$\\
$\mathbf{{{}\mathchoice{\mbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle\mathchar 267$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\textstyle\mathchar 267$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle\mathchar 267$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle\mathchar 267$}}}}_{2}$&$=$&$2\mathbf{{{}\mathchoice{\mbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle\mathchar 277$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\textstyle\mathchar 277$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle\mathchar 277$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle\mathchar 277$}}}}_{2}-\mathbf{{{}\mathchoice{\mbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle\mathchar 277$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\textstyle\mathchar 277$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle\mathchar 277$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle\mathchar 277$}}}}_{1}$\end{tabular}\qquad,\qquad\begin{tabular}[]{lll}$\mathbf{{{}\mathchoice{\mbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle\mathchar 277$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\textstyle\mathchar 277$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle\mathchar 277$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle\mathchar 277$}}}}_{1}$&$=$&$\frac{2}{3}\mathbf{{{}\mathchoice{\mbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle\mathchar 267$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\textstyle\mathchar 267$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle\mathchar 267$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle\mathchar 267$}}}}_{1}+\frac{1}{3}\mathbf{{{}\mathchoice{\mbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle\mathchar 267$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\textstyle\mathchar 267$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle\mathchar 267$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle\mathchar 267$}}}}_{2}$\\
$\mathbf{{{}\mathchoice{\mbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle\mathchar 277$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\textstyle\mathchar 277$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle\mathchar 277$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle\mathchar 277$}}}}_{2}$&$=$&$\frac{1}{3}\mathbf{{{}\mathchoice{\mbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle\mathchar 267$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\textstyle\mathchar 267$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle\mathchar 267$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle\mathchar 267$}}}}_{1}+\frac{2}{3}\mathbf{{{}\mathchoice{\mbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle\mathchar 267$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\textstyle\mathchar 267$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle\mathchar 267$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle\mathchar 267$}}}}_{2}$\end{tabular}

(79)

Notice moreover that
\mathbf{{{}\mathchoice{\mbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle\mathchar 277$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\textstyle\mathchar 277$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle\mathchar 277$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle\mathchar 277$}}}}_{1}
is the highest weight vector of
the su(3) fundamental representation
3
; and
\mathbf{{{}\mathchoice{\mbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle\mathchar 277$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\textstyle\mathchar 277$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle\mathchar 277$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle\mathchar 277$}}}}_{2}

is the highest weight vector of the conjugate representation
3̄
.
The weight vectors
{{}\mathchoice{\mbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle\mathchar 279$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\textstyle\mathchar 279$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle\mathchar 279$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle\mathchar 279$}}}_{i}
of the states of these representations in the
basis (
\mathbf{{{}\mathchoice{\mbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle\mathchar 267$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\textstyle\mathchar 267$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle\mathchar 267$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle\mathchar 267$}}}}_{1},\mathbf{{{}\mathchoice{\mbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle\mathchar 267$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\textstyle\mathchar 267$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle\mathchar 267$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle\mathchar 267$}}}}_{2}
) are given by

\displaystyle\mathbf{3}

\displaystyle:

\displaystyle{{}\mathchoice{\mbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle\mathchar 279$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\textstyle\mathchar 279$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle\mathchar 279$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle\mathchar 279$}}}_{1}=\left(\frac{2}{3}\mathbf{,}\frac{1}{3}\right),\quad{{}\mathchoice{\mbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle\mathchar 279$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\textstyle\mathchar 279$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle\mathchar 279$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle\mathchar 279$}}}_{2}=\left(-\frac{1}{3}\mathbf{,}\frac{1}{3}\right),\quad{{}\mathchoice{\mbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle\mathchar 279$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\textstyle\mathchar 279$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle\mathchar 279$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle\mathchar 279$}}}_{3}=\left(-\frac{1}{3}\mathbf{,-}\frac{2}{3}\right)

(80)

\displaystyle\mathbf{\bar{3}}

\displaystyle:

\displaystyle{{}\mathchoice{\mbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle\mathchar 279$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\textstyle\mathchar 279$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle\mathchar 279$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle\mathchar 279$}}}_{1}^{\ast}=\left(\frac{1}{3}\mathbf{,}\frac{2}{3}\right),\quad{{}\mathchoice{\mbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle\mathchar 279$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\textstyle\mathchar 279$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle\mathchar 279$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle\mathchar 279$}}}_{2}^{\ast}=\left(\frac{1}{3}\mathbf{,}-\frac{1}{3}\right),\quad{{}\mathchoice{\mbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle\mathchar 279$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\textstyle\mathchar 279$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle\mathchar 279$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle\mathchar 279$}}}_{3}^{\ast}=\left(-\frac{2}{3}\mathbf{,}-\frac{1}{3}\right)

(81)

3.1.1
Sitting at the point
x_{i}=1
in
\mathcal{M}_{\mathbf{su}_{3}}

Using the two simple roots
\mathbf{{{}\mathchoice{\mbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle\mathchar 267$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\textstyle\mathchar 267$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle\mathchar 267$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle\mathchar 267$}}}}_{i}
and the two fundamental
weights
\mathbf{{{}\mathchoice{\mbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle\mathchar 277$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\textstyle\mathchar 277$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle\mathchar 277$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle\mathchar 277$}}}}_{i}
of su(3), we can expand the left
\boldsymbol{p}_{L}
and the right
\boldsymbol{p}_{R}
momentum vectors of
the Narain CFT
{}_{2}^{\mathtt{su}_{3}}
for radii
R_{1}=R_{2}=1/2
as
follows

\boldsymbol{p}_{L}

=

\frac{1}{\sqrt{2}}\mathop{\displaystyle\sum}\limits_{i=1}^{2}\left(n^{i}\mathbf{{{}\mathchoice{\mbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle\mathchar 267$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\textstyle\mathchar 267$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle\mathchar 267$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle\mathchar 267$}}}}_{i}+w_{i}\mathbf{{{}\mathchoice{\mbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle\mathchar 277$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\textstyle\mathchar 277$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle\mathchar 277$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle\mathchar 277$}}}}^{i}\right)

\boldsymbol{p}_{R}

=

\frac{1}{\sqrt{2}}\mathop{\displaystyle\sum}\limits_{i=1}^{2}\left(n^{i}\mathbf{{{}\mathchoice{\mbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle\mathchar 267$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\textstyle\mathchar 267$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle\mathchar 267$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle\mathchar 267$}}}}_{i}-w_{i}\mathbf{{{}\mathchoice{\mbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle\mathchar 277$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\textstyle\mathchar 277$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle\mathchar 277$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle\mathchar 277$}}}}^{i}\right)

(82)

with Kaluza-Klein
\left\{n^{i}\right\}
modes and winding
\left\{w_{i}\right\}
integers. Here, the vielbeins for NCFT
{}^{\mathbf{su}_{3}}
read in our proposal as follows

\boldsymbol{E}_{\text{ {a}}}^{\text{{\@text@baccent{m}}}}=\frac{1}{\sqrt{2}}\left(\begin{array}[]{cc}\mathbf{{{}\mathchoice{\mbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle\mathchar 267$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\textstyle\mathchar 267$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle\mathchar 267$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle\mathchar 267$}}}}_{i}&+\mathbf{{{}\mathchoice{\mbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle\mathchar 277$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\textstyle\mathchar 277$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle\mathchar 277$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle\mathchar 277$}}}}^{j}\\
\mathbf{{{}\mathchoice{\mbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle\mathchar 267$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\textstyle\mathchar 267$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle\mathchar 267$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle\mathchar 267$}}}}_{i}&-\mathbf{{{}\mathchoice{\mbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle\mathchar 277$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\textstyle\mathchar 277$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle\mathchar 277$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle\mathchar 277$}}}}^{j}\end{array}\right)

(83)

The lengths of these quantum momenta are given by

\boldsymbol{p}_{\mathrm{L}}^{2}

=

\frac{1}{2}\mathop{\displaystyle\sum}\limits_{i,j=1}^{2}\left[n^{i}K_{ij}n^{j}+w_{i}\tilde{K}^{ij}w_{j}+n^{i}\left(\mathbf{{{}\mathchoice{\mbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle\mathchar 267$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\textstyle\mathchar 267$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle\mathchar 267$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle\mathchar 267$}}}}_{i}\mathbf{.{{}\mathchoice{\mbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle\mathchar 277$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\textstyle\mathchar 277$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle\mathchar 277$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle\mathchar 277$}}}}^{j}\right)w_{j}+w_{i}\left(\mathbf{{{}\mathchoice{\mbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle\mathchar 277$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\textstyle\mathchar 277$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle\mathchar 277$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle\mathchar 277$}}}}^{i}\mathbf{.{{}\mathchoice{\mbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle\mathchar 267$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\textstyle\mathchar 267$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle\mathchar 267$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle\mathchar 267$}}}}_{j}\right)n^{j}\right]

\boldsymbol{p}_{\mathrm{R}}^{2}

=

\frac{1}{2}\mathop{\displaystyle\sum}\limits_{i,j=1}^{2}\left[n^{i}K_{ij}n^{j}+w_{i}\tilde{K}^{ij}w_{j}-n^{i}\left(\mathbf{{{}\mathchoice{\mbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle\mathchar 267$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\textstyle\mathchar 267$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle\mathchar 267$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle\mathchar 267$}}}}_{i}\mathbf{.{{}\mathchoice{\mbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle\mathchar 277$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\textstyle\mathchar 277$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle\mathchar 277$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle\mathchar 277$}}}}^{j}\right)w_{j}-w_{i}\left(\mathbf{{{}\mathchoice{\mbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle\mathchar 277$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\textstyle\mathchar 277$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle\mathchar 277$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle\mathchar 277$}}}}^{i}\mathbf{.{{}\mathchoice{\mbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle\mathchar 267$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\textstyle\mathchar 267$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle\mathchar 267$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle\mathchar 267$}}}}_{j}\right)n^{j}\right]

(84)

Similarly to the su(2) conformal theory of the previous section, here also
we set
\mathfrak{Q}_{L}^{\mathtt{su}_{3}}:=\boldsymbol{p}_{L}^{2}
and
\mathfrak{Q}_{R}^{\mathtt{su}_{3}}:=\boldsymbol{p}_{R}^{2};
these are
quadratic forms having the structure

\mathfrak{Q}_{L}^{\mathtt{su}_{3}}=\boldsymbol{m}^{T}.\boldsymbol{Q}_{L}^{\mathtt{su}_{3}}.\boldsymbol{m}\qquad,\qquad\mathfrak{Q}_{R}^{\mathtt{su}_{3}}=\boldsymbol{m}^{T}.\boldsymbol{Q}_{R}^{\mathtt{su}_{3}}.\boldsymbol{m}

(85)

where
\boldsymbol{m}^{T}=\left(n^{1},n^{2},w_{1},w_{2}\right)
is a 4d
integer vector and
\boldsymbol{Q}_{L/R}^{\mathtt{su}_{3}}
are
4\times 4

matrices reading in terms of the Cartan matrix
K_{\mathtt{su}_{3}}
and its
inverse
K_{\mathtt{su}_{3}}^{-1}:=\tilde{K}_{\mathtt{su}_{3}}
like

\boldsymbol{Q}_{L}^{\mathtt{su}_{3}}=\frac{1}{2}\left(\begin{array}[]{cc}K_{ij}&{{}\mathchoice{\mbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle\mathchar 270$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\textstyle\mathchar 270$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle\mathchar 270$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle\mathchar 270$}}}_{i}^{l}\\
{{}\mathchoice{\mbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle\mathchar 270$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\textstyle\mathchar 270$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle\mathchar 270$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle\mathchar 270$}}}_{j}^{k}&\tilde{K}^{kl}\end{array}\right)\qquad,\qquad\boldsymbol{Q}_{R}^{\mathtt{su}_{3}}=\frac{1}{2}\left(\begin{array}[]{cc}K_{ij}&-{{}\mathchoice{\mbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle\mathchar 270$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\textstyle\mathchar 270$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle\mathchar 270$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle\mathchar 270$}}}_{i}^{l}\\
-{{}\mathchoice{\mbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle\mathchar 270$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\textstyle\mathchar 270$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle\mathchar 270$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle\mathchar 270$}}}_{j}^{k}&\tilde{K}^{kl}\end{array}\right)

(86)

By using

\mathcal{H}_{\mathtt{su}_{3}}

=

\mathfrak{Q}_{L}^{\mathtt{su}_{3}}+\mathfrak{Q}_{R}^{\mathtt{su}_{3}}

\mathfrak{Q}_{\mathtt{su}_{3}}

=

\mathfrak{Q}_{L}^{\mathtt{su}_{3}}-\mathfrak{Q}_{R}^{\mathtt{su}_{3}}

(87)

and denoting in bold letters the associated
4\times 4
matrices like

\boldsymbol{H}_{\mathtt{su}_{3}}

=

\boldsymbol{Q}_{L}^{\mathtt{su}_{3}}+\boldsymbol{Q}_{R}^{\mathtt{su}_{3}}

\boldsymbol{Q}_{\mathtt{su}_{3}}

=

\boldsymbol{Q}_{L}^{\mathtt{su}_{3}}-\boldsymbol{Q}_{R}^{\mathtt{su}_{3}}

(88)

we end up with the factorisations

\mathcal{H}_{\mathtt{su}_{3}}

=

\boldsymbol{m}^{T}.\boldsymbol{H}_{\mathtt{su}_{3}}.\boldsymbol{m}

\mathfrak{Q}_{\mathtt{su}_{3}}

=

\boldsymbol{m}^{T}.\boldsymbol{Q}_{\mathtt{su}_{3}}.\boldsymbol{m}

(89)

with

\boldsymbol{H}_{\mathtt{su}_{3}}=\left(\begin{array}[]{cc}K_{ij}&0\\
0&\tilde{K}^{kl}\end{array}\right)\qquad,\qquad\boldsymbol{Q}_{\mathtt{su}_{3}}=\left(\begin{array}[]{cc}0&{{}\mathchoice{\mbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle\mathchar 270$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\textstyle\mathchar 270$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle\mathchar 270$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle\mathchar 270$}}}_{i}^{l}\\
{{}\mathchoice{\mbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle\mathchar 270$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\textstyle\mathchar 270$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle\mathchar 270$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle\mathchar 270$}}}_{j}^{k}&0\end{array}\right)

(90)

Here also, the matrix
\boldsymbol{H}_{\mathtt{su}_{3}}
is block diagonal
and
\boldsymbol{Q}_{\mathtt{su}_{3}}
is an off block diagonal unimodular
matrix
\left|\det\boldsymbol{Q}_{\mathtt{su}_{3}}\right|=1.
By
substituting
\mathbf{{{}\mathchoice{\mbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle\mathchar 267$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\textstyle\mathchar 267$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle\mathchar 267$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle\mathchar 267$}}}}_{i}=K_{ij}\mathbf{{{}\mathchoice{\mbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle\mathchar 277$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\textstyle\mathchar 277$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle\mathchar 277$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle\mathchar 277$}}}}^{j}
in (
79
), we can put the momenta in the form

\boldsymbol{p}_{L}

=

\frac{1}{\sqrt{2}}\mathop{\displaystyle\sum}\limits_{i=1}^{2}\left(K_{ij}n^{j}+w_{i}\right)\mathbf{{{}\mathchoice{\mbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle\mathchar 277$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\textstyle\mathchar 277$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle\mathchar 277$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle\mathchar 277$}}}}^{i}

\equiv

\frac{1}{\sqrt{2}}\mathop{\displaystyle\sum}\limits_{i=1}^{2}\left(\tilde{n}_{i}+w_{i}\right)\mathbf{{{}\mathchoice{\mbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle\mathchar 277$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\textstyle\mathchar 277$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle\mathchar 277$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle\mathchar 277$}}}}^{i}

\boldsymbol{p}_{R}

=

\frac{1}{\sqrt{2}}\mathop{\displaystyle\sum}\limits_{i=1}^{2}\left(K_{ij}n^{j}-w_{i}\right)\mathbf{{{}\mathchoice{\mbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle\mathchar 277$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\textstyle\mathchar 277$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle\mathchar 277$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle\mathchar 277$}}}}^{i}

\equiv

\frac{1}{\sqrt{2}}\mathop{\displaystyle\sum}\limits_{i=1}^{2}\left(\tilde{n}_{i}-w_{i}\right)\mathbf{{{}\mathchoice{\mbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle\mathchar 277$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\textstyle\mathchar 277$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle\mathchar 277$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle\mathchar 277$}}}}^{i}

(91)

with the following features ensues:

\left(\mathbf{1}\right)

the left
\boldsymbol{p}_{L}
and the
right
\boldsymbol{p}_{R}
momenta are given by shifts of the KK momentum

\boldsymbol{p}_{(\mathbf{n})}=\frac{1}{\sqrt{2}}\mathop{\displaystyle\sum}\limits_{i,j=1}^{2}\left(n^{i}K_{ij}\mathbf{{{}\mathchoice{\mbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle\mathchar 277$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\textstyle\mathchar 277$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle\mathchar 277$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle\mathchar 277$}}}}^{j}\right)\equiv\frac{1}{\sqrt{2}}\mathop{\displaystyle\sum}\limits_{i=1}^{2}\tilde{n}_{j}\mathbf{{{}\mathchoice{\mbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle\mathchar 277$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\textstyle\mathchar 277$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle\mathchar 277$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle\mathchar 277$}}}}^{j}

(92)

by the amounts
\pm\sum_{i=1}^{2}\left(w_{i}\mathbf{{{}\mathchoice{\mbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle\mathchar 277$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\textstyle\mathchar 277$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle\mathchar 277$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle\mathchar 277$}}}}^{i}\right)/\sqrt{2}
induced by non trivial windings.

\left(\mathbf{2}\right)

Regarding the factorisation
\mathfrak{Q}_{\mathtt{su}_{3}}=\mathfrak{p}^{\text{{\@text@baccent{m}}}}{{}\mathchoice{\mbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle\mathchar 273$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\textstyle\mathchar 273$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle\mathchar 273$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle\mathchar 273$}}}_{\text{{\@text@baccent{m}\@text@baccent{n}}}}\mathfrak{p}^{\text{{\@text@baccent{n}}}}
with metric
{{}\mathchoice{\mbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle\mathchar 273$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\textstyle\mathchar 273$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle\mathchar 273$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle\mathchar 273$}}}_{\text{{\@text@baccent{m}\@text@baccent{n}}}}=diag(+,+,-,-)
, the expressions of the vectors
\mathfrak{p}^{\text{{\@text@baccent{m}}}}
in terms of
\boldsymbol{p}_{L}
and
\boldsymbol{p}_{R}
of the su(3) theory are given by

\mathfrak{p}^{\text{{\@text@baccent{m}}}}=\left(\begin{array}[]{c}\frac{1}{\sqrt{2}}\boldsymbol{p}_{L}.\mathbf{{{}\mathchoice{\mbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle\mathchar 267$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\textstyle\mathchar 267$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle\mathchar 267$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle\mathchar 267$}}}}_{i}\\
\frac{1}{\sqrt{2}}\boldsymbol{p}_{R}.\mathbf{{{}\mathchoice{\mbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle\mathchar 267$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\textstyle\mathchar 267$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle\mathchar 267$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle\mathchar 267$}}}}_{i}\end{array}\right)=\left(\begin{array}[]{c}\frac{1}{2}\left(K_{ij}n^{j}+w_{i}\right)\\
\frac{1}{2}\left(K_{ij}n^{j}-w_{i}\right)\end{array}\right)

(93)

\left(\mathbf{3}\right)

The condition
\mathfrak{p}^{\text{{\@text@baccent{m}}}}{{}\mathchoice{\mbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle\mathchar 273$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\textstyle\mathchar 273$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle\mathchar 273$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle\mathchar 273$}}}_{\text{{\@text@baccent{m}\@text@baccent{n}}}}\mathfrak{p}^{\text{{\@text@baccent{n}}}}\in 2\mathbb{Z}
is solved in our proposal like
\mathfrak{Q}_{\mathtt{su}_{3}}=\boldsymbol{m}^{T}.\boldsymbol{Q}_{\mathtt{su}_{3}}.\boldsymbol{m}
indicating that the metric
{\large g}_{\text{{ab}}}

of the lattice
{\large\Lambda}_{\mathtt{su}_{3}}
is defined by
\boldsymbol{Q}_{\text{{ab}}}^{\mathtt{su}_{3}};
that is

{\large\Lambda}_{\mathtt{su}_{3}}=\left\{\boldsymbol{m}\in\mathbb{Z}^{4},\quad\mathfrak{Q}_{\mathtt{su}_{3}}=\boldsymbol{m}^{\text{{a}}}{\large g}_{\text{{ab}}}\boldsymbol{m}^{\text{{b}}}\quad|\quad{\large g}_{\text{{ab}}}=\boldsymbol{Q}_{\text{{ab}}}^{\mathtt{su}_{3}}\right\}

(94)

where
\boldsymbol{m}=\left(\mathbf{n},\mathbf{w}\right)\in\mathbb{Z}^{2}\times\mathbb{Z}^{2}
. For this lattice, we then have the property
\mathfrak{Q}_{\mathtt{su}_{3}}=2\mathbf{n}.\mathbf{w}
which is an even
integer that vanishes for
\mathbf{n}\perp\mathbf{w}.

3.1.2
NCFT
{}_{2}^{su_{3}}
at generic moduli
\left\{x_{1},x_{2}\right\}

Similarly to the Narain CFT
{}_{2}^{\mathtt{su}_{2}},
one can exhibit the

four
moduli
\left\{x_{11},x_{12},x_{21},x_{22}\right\}
of the
su(3) conformal theory including the diagonal
\left\{x_{1},x_{2}\right\}

given by the radii
\left(R_{1},R_{2}\right)
of the 2-torus
\mathbb{T}^{2}=\mathbb{S}_{1}^{1}\times\mathbb{S}_{2}^{1}
. Instead of the two simple
roots
\left\{\mathbf{{{}\mathchoice{\mbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle\mathchar 267$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\textstyle\mathchar 267$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle\mathchar 267$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle\mathchar 267$}}}}_{1},\mathbf{{{}\mathchoice{\mbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle\mathchar 267$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\textstyle\mathchar 267$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle\mathchar 267$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle\mathchar 267$}}}}_{2}\right\}
and the
two fundamental weights
\left\{\mathbf{{{}\mathchoice{\mbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle\mathchar 277$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\textstyle\mathchar 277$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle\mathchar 277$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle\mathchar 277$}}}}_{1},\mathbf{{{}\mathchoice{\mbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle\mathchar 277$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\textstyle\mathchar 277$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle\mathchar 277$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle\mathchar 277$}}}}_{2}\right\}
, we introduce the ”scaled” vectors
\mathbf{{{}\mathchoice{\mbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle\mathchar 268$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\textstyle\mathchar 268$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle\mathchar 268$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle\mathchar 268$}}}}_{i}=\sum_{k=1}^{2}x_{i}^{k}\mathbf{{{}\mathchoice{\mbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle\mathchar 267$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\textstyle\mathchar 267$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle\mathchar 267$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle\mathchar 267$}}}}_{k}
and ”scaled” weights
\mathbf{{{}\mathchoice{\mbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle\mathchar 287$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\textstyle\mathchar 287$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle\mathchar 287$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle\mathchar 287$}}}}^{j}=\sum_{l=1}^{2}y_{l}^{j}\mathbf{{{}\mathchoice{\mbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle\mathchar 277$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\textstyle\mathchar 277$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle\mathchar 277$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle\mathchar 277$}}}}^{l}
reading
explicitly as

\begin{tabular}[]{lll}$\mathbf{{{}\mathchoice{\mbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle\mathchar 268$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\textstyle\mathchar 268$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle\mathchar 268$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle\mathchar 268$}}}}_{1}$&$=$&$x_{1}^{1}\mathbf{{{}\mathchoice{\mbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle\mathchar 267$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\textstyle\mathchar 267$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle\mathchar 267$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle\mathchar 267$}}}}_{1}+x_{1}^{2}\mathbf{{{}\mathchoice{\mbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle\mathchar 267$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\textstyle\mathchar 267$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle\mathchar 267$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle\mathchar 267$}}}}_{2}$\\
$\mathbf{{{}\mathchoice{\mbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle\mathchar 268$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\textstyle\mathchar 268$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle\mathchar 268$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle\mathchar 268$}}}}_{2}$&$=$&$x_{2}^{1}\mathbf{{{}\mathchoice{\mbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle\mathchar 267$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\textstyle\mathchar 267$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle\mathchar 267$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle\mathchar 267$}}}}_{1}+x_{2}^{2}\mathbf{{{}\mathchoice{\mbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle\mathchar 267$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\textstyle\mathchar 267$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle\mathchar 267$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle\mathchar 267$}}}}_{2}$\end{tabular}\qquad,\qquad\begin{tabular}[]{lll}$\mathbf{{{}\mathchoice{\mbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle\mathchar 287$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\textstyle\mathchar 287$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle\mathchar 287$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle\mathchar 287$}}}}^{1}$&$=$&$y_{1}^{1}\mathbf{{{}\mathchoice{\mbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle\mathchar 277$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\textstyle\mathchar 277$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle\mathchar 277$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle\mathchar 277$}}}}^{1}+y_{2}^{1}\mathbf{{{}\mathchoice{\mbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle\mathchar 277$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\textstyle\mathchar 277$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle\mathchar 277$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle\mathchar 277$}}}}^{2}$\\
$\mathbf{{{}\mathchoice{\mbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle\mathchar 287$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\textstyle\mathchar 287$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle\mathchar 287$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle\mathchar 287$}}}}^{2}$&$=$&$y_{1}^{2}\mathbf{{{}\mathchoice{\mbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle\mathchar 277$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\textstyle\mathchar 277$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle\mathchar 277$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle\mathchar 277$}}}}^{1}+y_{2}^{2}\mathbf{{{}\mathchoice{\mbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle\mathchar 277$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\textstyle\mathchar 277$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle\mathchar 277$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle\mathchar 277$}}}}^{2}$\end{tabular}

(95)

where the coefficients are constrained by the duality relation

\mathbf{{{}\mathchoice{\mbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle\mathchar 268$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\textstyle\mathchar 268$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle\mathchar 268$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle\mathchar 268$}}}}_{i}.\mathbf{{{}\mathchoice{\mbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle\mathchar 287$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\textstyle\mathchar 287$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle\mathchar 287$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle\mathchar 287$}}}}^{j}={{}\mathchoice{\mbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle\mathchar 270$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\textstyle\mathchar 270$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle\mathchar 270$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle\mathchar 270$}}}_{i}^{j}\qquad\Rightarrow\qquad\sum_{k=1}^{2}x_{i}^{k}y_{k}^{j}={{}\mathchoice{\mbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle\mathchar 270$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\textstyle\mathchar 270$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle\mathchar 270$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle\mathchar 270$}}}_{i}^{j}

(96)

showing that
y_{k}^{i}
is just the inverse of
x_{i}^{k}
provided that
\det(x_{i}^{k})\neq 0.
Hence, the four moduli
x_{i}^{k}
presented in our
proposal parameterise the moduli space
\mathcal{O}(2,2)/\mathcal{O}(2)\times\mathcal{O}(2).
From (
95
), we also have the ”scaled”
intersections

\mathcal{K}_{kl}^{\mathrm{su}_{\mathrm{3}}}=\mathbf{{{}\mathchoice{\mbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle\mathchar 268$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\textstyle\mathchar 268$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle\mathchar 268$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle\mathchar 268$}}}}_{i}.\mathbf{{{}\mathchoice{\mbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle\mathchar 268$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\textstyle\mathchar 268$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle\mathchar 268$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle\mathchar 268$}}}}_{j}\qquad,\qquad\mathcal{\tilde{K}}_{\mathrm{su}_{\mathrm{3}}}^{kl}=\mathbf{{{}\mathchoice{\mbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle\mathchar 287$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\textstyle\mathchar 287$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle\mathchar 287$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle\mathchar 287$}}}}^{i}.\mathbf{{{}\mathchoice{\mbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle\mathchar 287$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\textstyle\mathchar 287$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle\mathchar 287$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle\mathchar 287$}}}}^{j}

(97)

with the expansions

\mathcal{K}_{ij}^{\mathrm{su}_{\mathrm{3}}}=\sum_{k,l=1}^{2}x_{i}^{k}K_{kl}^{\mathrm{su}_{\mathrm{3}}}x_{j}^{l}\qquad,\qquad\mathcal{\tilde{K}}_{\mathrm{su}_{\mathrm{3}}}^{ij}=\sum_{k,l=1}^{2}y_{k}^{i}\tilde{K}_{\mathrm{su}_{\mathrm{3}}}^{kl}y_{l}^{j}

(98)

where
K_{kl}^{\mathrm{su}_{\mathrm{3}}}=\mathbf{{{}\mathchoice{\mbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle\mathchar 267$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\textstyle\mathchar 267$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle\mathchar 267$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle\mathchar 267$}}}}_{i}.\mathbf{{{}\mathchoice{\mbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle\mathchar 267$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\textstyle\mathchar 267$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle\mathchar 267$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle\mathchar 267$}}}}_{j}
and
\tilde{K}_{\mathrm{su}_{\mathrm{3}}}^{kl}=\mathbf{{{}\mathchoice{\mbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle\mathchar 277$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\textstyle\mathchar 277$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle\mathchar 277$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle\mathchar 277$}}}}^{i}.\mathbf{{{}\mathchoice{\mbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle\mathchar 277$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\textstyle\mathchar 277$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle\mathchar 277$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle\mathchar 277$}}}}^{j}.
These intersection matrices
\mathcal{K}_{ij}^{\mathrm{su}_{\mathrm{3}}}
and
\mathcal{\tilde{K}}_{\mathrm{su}_{\mathrm{3}}}^{ij}
are functions of
x_{i}^{k}
and their inverse. For the generic
case, the quantized momenta read as follows

\boldsymbol{p}_{L}

=

\frac{1}{\sqrt{2}}\mathop{\displaystyle\sum}\limits_{i=1}^{2}\left(n^{i}\mathbf{{{}\mathchoice{\mbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle\mathchar 268$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\textstyle\mathchar 268$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle\mathchar 268$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle\mathchar 268$}}}}_{i}+w_{i}\mathbf{{{}\mathchoice{\mbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle\mathchar 287$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\textstyle\mathchar 287$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle\mathchar 287$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle\mathchar 287$}}}}^{i}\right)

\boldsymbol{p}_{R}

=

\frac{1}{\sqrt{2}}\mathop{\displaystyle\sum}\limits_{i=1}^{2}\left(n^{i}\mathbf{{{}\mathchoice{\mbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle\mathchar 268$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\textstyle\mathchar 268$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle\mathchar 268$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle\mathchar 268$}}}}_{i}-w_{i}\mathbf{{{}\mathchoice{\mbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle\mathchar 287$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\textstyle\mathchar 287$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle\mathchar 287$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle\mathchar 287$}}}}^{i}\right)

(99)

from which we deduce the vielbeins

\boldsymbol{E}_{\text{ {a}}}^{\text{{\@text@baccent{m}}}}=\frac{1}{\sqrt{2}}\left(\begin{array}[]{cc}\mathbf{{{}\mathchoice{\mbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle\mathchar 268$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\textstyle\mathchar 268$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle\mathchar 268$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle\mathchar 268$}}}}_{i}&+\mathbf{{{}\mathchoice{\mbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle\mathchar 287$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\textstyle\mathchar 287$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle\mathchar 287$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle\mathchar 287$}}}}^{j}\\
\mathbf{{{}\mathchoice{\mbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle\mathchar 268$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\textstyle\mathchar 268$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle\mathchar 268$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle\mathchar 268$}}}}_{i}&\mathbf{{{}\mathchoice{\mbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle\mathchar 287$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\textstyle\mathchar 287$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle\mathchar 287$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle\mathchar 287$}}}}^{j}\end{array}\right)

(100)

satisfying
\mathbf{Q}_{\text{{ab}}}=\boldsymbol{E}_{\text{{a}}}^{\text{ {\@text@baccent{m}}}}{{}\mathchoice{\mbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle\mathchar 273$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\textstyle\mathchar 273$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle\mathchar 273$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle\mathchar 273$}}}_{\text{{\@text@baccent{m}\@text@baccent{n}}}}\boldsymbol{E}_{\text{ {b}}}^{\text{{\@text@baccent{n}}}}
meaning that they are invariant
under
\mathcal{O}(2,2)/\left[\mathcal{O}(2)_{L}\times\mathcal{O}(2)_{R}\right].
The
\mathcal{O}(2,2)
acts on
\boldsymbol{E}_{\text{{a}}}^{\text{{\@text@baccent{m}}}};
it mixes
\mathbf{{{}\mathchoice{\mbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle\mathchar 268$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\textstyle\mathchar 268$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle\mathchar 268$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle\mathchar 268$}}}}_{i}
and
\mathbf{{{}\mathchoice{\mbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle\mathchar 287$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\textstyle\mathchar 287$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle\mathchar 287$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle\mathchar 287$}}}}^{j}
while preserving the pseudo-metric
{{}\mathchoice{\mbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle\mathchar 273$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\textstyle\mathchar 273$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle\mathchar 273$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle\mathchar 273$}}}_{\text{{\@text@baccent{m}\@text@baccent{n}}}}.
The
\mathcal{O}(2)_{L}
rotates the two
\mathbf{{{}\mathchoice{\mbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle\mathchar 268$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\textstyle\mathchar 268$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle\mathchar 268$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle\mathchar 268$}}}}_{i}
’s like
\mathbf{{{}\mathchoice{\mbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle\mathchar 268$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\textstyle\mathchar 268$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle\mathchar 268$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle\mathchar 268$}}}}_{i}^{\prime}=\left(\mathcal{O}_{L}\right)_{i}^{k}\mathbf{{{}\mathchoice{\mbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle\mathchar 268$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\textstyle\mathchar 268$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle\mathchar 268$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle\mathchar 268$}}}}_{k}
, it preserves
\mathbf{{{}\mathchoice{\mbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle\mathchar 268$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\textstyle\mathchar 268$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle\mathchar 268$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle\mathchar 268$}}}}_{i}.\mathbf{{{}\mathchoice{\mbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle\mathchar 268$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\textstyle\mathchar 268$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle\mathchar 268$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle\mathchar 268$}}}}_{j}
. The
\mathcal{O}(2)_{R}
rotates the two
\mathbf{{{}\mathchoice{\mbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle\mathchar 287$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\textstyle\mathchar 287$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle\mathchar 287$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle\mathchar 287$}}}}^{j}
’s; it acts as
\mathbf{{{}\mathchoice{\mbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle\mathchar 287$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\textstyle\mathchar 287$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle\mathchar 287$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle\mathchar 287$}}}}^{j\prime}=\left(\mathcal{O}_{R}\right)_{l}^{j}\mathbf{{{}\mathchoice{\mbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle\mathchar 287$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\textstyle\mathchar 287$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle\mathchar 287$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle\mathchar 287$}}}}^{l}
and preserves
\mathbf{{{}\mathchoice{\mbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle\mathchar 287$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\textstyle\mathchar 287$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle\mathchar 287$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle\mathchar 287$}}}}^{i}.\mathbf{{{}\mathchoice{\mbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle\mathchar 287$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\textstyle\mathchar 287$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle\mathchar 287$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle\mathchar 287$}}}}^{j}.
Moreover, the
property
\mathbf{{{}\mathchoice{\mbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle\mathchar 268$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\textstyle\mathchar 268$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle\mathchar 268$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle\mathchar 268$}}}}_{i}.\mathbf{{{}\mathchoice{\mbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle\mathchar 287$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\textstyle\mathchar 287$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle\mathchar 287$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle\mathchar 287$}}}}^{j}={{}\mathchoice{\mbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle\mathchar 270$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\textstyle\mathchar 270$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle\mathchar 270$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle\mathchar 270$}}}_{i}^{j}
requires
that
\mathcal{O}_{R}=\mathcal{O}_{L}^{-1}.

By substituting
\mathbf{{{}\mathchoice{\mbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle\mathchar 268$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\textstyle\mathchar 268$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle\mathchar 268$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle\mathchar 268$}}}}_{i}=\sum_{k=1}^{2}x_{i}^{k}\mathbf{{{}\mathchoice{\mbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle\mathchar 267$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\textstyle\mathchar 267$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle\mathchar 267$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle\mathchar 267$}}}}_{k}
and
\mathbf{{{}\mathchoice{\mbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle\mathchar 287$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\textstyle\mathchar 287$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle\mathchar 287$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle\mathchar 287$}}}}^{i}=\sum_{l=1}^{2}y_{l}^{j}\mathbf{{{}\mathchoice{\mbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle\mathchar 277$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\textstyle\mathchar 277$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle\mathchar 277$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle\mathchar 277$}}}}^{l}
,
we get

\boldsymbol{p}_{L}

=

\frac{1}{\sqrt{2}}\mathop{\displaystyle\sum}\limits_{i,k=1}^{2}\left(n^{i}x_{i}^{k}K_{kl}+w_{j}y_{l}^{j}\right)\mathbf{{{}\mathchoice{\mbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle\mathchar 277$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\textstyle\mathchar 277$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle\mathchar 277$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle\mathchar 277$}}}}^{l}

\boldsymbol{p}_{R}

=

\frac{1}{\sqrt{2}}\mathop{\displaystyle\sum}\limits_{i,k=1}^{2}\left(n^{i}x_{i}^{k}K_{kl}-w_{j}y_{l}^{j}\right)\mathbf{{{}\mathchoice{\mbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle\mathchar 277$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\textstyle\mathchar 277$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle\mathchar 277$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle\mathchar 277$}}}}^{l}

(101)

In what follows, we often restrict the analysis to the
diagonal
case
where the scaled roots and the scaled weights simplify as,

\begin{tabular}[]{lll}$\mathbf{{{}\mathchoice{\mbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle\mathchar 268$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\textstyle\mathchar 268$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle\mathchar 268$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle\mathchar 268$}}}}_{1}$&$=$&$x_{1}\mathbf{{{}\mathchoice{\mbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle\mathchar 267$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\textstyle\mathchar 267$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle\mathchar 267$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle\mathchar 267$}}}}_{1}$\\
$\mathbf{{{}\mathchoice{\mbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle\mathchar 268$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\textstyle\mathchar 268$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle\mathchar 268$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle\mathchar 268$}}}}_{2}$&$=$&$x_{2}\mathbf{{{}\mathchoice{\mbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle\mathchar 267$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\textstyle\mathchar 267$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle\mathchar 267$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle\mathchar 267$}}}}_{2}$\end{tabular}\qquad,\qquad\begin{tabular}[]{lll}$x_{1}$&$=$&$\mathbf{{{}\mathchoice{\mbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle\mathchar 268$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\textstyle\mathchar 268$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle\mathchar 268$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle\mathchar 268$}}}}_{1}.\mathbf{{{}\mathchoice{\mbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle\mathchar 277$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\textstyle\mathchar 277$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle\mathchar 277$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle\mathchar 277$}}}}_{1}$\\
$x_{2}$&$=$&$\mathbf{{{}\mathchoice{\mbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle\mathchar 268$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\textstyle\mathchar 268$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle\mathchar 268$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle\mathchar 268$}}}}_{2}.\mathbf{{{}\mathchoice{\mbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle\mathchar 277$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\textstyle\mathchar 277$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle\mathchar 277$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle\mathchar 277$}}}}_{2}$\end{tabular}

(102)

and

\begin{tabular}[]{lll}$\mathbf{{{}\mathchoice{\mbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle\mathchar 287$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\textstyle\mathchar 287$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle\mathchar 287$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle\mathchar 287$}}}}_{1}$&$=$&$\frac{1}{x_{1}}\mathbf{{{}\mathchoice{\mbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle\mathchar 277$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\textstyle\mathchar 277$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle\mathchar 277$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle\mathchar 277$}}}}_{1}$\\
$\mathbf{{{}\mathchoice{\mbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle\mathchar 287$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\textstyle\mathchar 287$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle\mathchar 287$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle\mathchar 287$}}}}_{2}$&$=$&$\frac{1}{x_{2}}\mathbf{{{}\mathchoice{\mbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle\mathchar 277$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\textstyle\mathchar 277$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle\mathchar 277$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle\mathchar 277$}}}}_{2}$\end{tabular}\qquad,\qquad\begin{tabular}[]{lll}$\mathbf{{{}\mathchoice{\mbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle\mathchar 287$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\textstyle\mathchar 287$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle\mathchar 287$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle\mathchar 287$}}}}_{1}$&$=$&$\frac{1}{3x_{1}}\left(2\mathbf{{{}\mathchoice{\mbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle\mathchar 267$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\textstyle\mathchar 267$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle\mathchar 267$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle\mathchar 267$}}}}_{1}+\mathbf{{{}\mathchoice{\mbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle\mathchar 267$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\textstyle\mathchar 267$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle\mathchar 267$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle\mathchar 267$}}}}_{2}\right)$\\
$\mathbf{{{}\mathchoice{\mbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle\mathchar 287$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\textstyle\mathchar 287$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle\mathchar 287$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle\mathchar 287$}}}}_{2}$&$=$&$\frac{1}{3x_{2}}\left(\mathbf{{{}\mathchoice{\mbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle\mathchar 267$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\textstyle\mathchar 267$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle\mathchar 267$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle\mathchar 267$}}}}_{1}+2\mathbf{{{}\mathchoice{\mbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle\mathchar 267$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\textstyle\mathchar 267$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle\mathchar 267$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle\mathchar 267$}}}}_{2}\right)$\end{tabular}

(103)

with positive definite moduli
x_{i}=1/(2R_{i}).
This permits to fix ideas
and to get an overview; the general relationships follow straightforwardly.

3.2
Results for diagonal NCFT
{}_{2}^{\mathtt{su}_{3}}

With the above diagonal scaled cycles, we derive the particular intersection
matrices
\mathbf{{{}\mathchoice{\mbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle\mathchar 268$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\textstyle\mathchar 268$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle\mathchar 268$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle\mathchar 268$}}}}_{i}.\mathbf{{{}\mathchoice{\mbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle\mathchar 268$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\textstyle\mathchar 268$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle\mathchar 268$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle\mathchar 268$}}}}_{j}=\mathcal{K}_{ij}^{su_{3}}

and
\mathbf{{{}\mathchoice{\mbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle\mathchar 287$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\textstyle\mathchar 287$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle\mathchar 287$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle\mathchar 287$}}}}^{i}.\mathbf{{{}\mathchoice{\mbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle\mathchar 287$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\textstyle\mathchar 287$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle\mathchar 287$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle\mathchar 287$}}}}^{j}=\mathcal{\tilde{K}}_{su_{3}}^{ij}
which explicitly read as follows,

\mathcal{K}_{ij}^{su_{3}}=\left(\begin{array}[]{cc}2x_{1}^{2}&-x_{1}x_{2}\\
-x_{1}x_{2}&2x_{2}^{2}\end{array}\right)\qquad,\qquad\mathcal{\tilde{K}}_{su_{3}}^{ij}=\left(\begin{array}[]{cc}\frac{2}{3x_{1}^{2}}&\frac{1}{3x_{1}x_{2}}\\
\frac{1}{3x_{1}x_{2}}&\frac{2}{3x_{2}^{2}}\end{array}\right)

(104)

From these matrices, we learn that
\mathbf{{{}\mathchoice{\mbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle\mathchar 268$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\textstyle\mathchar 268$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle\mathchar 268$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle\mathchar 268$}}}}_{i}^{2}=2x_{i}^{2}
and
\mathbf{{{}\mathchoice{\mbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle\mathchar 287$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\textstyle\mathchar 287$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle\mathchar 287$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle\mathchar 287$}}}}_{i}^{2}=2/(3x_{i}^{2}).
We also have the following
expressions for the left and the right momenta

\boldsymbol{p}_{L}\left(x_{1},x_{2}\right)

=

\frac{1}{\sqrt{2}}\mathop{\displaystyle\sum}\limits_{i=1}^{2}\left(\mathcal{K}_{ij}n^{j}+w_{i}\right)\mathbf{{{}\mathchoice{\mbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle\mathchar 287$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\textstyle\mathchar 287$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle\mathchar 287$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle\mathchar 287$}}}}^{i}

\boldsymbol{p}_{R}\left(x_{1},x_{2}\right)

=

\frac{1}{\sqrt{2}}\mathop{\displaystyle\sum}\limits_{i=1}^{2}\left(\mathcal{K}_{ij}n^{j}-w_{i}\right)\mathbf{{{}\mathchoice{\mbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle\mathchar 287$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\textstyle\mathchar 287$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle\mathchar 287$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle\mathchar 287$}}}}^{i}

(105)

they lead to the matrix

\boldsymbol{A}_{\mathtt{su}_{3}}\left(x\right)={{}\mathchoice{\mbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle\mathchar 281$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\textstyle\mathchar 281$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle\mathchar 281$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle\mathchar 281$}}}\left(\begin{array}[]{cc}-{{}\mathchoice{\mbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle\mathchar 284$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\textstyle\mathchar 284$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle\mathchar 284$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle\mathchar 284$}}}_{2}\mathcal{K}_{ij}&i{{}\mathchoice{\mbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle\mathchar 284$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\textstyle\mathchar 284$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle\mathchar 284$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle\mathchar 284$}}}_{1}{{}\mathchoice{\mbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle\mathchar 270$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\textstyle\mathchar 270$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle\mathchar 270$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle\mathchar 270$}}}_{i}^{\text{ \ }l}\\
i{{}\mathchoice{\mbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle\mathchar 284$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\textstyle\mathchar 284$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle\mathchar 284$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle\mathchar 284$}}}_{1}{{}\mathchoice{\mbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle\mathchar 270$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\textstyle\mathchar 270$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle\mathchar 270$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle\mathchar 270$}}}_{\text{ \ }j}^{k}&-{{}\mathchoice{\mbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle\mathchar 284$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\textstyle\mathchar 284$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle\mathchar 284$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle\mathchar 284$}}}_{2}\mathcal{\tilde{K}}^{kl}\end{array}\right)

(106)

3.2.1
Metric of
\mathcal{M}_{\mathbf{su}_{3}}
and partition
function
Z_{\mathbf{su}_{3}}

The realisations (
104
-
105
) allow the computation of interesting
quantities characterising the (diagonal) NCFT
{}_{2}^{\mathtt{su}_{3}}
, in
particular the two following:

\left(\mathbf{1}\right)

Metric of

\mathcal{M}_{su_{3}}
: the metric of the Narain moduli space generalises (
67
) to the rank
2 conformal models. The length
\mathbf{{{}\mathchoice{\mbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle\mathchar 268$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\textstyle\mathchar 268$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle\mathchar 268$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle\mathchar 268$}}}}^{2}=2x^{2}
in the su(2)
theory extends to the intersection matrix
(\mathcal{K}_{su_{3}})_{ij}=\mathbf{{{}\mathchoice{\mbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle\mathchar 268$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\textstyle\mathchar 268$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle\mathchar 268$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle\mathchar 268$}}}}_{i}.\mathbf{{{}\mathchoice{\mbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle\mathchar 268$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\textstyle\mathchar 268$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle\mathchar 268$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle\mathchar 268$}}}}_{j},
and the 1-form
\mathcal{K}_{su_{2}}^{-1}d\mathcal{K}_{su_{2}}
becomes the generalised 2
\times
2
matrix 1-form
\mathcal{K}_{su_{3}}^{-1}d\mathcal{K}_{su_{3}}
reading as
follows

d{{}\mathchoice{\mbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle\mathchar 285$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\textstyle\mathchar 285$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle\mathchar 285$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle\mathchar 285$}}}_{j}^{i}=\sum_{k=1}^{2}\left(\mathcal{K}_{su_{3}}^{-1}\right)^{ik}\left(d\mathcal{K}_{su_{3}}\right)_{kj}

(107)

Using the
\mathcal{K}_{su_{3}}
and
\mathcal{K}_{su_{3}}^{-1}
matrices of
(
104
), we calculate

d\mathcal{K}_{ij}^{su_{3}}=\left(\begin{array}[]{cc}4x_{1}dx_{1}&-dx_{1}x_{2}-x_{1}dx_{2}\\
-dx_{1}x_{2}-x_{1}dx_{2}&4x_{2}dx_{2}\end{array}\right)

(108)

and then

d{{}\mathchoice{\mbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle\mathchar 285$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\textstyle\mathchar 285$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle\mathchar 285$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle\mathchar 285$}}}_{j}^{i}=\left(\begin{array}[]{cc}\frac{7}{3x_{1}}&-\frac{2x_{2}}{3x_{1}^{2}}\\
\frac{2}{3x_{2}}&-\frac{1}{3x_{1}}\end{array}\right)dx_{1}+\left(\begin{array}[]{cc}-\frac{1}{3x_{2}}&\frac{2}{3x_{1}}\\
-\frac{2x_{1}}{3x_{2}^{2}}&\frac{7}{3x_{2}}\end{array}\right)dx_{2}

(109)

In term of this matrix 1-form
d{{}\mathchoice{\mbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle\mathchar 285$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\textstyle\mathchar 285$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle\mathchar 285$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle\mathchar 285$}}}_{j}^{i}
, the metric
ds_{su_{3}}^{2}
of the Narain moduli space is given by the trace
(d{{}\mathchoice{\mbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle\mathchar 285$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\textstyle\mathchar 285$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle\mathchar 285$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle\mathchar 285$}}}_{j}^{i})(d{{}\mathchoice{\mbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle\mathchar 285$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\textstyle\mathchar 285$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle\mathchar 285$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle\mathchar 285$}}}_{i}^{j});
it is expressed like

ds_{su_{3}}^{2}=\left(\mathcal{K}_{su_{3}}^{-1}\right)^{ik}\left(\mathcal{K}_{su_{3}}^{-1}\right)^{jl}\left(d\mathcal{K}_{su_{3}}\right)_{kj}\left(d\mathcal{K}_{su_{3}}\right)_{li}

(110)

which explicitly reads as:

\displaystyle ds_{su_{3}}^{2}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\left(\frac{50}{9x_{1}^{2}}+\frac{4}{9x_{2}^{2}}+\frac{4x_{2}^{2}}{9x_{1}^{4}}\right)dx_{1}^{2}+\left(\frac{50}{9x_{2}^{2}}+\frac{4}{9x_{1}^{2}}+\frac{4x_{1}^{2}}{9x_{2}^{4}}\right)dx_{2}^{2}

(111)

\displaystyle-\left(\frac{28}{9x_{1}x_{2}}+\frac{8x_{1}}{9x_{2}^{3}}+\frac{8x_{2}}{9x_{1}^{3}}\right)dx_{1}dx_{2}

\left(\mathbf{2}\right)

Partition function of NCFT
{}_{2}^{\mathtt{su}_{3}}
: the genus-one partition function of the Narain CFT
{}_{2}^{\mathtt{su}_{3}}
is defined as follows

\displaystyle Z_{\mathrm{su}_{3}}\left[{{}\mathchoice{\mbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle\mathchar 284$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\textstyle\mathchar 284$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle\mathchar 284$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle\mathchar 284$}}},\bar{{{}\mathchoice{\mbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle\mathchar 284$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\textstyle\mathchar 284$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle\mathchar 284$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle\mathchar 284$}}}};x\right]

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\frac{1}{\left|{{}\mathchoice{\mbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle\mathchar 273$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\textstyle\mathchar 273$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle\mathchar 273$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle\mathchar 273$}}}\left({{}\mathchoice{\mbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle\mathchar 284$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\textstyle\mathchar 284$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle\mathchar 284$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle\mathchar 284$}}}\right)\right|^{4}}\mathop{\displaystyle\sum}\limits_{\left(p_{L},p_{R}\right)}q^{\frac{1}{2}p_{L}^{2}}\bar{q}^{\frac{1}{2}p_{R}^{2}}

(112)

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\frac{1}{\left|{{}\mathchoice{\mbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle\mathchar 273$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\textstyle\mathchar 273$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle\mathchar 273$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle\mathchar 273$}}}\left({{}\mathchoice{\mbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle\mathchar 284$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\textstyle\mathchar 284$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle\mathchar 284$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle\mathchar 284$}}}\right)\right|^{4}}\mathop{\displaystyle\sum}\limits_{(\mathfrak{Q}_{L}^{\mathtt{su}_{3}},\mathfrak{Q}_{R}^{\mathtt{su}_{3}})}q^{\frac{1}{2}\mathfrak{Q}_{L}^{\mathtt{su}_{3}}}\bar{q}^{\frac{1}{2}\mathfrak{Q}_{R}^{\mathtt{su}_{3}}}

Or equivalently, by substituting
\mathfrak{Q}_{L}^{\mathtt{su}_{3}}
and
\mathfrak{Q}_{R}^{\mathtt{su}_{3}}
:

Z_{\mathrm{su}_{3}}\left[{{}\mathchoice{\mbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle\mathchar 284$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\textstyle\mathchar 284$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle\mathchar 284$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle\mathchar 284$}}},\bar{{{}\mathchoice{\mbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle\mathchar 284$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\textstyle\mathchar 284$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle\mathchar 284$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle\mathchar 284$}}}};x\right]=\frac{1}{\left|{{}\mathchoice{\mbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle\mathchar 273$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\textstyle\mathchar 273$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle\mathchar 273$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle\mathchar 273$}}}\left({{}\mathchoice{\mbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle\mathchar 284$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\textstyle\mathchar 284$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle\mathchar 284$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle\mathchar 284$}}}\right)\right|^{4}}\mathop{\displaystyle\sum}\limits_{\boldsymbol{m}\in\mathbb{Z}^{2}}\exp\left(\boldsymbol{m}^{T}.\boldsymbol{A}_{\mathrm{su}_{3}}.\boldsymbol{m}\right)

(113)

with
\boldsymbol{A}_{\mathrm{su}_{3}}=i{{}\mathchoice{\mbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle\mathchar 281$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\textstyle\mathchar 281$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle\mathchar 281$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle\mathchar 281$}}}\left({{}\mathchoice{\mbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle\mathchar 284$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\textstyle\mathchar 284$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle\mathchar 284$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle\mathchar 284$}}}\boldsymbol{Q}_{L}-\bar{{{}\mathchoice{\mbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle\mathchar 284$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\textstyle\mathchar 284$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle\mathchar 284$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle\mathchar 284$}}}}\boldsymbol{Q}_{R}\right)
a 4
\times
4 matrix reading like

\boldsymbol{A}_{\mathrm{su}_{3}}=i{{}\mathchoice{\mbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle\mathchar 281$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\textstyle\mathchar 281$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle\mathchar 281$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle\mathchar 281$}}}{{}\mathchoice{\mbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle\mathchar 284$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\textstyle\mathchar 284$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle\mathchar 284$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle\mathchar 284$}}}_{1}\boldsymbol{Q}_{\mathrm{su}_{3}}-{{}\mathchoice{\mbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle\mathchar 281$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\textstyle\mathchar 281$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle\mathchar 281$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle\mathchar 281$}}}{{}\mathchoice{\mbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle\mathchar 284$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\textstyle\mathchar 284$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle\mathchar 284$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle\mathchar 284$}}}_{2}\boldsymbol{H}_{\mathrm{su}_{3}}

(114)

where the
\boldsymbol{H}_{\mathrm{su}_{3}}
and the
\boldsymbol{Q}_{\mathrm{su}_{3}}
matrices are as in eq(

) with
\mathcal{K}_{ij}

and
\mathcal{\tilde{K}}^{kl}
given by eq(

). By replacing, we
have the following moduli dependent matrix

\boldsymbol{A}_{\mathrm{su}_{3}}\left(x\right)={{}\mathchoice{\mbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle\mathchar 281$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\textstyle\mathchar 281$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle\mathchar 281$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle\mathchar 281$}}}\left(\begin{array}[]{cccc}-2\mathrm{{{}\mathchoice{\mbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle\mathchar 284$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\textstyle\mathchar 284$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle\mathchar 284$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle\mathchar 284$}}}}_{2}x_{1}^{2}&\mathrm{{{}\mathchoice{\mbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle\mathchar 284$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\textstyle\mathchar 284$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle\mathchar 284$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle\mathchar 284$}}}}_{2}x_{1}x_{2}&i\mathrm{{{}\mathchoice{\mbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle\mathchar 284$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\textstyle\mathchar 284$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle\mathchar 284$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle\mathchar 284$}}}}_{1}&0\\
\mathrm{{{}\mathchoice{\mbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle\mathchar 284$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\textstyle\mathchar 284$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle\mathchar 284$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle\mathchar 284$}}}}_{2}x_{1}x_{2}&-2\mathrm{{{}\mathchoice{\mbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle\mathchar 284$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\textstyle\mathchar 284$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle\mathchar 284$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle\mathchar 284$}}}}_{2}x_{2}^{2}&0&i\mathrm{{{}\mathchoice{\mbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle\mathchar 284$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\textstyle\mathchar 284$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle\mathchar 284$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle\mathchar 284$}}}}_{1}\\

(115)

with

and moduli-independent determinant

At the
point

it reduces to:

\left.\boldsymbol{A}_{\mathrm{su}_{3}}\right|_{x_{i}=1}=\left(\begin{array}[]{cc}-{{}\mathchoice{\mbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle\mathchar 281$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\textstyle\mathchar 281$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle\mathchar 281$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle\mathchar 281$}}}\mathrm{{{}\mathchoice{\mbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle\mathchar 284$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\textstyle\mathchar 284$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle\mathchar 284$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle\mathchar 284$}}}}_{2}K_{\mathtt{su}_{3}}&i{{}\mathchoice{\mbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle\mathchar 281$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\textstyle\mathchar 281$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle\mathchar 281$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle\mathchar 281$}}}\mathrm{{{}\mathchoice{\mbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle\mathchar 284$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\textstyle\mathchar 284$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle\mathchar 284$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle\mathchar 284$}}}}_{1}I_{{\small 2}}\\

(116)

3.2.2
Flux moduli for so(5) and G
2

Following
[
46
]
, the generalised relations of compactified momentums
can be also presented in terms of the symmetric metric
\mathcal{G}_{ij}
of
the moduli space (
76
) and the antisymmetric flux
\mathcal{B}_{ij}

as follows

p_{L,i}

=

n_{i}+\frac{1}{2}\left(\mathcal{G}_{ij}-\mathcal{B}_{ij}\right)w^{j}

p_{R,i}

=

n_{i}-\frac{1}{2}\left(\mathcal{G}_{ij}+\mathcal{B}_{ij}\right)w^{j}

(117)

Using this realisation, the vielbein
\mathfrak{p}^{\text{{\@text@baccent{n}}}}=E_{\text{ {b}}}^{\text{{\@text@baccent{n}}}}m^{\text{{b}}}

relation permits to rewrite
\mathfrak{p}^{\text{{\@text@baccent{m}}}}{{}\mathchoice{\mbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle\mathchar 273$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\textstyle\mathchar 273$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle\mathchar 273$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle\mathchar 273$}}}_{\text{{\@text@baccent{m}\@text@baccent{n}}}}\mathfrak{p}^{\text{{\@text@baccent{n}}}}
like the
quadratic form
m^{\text{{a}}}\mathbf{Q}_{\text{{ab}}}m^{\text{{b}}}
having the structure,

E_{\text{ {a}}}^{\text{{\@text@baccent{m}}}}=\left(\begin{array}[]{cc}{{}\mathchoice{\mbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle\mathchar 270$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\textstyle\mathchar 270$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle\mathchar 270$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle\mathchar 270$}}}_{ij}&+\frac{1}{2}\left(\mathcal{G}_{il}-\mathcal{B}_{il}\right)\\
{{}\mathchoice{\mbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle\mathchar 270$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\textstyle\mathchar 270$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle\mathchar 270$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle\mathchar 270$}}}_{kj}&-\frac{1}{2}\left(\mathcal{G}_{kl}+\mathcal{B}_{kl}\right)\end{array}\right)\qquad,\qquad\mathbf{Q}_{\text{{ab}}}=\left(\begin{array}[]{cc}0&I_{d}\\
I_{d}&0\end{array}\right)

(118)

By turning off the flux
\mathcal{B}_{ij}
and restricting the metric
\mathcal{G}_{ij}
to
{{}\mathchoice{\mbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle\mathchar 270$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\textstyle\mathchar 270$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle\mathchar 270$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle\mathchar 270$}}}_{ij},
we get

p_{L,i}

=

n_{i}+\frac{1}{2}{{}\mathchoice{\mbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle\mathchar 270$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\textstyle\mathchar 270$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle\mathchar 270$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle\mathchar 270$}}}_{ij}w^{j}

p_{R,a}

=

n_{a}-\frac{1}{2}{{}\mathchoice{\mbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle\mathchar 270$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\textstyle\mathchar 270$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle\mathchar 270$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle\mathchar 270$}}}_{ij}w^{j}

(119)

From these relations, we deduce

E_{\text{ {a}}}^{\text{{\@text@baccent{m}}}}=\left(\begin{array}[]{cc}{{}\mathchoice{\mbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle\mathchar 270$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\textstyle\mathchar 270$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle\mathchar 270$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle\mathchar 270$}}}_{ij}&+\frac{1}{2}{{}\mathchoice{\mbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle\mathchar 270$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\textstyle\mathchar 270$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle\mathchar 270$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle\mathchar 270$}}}_{ij}\\
{{}\mathchoice{\mbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle\mathchar 270$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\textstyle\mathchar 270$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle\mathchar 270$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle\mathchar 270$}}}_{kj}&-\frac{1}{2}{{}\mathchoice{\mbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle\mathchar 270$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\textstyle\mathchar 270$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle\mathchar 270$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle\mathchar 270$}}}_{ij}\end{array}\right)

(120)

while
\mathbf{Q}_{\text{{ab}}}
remains unchanged as it is
independent of the moduli.

To compare (
117
) with our realisation using the matrix moduli
x_{i}^{k}
, we consider the parametrisation (
99
) and perform its
projection along the
\sqrt{2}\mathbf{{{}\mathchoice{\mbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle\mathchar 287$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\textstyle\mathchar 287$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle\mathchar 287$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle\mathchar 287$}}}}^{i}
directions. Setting

p
{}_{L/R}^{i}=\sqrt{2}\boldsymbol{p}_{L/R}.\mathbf{{{}\mathchoice{\mbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle\mathchar 287$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\textstyle\mathchar 287$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle\mathchar 287$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle\mathchar 287$}}}}^{i}
, this
yields

\text{{p}}_{L}^{k}

=

n^{k}+w_{i}\mathcal{K}^{ik}

\text{{p}}_{R}^{k}

=

n^{k}-\mathcal{K}^{ki}w_{i}

(121)

By comparing these relationships with (
119
), we learn that non
vanishing flux
\mathcal{B}_{ij}
requires non symmetric Cartan matrix
\mathcal{K}_{ik}=\mathbf{{{}\mathchoice{\mbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle\mathchar 267$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\textstyle\mathchar 267$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle\mathchar 267$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle\mathchar 267$}}}}_{i}^{\vee}.\mathbf{{{}\mathchoice{\mbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle\mathchar 267$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\textstyle\mathchar 267$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle\mathchar 267$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle\mathchar 267$}}}}_{j}
of rank
2 Lie algebras. This is effectively the case of the NCFT
{}_{2}^{\mathbf{g}}
s
with underlying rank 2 symmetries such as the orthogonal so(5) and the
exceptional G
2
with Cartan matrices as follows

K_{ij}^{\mathbf{so}_{\mathbf{5}}}=\left(\begin{array}[]{cc}2&-2\\
-1&2\end{array}\right)\qquad,\qquad\tilde{K}_{\mathbf{so}_{\mathbf{5}}}^{ij}=\frac{1}{2}\left(\begin{array}[]{cc}2&2\\
1&2\end{array}\right)

(122)

and

K_{ij}^{\mathbf{G}_{2}}=\left(\begin{array}[]{cc}2&-3\\
-1&2\end{array}\right)\qquad,\qquad\tilde{K}_{\mathbf{G}_{2}}^{ij}=\left(\begin{array}[]{cc}2&3\\
1&2\end{array}\right)

(123)

4
Higher rank NCFT
{}_{2}^{\text{{\@text@baccent{g}}}}

In this section, we extend the analysis of the previous
descriptions to higher rank symmetries. Specifically, we study
Narain sigma-models with target torus
\mathbb{T}^{r}\simeq U\left(1\right)^{r}
for the unitary family characterised by the Lie algebra

\mathbf{g}_{r}=su(r+1)\qquad,\qquad u\left(1\right)^{r}\subset\mathbf{g}_{r}

(124)

We then derive results for the other families appearing in eq(
4
), classified by finite dimensional Lie algebras
\mathbf{g}_{r}
. For
convenience, our focus will be directed towards simply laced Lie algebras; a
similar description holds also for non simply laced symmetries.

4.1
Narain CFT
{}_{2}^{\mathbf{su}_{r+1}}

In the case of Narain CFT
{}_{2}^{\mathrm{su}_{\mathrm{r+1}}}
, the
left
\boldsymbol{p}_{L}
and the right
\boldsymbol{p}_{R}
momenta of the
r two dimensional scalar fields
X^{i}=(X_{L}^{i}+X_{R}^{i})/\sqrt{2}

compactified on the r-dimensional torus
\mathbb{T}^{r}=\otimes_{i=1}^{r}\mathbb{S}_{i}^{1}
are given by the following linear combinations

\boldsymbol{p}_{L}

=

\frac{1}{\sqrt{2}}\mathop{\displaystyle\sum}\limits_{i=1}^{r}\left(n^{i}\mathbf{{{}\mathchoice{\mbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle\mathchar 268$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\textstyle\mathchar 268$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle\mathchar 268$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle\mathchar 268$}}}}_{i}+w_{i}\mathbf{{{}\mathchoice{\mbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle\mathchar 287$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\textstyle\mathchar 287$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle\mathchar 287$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle\mathchar 287$}}}}^{i}\right)

\boldsymbol{p}_{R}

=

\frac{1}{\sqrt{2}}\mathop{\displaystyle\sum}\limits_{i=1}^{r}\left(n^{i}\mathbf{{{}\mathchoice{\mbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle\mathchar 268$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\textstyle\mathchar 268$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle\mathchar 268$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle\mathchar 268$}}}}_{i}-w_{i}\mathbf{{{}\mathchoice{\mbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle\mathchar 287$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\textstyle\mathchar 287$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle\mathchar 287$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle\mathchar 287$}}}}^{i}\right)

(125)

with the constraint relation
\mathbf{{{}\mathchoice{\mbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle\mathchar 268$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\textstyle\mathchar 268$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle\mathchar 268$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle\mathchar 268$}}}}_{i}.\mathbf{{{}\mathchoice{\mbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle\mathchar 287$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\textstyle\mathchar 287$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle\mathchar 287$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle\mathchar 287$}}}}^{j}={{}\mathchoice{\mbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle\mathchar 270$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\textstyle\mathchar 270$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle\mathchar 270$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle\mathchar 270$}}}_{i}^{j}
. Here, the
\mathbf{{{}\mathchoice{\mbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle\mathchar 268$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\textstyle\mathchar 268$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle\mathchar 268$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle\mathchar 268$}}}}_{i}
s and the
\mathbf{{{}\mathchoice{\mbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle\mathchar 287$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\textstyle\mathchar 287$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle\mathchar 287$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle\mathchar 287$}}}}^{i}
’s
are linear combinations of the scaled roots
\sum_{k=1}^{r}x_{i}^{k}\mathbf{{{}\mathchoice{\mbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle\mathchar 267$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\textstyle\mathchar 267$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle\mathchar 267$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle\mathchar 267$}}}}_{k}
and the scaled weights
\sum_{l=1}^{2}y_{l}^{j}\mathbf{{{}\mathchoice{\mbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle\mathchar 277$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\textstyle\mathchar 277$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle\mathchar 277$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle\mathchar 277$}}}}^{l}
. The coefficients are conditioned by the duality relation
where
y_{k}^{i}
is the inverse of
x_{i}^{k};
this leaves the
r^{2}
moduli that parameterise the moduli space
of the NCFT
{}_{2}^{\mathrm{su}_{\mathrm{r+1}}}
. The corresponding
intersection matrices
\mathcal{K}_{kl}^{\mathrm{su}_{\mathrm{r+1}}}=\mathbf{{{}\mathchoice{\mbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle\mathchar 268$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\textstyle\mathchar 268$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle\mathchar 268$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle\mathchar 268$}}}}_{i}.\mathbf{{{}\mathchoice{\mbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle\mathchar 268$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\textstyle\mathchar 268$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle\mathchar 268$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle\mathchar 268$}}}}_{j}
and
\mathcal{\tilde{K}}_{\mathrm{su}_{\mathrm{r+1}}}^{ij}=\mathbf{{{}\mathchoice{\mbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle\mathchar 287$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\textstyle\mathchar 287$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle\mathchar 287$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle\mathchar 287$}}}}^{i}.\mathbf{{{}\mathchoice{\mbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle\mathchar 287$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\textstyle\mathchar 287$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle\mathchar 287$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle\mathchar 287$}}}}^{j}
read as

\mathcal{K}_{ij}^{\mathrm{su}_{\mathrm{r+1}}}=\sum_{k,l=1}^{r}x_{i}^{k}K_{kl}^{\mathrm{su}_{\mathrm{r+1}}}x_{j}^{l}\qquad,\qquad\mathcal{\tilde{K}}_{\mathrm{su}_{\mathrm{r+1}}}^{ij}=\sum_{k,l=1}^{r}y_{k}^{i}\tilde{K}_{\mathrm{su}_{\mathrm{r+1}}}^{kl}y_{l}^{j}

(126)

with
K_{kl}^{\mathrm{su}_{\mathrm{r+1}}}=\mathbf{{{}\mathchoice{\mbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle\mathchar 267$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\textstyle\mathchar 267$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle\mathchar 267$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle\mathchar 267$}}}}_{i}.\mathbf{{{}\mathchoice{\mbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle\mathchar 267$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\textstyle\mathchar 267$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle\mathchar 267$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle\mathchar 267$}}}}_{j}
and
