\tilde{K}_{\mathrm{su}_{\mathrm{r+1}}}^{kl}=\mathbf{{{}\mathchoice{\mbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle\mathchar 277$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\textstyle\mathchar 277$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle\mathchar 277$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle\mathchar 277$}}}}^{i}.\mathbf{{{}\mathchoice{\mbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle\mathchar 277$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\textstyle\mathchar 277$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle\mathchar 277$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle\mathchar 277$}}}}^{j};
they are respectively quadratic in
x_{i}^{k}
and
y_{k}^{i}.
For simplicity, we restrict the
analysis to the diagonal values with
x_{i}^{k}=x_{i}{{}\mathchoice{\mbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle\mathchar 270$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\textstyle\mathchar 270$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle\mathchar 270$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle\mathchar 270$}}}_{i}^{k}
.

The ingredients in the series (
125
) are as follows:

\left(\mathbf{i}\right)

The integer vector
\boldsymbol{n}^{T}=\left(n^{1},...,n^{r}\right)
describing the KK modes and
the integer vector
\boldsymbol{w}^{T}=\left(w_{1},...,w_{r}\right)

representing the winding numbers.

\left(\mathbf{ii}\right)

The vectors
\mathbf{{{}\mathchoice{\mbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle\mathchar 268$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\textstyle\mathchar 268$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle\mathchar 268$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle\mathchar 268$}}}}_{i}=x_{i}\mathbf{{{}\mathchoice{\mbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle\mathchar 267$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\textstyle\mathchar 267$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle\mathchar 267$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle\mathchar 267$}}}}_{i}
, with fixed label i (e.g:
\mathbf{{{}\mathchoice{\mbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle\mathchar 268$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\textstyle\mathchar 268$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle\mathchar 268$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle\mathchar 268$}}}}_{1}=x_{1}\mathbf{{{}\mathchoice{\mbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle\mathchar 267$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\textstyle\mathchar 267$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle\mathchar 267$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle\mathchar 267$}}}}_{1}
and so on), are given by diagonal scalings of the
simple roots of su(r+1); the intersection matrix
\mathcal{K}_{ij}^{su_{r+1}}=\mathbf{{{}\mathchoice{\mbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle\mathchar 268$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\textstyle\mathchar 268$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle\mathchar 268$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle\mathchar 268$}}}}_{i}.\mathbf{{{}\mathchoice{\mbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle\mathchar 268$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\textstyle\mathchar 268$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle\mathchar 268$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle\mathchar 268$}}}}_{j}
reads explicitly
as follows

\left(\begin{array}[]{ccccccc}2x_{1}^{2}&-x_{1}x_{2}&0&\cdots&0&0&0\\
-x_{1}x_{2}&2x_{2}^{2}&-x_{2}x_{3}&&0&0&0\\
0&-x_{2}x_{3}&2x_{3}^{2}&&0&0&0\\
\vdots&&&\ddots&&&\vdots\\
0&0&0&&2x_{r-2}^{2}&-x_{r-2}x_{r-1}&0\\
0&0&0&&-x_{r-2}x_{r-1}&2x_{r-1}^{2}&-x_{r-1}x_{r}\\
0&0&0&\cdots&0&-x_{r-1}x_{r}&2x_{r}^{2}\end{array}\right)

(127)

with moduli
x_{i}=1/(2R_{i})
.

The vectors
\mathbf{{{}\mathchoice{\mbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle\mathchar 287$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\textstyle\mathchar 287$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle\mathchar 287$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle\mathchar 287$}}}}_{i}=\mathbf{{{}\mathchoice{\mbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle\mathchar 277$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\textstyle\mathchar 277$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle\mathchar 277$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle\mathchar 277$}}}}_{i}/x_{i}
are scaled
fundamental weights of su(r+1) whose expression follows from the usual
duality property
\mathbf{{{}\mathchoice{\mbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle\mathchar 268$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\textstyle\mathchar 268$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle\mathchar 268$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle\mathchar 268$}}}}_{i}\mathbf{.{{}\mathchoice{\mbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle\mathchar 287$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\textstyle\mathchar 287$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle\mathchar 287$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle\mathchar 287$}}}}^{j}=\mathbf{{{}\mathchoice{\mbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle\mathchar 267$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\textstyle\mathchar 267$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle\mathchar 267$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle\mathchar 267$}}}}_{i}\mathbf{.{{}\mathchoice{\mbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle\mathchar 277$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\textstyle\mathchar 277$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle\mathchar 277$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle\mathchar 277$}}}}^{j}={{}\mathchoice{\mbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle\mathchar 270$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\textstyle\mathchar 270$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle\mathchar 270$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle\mathchar 270$}}}_{i}^{j}.
Their intersections
\mathcal{\tilde{K}}_{su_{r+1}}^{ij}=\mathbf{{{}\mathchoice{\mbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle\mathchar 287$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\textstyle\mathchar 287$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle\mathchar 287$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle\mathchar 287$}}}}^{i}.\mathbf{{{}\mathchoice{\mbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle\mathchar 287$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\textstyle\mathchar 287$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle\mathchar 287$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle\mathchar 287$}}}}^{j}
are given
by the inverse of the matrix
\mathcal{K}_{ij}^{su_{r+1}}
.

4.1.1
Sitting at
x_{i}=1
in
\mathcal{M}_{\mathbf{su}_{r+1}}

For computational convenience, we
\left(\mathbf{i}\right)
sit
at
x_{i}=1
in the moduli space of the NCFT
{}_{2}^{su_{r+1}}
,
\left(\mathbf{ii}\right)
use
\mathbf{{{}\mathchoice{\mbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle\mathchar 277$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\textstyle\mathchar 277$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle\mathchar 277$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle\mathchar 277$}}}}^{i}.\mathbf{{{}\mathchoice{\mbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle\mathchar 277$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\textstyle\mathchar 277$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle\mathchar 277$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle\mathchar 277$}}}}^{j}=\mathbf{\tilde{K}}_{su_{r+1}}^{ij}
given by the inverse
\left(\mathbf{K}_{ij}^{su_{r+1}}\right)^{-1}
, and then
\left(\mathbf{iii}\right)
substitute
\mathbf{{{}\mathchoice{\mbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle\mathchar 267$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\textstyle\mathchar 267$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle\mathchar 267$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle\mathchar 267$}}}}_{i}=\mathbf{K}_{ij}\mathbf{{{}\mathchoice{\mbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle\mathchar 277$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\textstyle\mathchar 277$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle\mathchar 277$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle\mathchar 277$}}}}^{j}.
The resulting left and right momenta are expressed as

\boldsymbol{p}_{L}

=

\frac{1}{\sqrt{2}}\mathop{\displaystyle\sum}\limits_{i=1}^{r}\left(n^{i}\mathbf{K}_{ij}+w_{j}\right)\mathbf{{{}\mathchoice{\mbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle\mathchar 277$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\textstyle\mathchar 277$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle\mathchar 277$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle\mathchar 277$}}}}^{j}

\boldsymbol{p}_{R}

=

\frac{1}{\sqrt{2}}\mathop{\displaystyle\sum}\limits_{i=1}^{r}\left(n^{i}\mathbf{K}_{ij}-w_{j}\right)\mathbf{{{}\mathchoice{\mbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle\mathchar 277$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\textstyle\mathchar 277$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle\mathchar 277$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle\mathchar 277$}}}}^{j}

(128)

From these expansions, we calculate

\boldsymbol{p}_{L}^{2}

=

\frac{1}{2}\mathop{\displaystyle\sum}\limits_{i,j=1}^{r}\left(n^{i}\mathbf{K}_{ij}n^{j}+w_{i}\mathbf{\tilde{K}}^{ij}w_{j}\right)+\mathop{\displaystyle\sum}\limits_{i=1}^{r}n^{i}w_{i}

\boldsymbol{p}_{R}^{2}

=

\frac{1}{2}\mathop{\displaystyle\sum}\limits_{i,j=1}^{r}\left(n^{i}\mathbf{K}_{ij}n^{j}+w_{i}\mathbf{\tilde{K}}^{ij}w_{j}\right)-\mathop{\displaystyle\sum}\limits_{i=1}^{r}n^{i}w_{i}

(129)

with the difference

\boldsymbol{p}_{L}^{2}-\boldsymbol{p}_{R}^{2}=2\mathop{\displaystyle\sum}\limits_{i=1}^{r}n^{i}w_{i}\in 2\mathbb{Z}

(130)

Setting
\mathfrak{Q}_{L}^{su_{r+1}}=\boldsymbol{p}_{L}^{2}
and
\mathfrak{Q}_{R}^{su_{r+1}}=\boldsymbol{p}_{R}^{2};
these relations can be
expressed compactly as follows

\displaystyle\mathfrak{Q}_{L}^{su_{r+1}}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\frac{1}{2}\boldsymbol{n}^{T}.\mathbf{K}.\boldsymbol{n}+\frac{1}{2}\boldsymbol{w}^{T}.\mathbf{\tilde{K}}.\boldsymbol{w}+\frac{1}{2}\left(\boldsymbol{n}^{T}.\boldsymbol{w}+\boldsymbol{w}^{T}.\boldsymbol{n}\right)

(131)

\displaystyle\mathfrak{Q}_{R}^{su_{r+1}}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\frac{1}{2}\boldsymbol{n}^{T}.\mathbf{K}.\boldsymbol{n}+\frac{1}{2}\boldsymbol{w}^{T}.\mathbf{\tilde{K}}.\boldsymbol{w}-\frac{1}{2}\left(\boldsymbol{n}^{T}.\boldsymbol{w}+\boldsymbol{w}^{T}.\boldsymbol{n}\right)

(132)

with matrices
\boldsymbol{Q}_{L}^{su_{r+1}}
and
\boldsymbol{Q}_{R}^{su_{r+1}}
given by

\boldsymbol{Q}_{L}^{su_{r+1}}=\frac{1}{2}\left(\begin{array}[]{cc}\mathbf{K}_{ij}&\mathbf{{{}\mathchoice{\mbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle\mathchar 267$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\textstyle\mathchar 267$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle\mathchar 267$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle\mathchar 267$}}}}_{i}\mathbf{.{{}\mathchoice{\mbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle\mathchar 277$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\textstyle\mathchar 277$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle\mathchar 277$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle\mathchar 277$}}}}^{l}\\
\mathbf{{{}\mathchoice{\mbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle\mathchar 277$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\textstyle\mathchar 277$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle\mathchar 277$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle\mathchar 277$}}}}^{k}\mathbf{.{{}\mathchoice{\mbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle\mathchar 267$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\textstyle\mathchar 267$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle\mathchar 267$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle\mathchar 267$}}}}_{j}&\mathbf{\tilde{K}}^{kl}\end{array}\right)\qquad,\qquad\boldsymbol{Q}_{R}^{su_{r+1}}=\frac{1}{2}\left(\begin{array}[]{cc}\mathbf{K}_{ij}&-\mathbf{{{}\mathchoice{\mbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle\mathchar 267$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\textstyle\mathchar 267$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle\mathchar 267$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle\mathchar 267$}}}}_{i}\mathbf{.{{}\mathchoice{\mbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle\mathchar 277$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\textstyle\mathchar 277$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle\mathchar 277$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle\mathchar 277$}}}}^{l}\\
-\mathbf{{{}\mathchoice{\mbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle\mathchar 277$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\textstyle\mathchar 277$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle\mathchar 277$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle\mathchar 277$}}}}^{k}\mathbf{.{{}\mathchoice{\mbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle\mathchar 267$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\textstyle\mathchar 267$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle\mathchar 267$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle\mathchar 267$}}}}_{j}&\mathbf{\tilde{K}}^{kl}\end{array}\right)

(133)

Putting them into the following definitions

\boldsymbol{H}_{su_{r+1}}

=

\boldsymbol{Q}_{L}^{su_{r+1}}+\boldsymbol{Q}_{R}^{su_{r+1}}

\boldsymbol{Q}_{su_{r+1}}

=

\boldsymbol{Q}_{L}^{su_{r+1}}-\boldsymbol{Q}_{R}^{su_{r+1}}

(134)

we get

\boldsymbol{H}_{su_{r+1}}=\left(\begin{array}[]{cc}\mathbf{K}_{ij}&\mathbf{0}\\
\mathbf{0}&\mathbf{\tilde{K}}^{kl}\end{array}\right)\qquad,\qquad\boldsymbol{Q}_{su_{r+1}}=\left(\begin{array}[]{cc}0&\mathbf{{{}\mathchoice{\mbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle\mathchar 267$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\textstyle\mathchar 267$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle\mathchar 267$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle\mathchar 267$}}}}_{i}\mathbf{.{{}\mathchoice{\mbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle\mathchar 277$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\textstyle\mathchar 277$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle\mathchar 277$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle\mathchar 277$}}}}^{l}\\
\mathbf{{{}\mathchoice{\mbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle\mathchar 277$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\textstyle\mathchar 277$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle\mathchar 277$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle\mathchar 277$}}}}^{k}\mathbf{.{{}\mathchoice{\mbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle\mathchar 267$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\textstyle\mathchar 267$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle\mathchar 267$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle\mathchar 267$}}}}_{j}&0\end{array}\right)

(135)

Using
\mathbf{{{}\mathchoice{\mbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle\mathchar 267$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\textstyle\mathchar 267$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle\mathchar 267$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle\mathchar 267$}}}}_{i}\mathbf{.{{}\mathchoice{\mbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle\mathchar 277$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\textstyle\mathchar 277$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle\mathchar 277$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle\mathchar 277$}}}}^{l}={{}\mathchoice{\mbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle\mathchar 270$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\textstyle\mathchar 270$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle\mathchar 270$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle\mathchar 270$}}}_{i}^{l}\mathbf{,}

we have
1
1

1

Given a
2r\times 2r
matrix M with bloc form
\left(A,B,C,D\right)

such that AC=CA, we have

\det M=\det\left(\begin{array}[]{cc}A&B\\
C&D\end{array}\right)=\det\left(AD-CB\right)

\det\boldsymbol{Q}_{su_{r+1}}=\det\left(-I_{r}\right)=\left(-\right)^{r}

(136)

Physically, the quadratic form
\mathcal{H}_{\mathbf{su}_{r+1}}=\boldsymbol{m}^{T}.\boldsymbol{H}_{su_{r+1}}.\boldsymbol{m},
equal to
\boldsymbol{p}_{L}^{2}+\boldsymbol{p}_{R}^{2},
represents the
total squared mass of the KK and winding modes

\mathcal{H}_{su_{r+1}}=\mathop{\displaystyle\sum}\limits_{i,j=1}^{r}n^{i}\mathbf{K}_{ij}n^{j}+\mathop{\displaystyle\sum}\limits_{i,j=1}^{r}w_{i}\mathbf{\tilde{K}}^{ij}w_{j}

(137)

As for the quadratic form
\mathfrak{Q}_{\mathbf{su}_{r+1}}=\boldsymbol{m}^{T}.\boldsymbol{Q}_{su_{r+1}}.\boldsymbol{m},
defined by
\boldsymbol{p}_{L}^{2}-\boldsymbol{p}_{R}^{2}
, it gives the gap
mass between left and right moving modes namely

\mathfrak{Q}_{su_{r+1}}=2\mathop{\displaystyle\sum}\limits_{i=1}^{r}n^{i}w_{i}

(138)

Considering
\boldsymbol{p}_{1}=\left(\boldsymbol{p}_{L}+\boldsymbol{p}_{R}\right)/\sqrt{2}
and
\boldsymbol{p}_{2}=\left(\boldsymbol{p}_{L}-\boldsymbol{p}_{R}\right)/\sqrt{2},
we obtain

\boldsymbol{p}_{1}=\mathop{\displaystyle\sum}\limits_{i=1}^{r}n^{i}\mathbf{{{}\mathchoice{\mbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle\mathchar 267$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\textstyle\mathchar 267$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle\mathchar 267$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle\mathchar 267$}}}}_{i}\qquad,\qquad\boldsymbol{p}_{2}=\mathop{\displaystyle\sum}\limits_{i=1}^{r}w_{i}\mathbf{{{}\mathchoice{\mbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle\mathchar 277$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\textstyle\mathchar 277$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle\mathchar 277$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle\mathchar 277$}}}}^{i}

(139)

indicating that
\boldsymbol{p}_{1}
labels the root lattice of su(r+1)
while
\boldsymbol{p}_{2}
parameterises its weight lattice. Furthermore, we have

\mathcal{H}_{su_{r+1}}=\boldsymbol{p}_{1}^{2}+\boldsymbol{p}_{2}^{2}=\boldsymbol{n}^{T}.\mathbf{K}.\boldsymbol{n}+\boldsymbol{w}^{T}.\mathbf{\tilde{K}}.\boldsymbol{w}

(140)

and

\mathfrak{Q}_{su_{r+1}}=2\boldsymbol{p}_{1}.\boldsymbol{p}_{2}=2\mathop{\displaystyle\sum}\limits_{i=1}^{r}n^{i}w_{i}

(141)

This intersection can be factorised like
\mathfrak{Q}_{\mathbf{su}_{r+1}}=\mathfrak{p}^{\text{{\@text@baccent{m}}}}{{}\mathchoice{\mbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle\mathchar 273$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\textstyle\mathchar 273$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle\mathchar 273$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle\mathchar 273$}}}_{\text{{\@text@baccent{m}\@text@baccent{n}}}}\mathfrak{p}^{\text{{\@text@baccent{n}}}}
with diagonal pseudo-metric
{{}\mathchoice{\mbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle\mathchar 273$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\textstyle\mathchar 273$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle\mathchar 273$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle\mathchar 273$}}}_{\text{{\@text@baccent{m}\@text@baccent{n}}}}
given by
diag({\small[+]}^{r},{\small[-]}^{r})
and

\mathfrak{p}^{\text{{\@text@baccent{m}}}}=\left(\begin{array}[]{c}\frac{1}{\sqrt{2}}\left(K_{ij}n^{j}+{{}\mathchoice{\mbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle\mathchar 270$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\textstyle\mathchar 270$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle\mathchar 270$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle\mathchar 270$}}}_{i}^{l}w_{l}\right)\\
\frac{1}{\sqrt{2}}\left(K_{kj}n^{j}-{{}\mathchoice{\mbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle\mathchar 270$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\textstyle\mathchar 270$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle\mathchar 270$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle\mathchar 270$}}}_{k}^{l}w_{l}\right)\end{array}\right)

(142)

For the su(r+1) symmetry, the condition
\mathfrak{p}^{\text{{\@text@baccent{m}}}}{{}\mathchoice{\mbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle\mathchar 273$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\textstyle\mathchar 273$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle\mathchar 273$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle\mathchar 273$}}}_{\text{{\@text@baccent{m}\@text@baccent{n}}}}\mathfrak{p}^{\text{{\@text@baccent{n}}}}\in 2\mathbb{Z}
is satisfied by the lattice

{\large\Lambda}_{su_{r+1}}=\left\{\boldsymbol{m}\in\mathbb{Z}^{4},\quad\mathfrak{Q}_{su_{r+1}}=\boldsymbol{m}^{\text{{a}}}{\large g}_{\text{{ab}}}\boldsymbol{m}^{\text{{b}}}\quad|\quad{\large g}_{\text{{ab}}}=(\boldsymbol{Q}_{su_{r+1}})_{\text{{ab}}}\right\}

(143)

where
\boldsymbol{m}=\left(\mathbf{n},\mathbf{w}\right)\in\mathbb{Z}^{r}\times\mathbb{Z}^{r}
and the uni-modularity property
\left|\det\boldsymbol{Q}_{su_{r+1}}\right|=1
holds
.
For this
lattice, we indeed have the property
\mathfrak{Q}_{su_{r+1}}=2\mathbf{n}.\mathbf{w}
which is an even integer.

4.1.2
Partition function
Z_{\mathbf{su}_{r+1}}

The genus-one partition function
Z_{\mathbf{su}_{r+1}}\left[{{}\mathchoice{\mbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle\mathchar 284$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\textstyle\mathchar 284$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle\mathchar 284$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle\mathchar 284$}}},\bar{{{}\mathchoice{\mbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle\mathchar 284$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\textstyle\mathchar 284$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle\mathchar 284$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle\mathchar 284$}}}};\mathbf{x}\right]
of the compactified conformal theory, with left
\boldsymbol{p}_{L}
and right
\boldsymbol{p}_{R}
momenta (
125
), is as
follows

Z_{\mathrm{su}_{r+1}}\left[{{}\mathchoice{\mbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle\mathchar 284$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\textstyle\mathchar 284$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle\mathchar 284$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle\mathchar 284$}}},\bar{{{}\mathchoice{\mbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle\mathchar 284$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\textstyle\mathchar 284$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle\mathchar 284$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle\mathchar 284$}}}};\mathbf{x}\right]=\frac{1}{\left|{{}\mathchoice{\mbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle\mathchar 273$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\textstyle\mathchar 273$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle\mathchar 273$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle\mathchar 273$}}}\left({{}\mathchoice{\mbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle\mathchar 284$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\textstyle\mathchar 284$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle\mathchar 284$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle\mathchar 284$}}}\right)\right|^{2r}}{{}\mathchoice{\mbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle\mathchar 291$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\textstyle\mathchar 291$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle\mathchar 291$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle\mathchar 291$}}}_{su_{r+1}}\left({{}\mathchoice{\mbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle\mathchar 284$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\textstyle\mathchar 284$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle\mathchar 284$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle\mathchar 284$}}},\bar{{{}\mathchoice{\mbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle\mathchar 284$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\textstyle\mathchar 284$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle\mathchar 284$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle\mathchar 284$}}}};\mathbf{x}\right)

(144)

where
{{}\mathchoice{\mbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle\mathchar 273$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\textstyle\mathchar 273$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle\mathchar 273$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle\mathchar 273$}}}\left({{}\mathchoice{\mbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle\mathchar 284$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\textstyle\mathchar 284$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle\mathchar 284$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle\mathchar 284$}}}\right)
is the Dedekin eta function given by
q^{1/24}\mathop{\displaystyle\prod}\nolimits_{n=1}^{\infty}\left(1-q^{n}\right)
and where

\displaystyle{{}\mathchoice{\mbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle\mathchar 291$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\textstyle\mathchar 291$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle\mathchar 291$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle\mathchar 291$}}}_{\mathrm{su}_{r+1}}\left({{}\mathchoice{\mbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle\mathchar 284$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\textstyle\mathchar 284$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle\mathchar 284$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle\mathchar 284$}}},\bar{{{}\mathchoice{\mbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle\mathchar 284$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\textstyle\mathchar 284$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle\mathchar 284$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle\mathchar 284$}}}};\mathbf{x}\right)

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\mathop{\displaystyle\sum}\limits_{\left(p_{L},p_{R}\right)}q^{\frac{1}{2}p_{L}^{2}}\bar{q}^{\frac{1}{2}p_{R}^{2}}

(145)

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\frac{1}{\left|{{}\mathchoice{\mbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle\mathchar 273$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\textstyle\mathchar 273$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle\mathchar 273$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle\mathchar 273$}}}\left({{}\mathchoice{\mbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle\mathchar 284$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\textstyle\mathchar 284$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle\mathchar 284$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle\mathchar 284$}}}\right)\right|^{2r}}\mathop{\displaystyle\sum}\limits_{(\mathfrak{Q}_{L}^{\mathtt{su}_{r+1}},\mathfrak{Q}_{R}^{\mathtt{su}_{r+1}})}q^{\frac{1}{2}\mathfrak{Q}_{L}^{\mathtt{su}_{r+1}}}\bar{q}^{\frac{1}{2}\mathfrak{Q}_{R}^{\mathtt{su}_{r+1}}}

with
q=e^{2{{}\mathchoice{\mbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle\mathchar 281$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\textstyle\mathchar 281$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle\mathchar 281$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle\mathchar 281$}}}i{{}\mathchoice{\mbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle\mathchar 284$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\textstyle\mathchar 284$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle\mathchar 284$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle\mathchar 284$}}}}
. Substituting
{{}\mathchoice{\mbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle\mathchar 284$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\textstyle\mathchar 284$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle\mathchar 284$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle\mathchar 284$}}}={{}\mathchoice{\mbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle\mathchar 284$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\textstyle\mathchar 284$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle\mathchar 284$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle\mathchar 284$}}}_{1}+i{{}\mathchoice{\mbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle\mathchar 284$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\textstyle\mathchar 284$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle\mathchar 284$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle\mathchar 284$}}}_{2}
(
\mathop{\mathrm{I}m}\nolimits{{}\mathchoice{\mbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle\mathchar 284$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\textstyle\mathchar 284$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle\mathchar 284$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle\mathchar 284$}}}_{2}>0
) and
\mathfrak{Q}_{\mathrm{su}_{r+1}}=p_{L}^{2}-p_{R}^{2}

as well as
\mathcal{H}_{\mathrm{su}_{r+1}}=p_{L}^{2}+p_{R}^{2},
we end up
with

Z_{\mathbf{su}_{r+1}}\left[{{}\mathchoice{\mbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle\mathchar 284$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\textstyle\mathchar 284$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle\mathchar 284$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle\mathchar 284$}}},\bar{{{}\mathchoice{\mbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle\mathchar 284$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\textstyle\mathchar 284$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle\mathchar 284$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle\mathchar 284$}}}};\mathbf{x}\right]=\frac{1}{\left|{{}\mathchoice{\mbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle\mathchar 273$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\textstyle\mathchar 273$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle\mathchar 273$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle\mathchar 273$}}}\left({{}\mathchoice{\mbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle\mathchar 284$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\textstyle\mathchar 284$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle\mathchar 284$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle\mathchar 284$}}}\right)\right|^{2r}}\mathop{\displaystyle\sum}\limits_{\boldsymbol{m}\in\mathbb{Z}^{2r}}\exp\left(\boldsymbol{m}^{T}.\boldsymbol{A}_{su_{r+1}}.\boldsymbol{m}\right)

(146)

where the
2r\times 2r
matrix
\boldsymbol{A}_{\mathbf{su}_{r+1}}
is given
by
i{{}\mathchoice{\mbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle\mathchar 281$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\textstyle\mathchar 281$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle\mathchar 281$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle\mathchar 281$}}}\left({{}\mathchoice{\mbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle\mathchar 284$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\textstyle\mathchar 284$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle\mathchar 284$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle\mathchar 284$}}}\boldsymbol{Q}_{L}^{\mathrm{su}_{r+1}}-\bar{{{}\mathchoice{\mbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle\mathchar 284$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\textstyle\mathchar 284$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle\mathchar 284$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle\mathchar 284$}}}}\boldsymbol{Q}_{R}^{\mathrm{su}_{r+1}}\right)
. It also splits in terms of
\boldsymbol{Q}_{su_{r+1}}
and
\boldsymbol{H}_{\mathbf{su}_{r+1}}
as
follows

\boldsymbol{A}_{\mathbf{su}_{r+1}}=i{{}\mathchoice{\mbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle\mathchar 281$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\textstyle\mathchar 281$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle\mathchar 281$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle\mathchar 281$}}}{{}\mathchoice{\mbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle\mathchar 284$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\textstyle\mathchar 284$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle\mathchar 284$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle\mathchar 284$}}}_{1}\boldsymbol{Q}_{su_{r+1}}-{{}\mathchoice{\mbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle\mathchar 281$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\textstyle\mathchar 281$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle\mathchar 281$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle\mathchar 281$}}}{{}\mathchoice{\mbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle\mathchar 284$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\textstyle\mathchar 284$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle\mathchar 284$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle\mathchar 284$}}}_{2}\boldsymbol{H}_{su_{r+1}}

(147)

reading explicitly like

\left.\boldsymbol{A}_{\mathrm{su}_{3}}\right|_{x_{i}=1}=\left(\begin{array}[]{cc}-{{}\mathchoice{\mbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle\mathchar 281$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\textstyle\mathchar 281$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle\mathchar 281$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle\mathchar 281$}}}\mathrm{{{}\mathchoice{\mbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle\mathchar 284$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\textstyle\mathchar 284$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle\mathchar 284$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle\mathchar 284$}}}}_{2}\mathcal{K}_{\mathtt{su}_{r+1}}&i{{}\mathchoice{\mbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle\mathchar 281$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\textstyle\mathchar 281$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle\mathchar 281$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle\mathchar 281$}}}\mathrm{{{}\mathchoice{\mbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle\mathchar 284$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\textstyle\mathchar 284$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle\mathchar 284$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle\mathchar 284$}}}}_{1}I_{{\small r}}\\
i{{}\mathchoice{\mbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle\mathchar 281$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\textstyle\mathchar 281$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle\mathchar 281$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle\mathchar 281$}}}\mathrm{{{}\mathchoice{\mbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle\mathchar 284$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\textstyle\mathchar 284$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle\mathchar 284$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle\mathchar 284$}}}}_{1}I_{{\small r}}&-{{}\mathchoice{\mbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle\mathchar 281$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\textstyle\mathchar 281$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle\mathchar 281$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle\mathchar 281$}}}\mathrm{{{}\mathchoice{\mbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle\mathchar 284$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\textstyle\mathchar 284$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle\mathchar 284$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle\mathchar 284$}}}}_{2}\mathcal{\tilde{K}}_{\mathtt{su}_{r+1}}\end{array}\right)

(148)

with
\det\boldsymbol{A}_{\mathrm{su}_{3}}
equal to
{{}\mathchoice{\mbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle\mathchar 281$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\textstyle\mathchar 281$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle\mathchar 281$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle\mathchar 281$}}}^{2r}\left({{}\mathchoice{\mbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle\mathchar 284$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\textstyle\mathchar 284$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle\mathchar 284$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle\mathchar 284$}}}\bar{{{}\mathchoice{\mbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle\mathchar 284$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\textstyle\mathchar 284$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle\mathchar 284$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle\mathchar 284$}}}}\right)^{r}
which is independent of the moduli
x_{i}.
From
this formulation, we deduce that the key properties of
Narain CFT
{}^{\mathrm{su}_{r+1}}
s are encapsulated in the two
square matrices
\boldsymbol{Q}_{su_{r+1}}
and
\boldsymbol{H}_{su_{r+1}}
.
Therefore, we proceed to determine the values of these two basic
ingredients in the following subsection.

4.2
Classification of Narain CFT
{}_{2}^{\text{{\@text@baccent{g}}}}

From the previous description of the family NCFT
{}_{2}^{\mathrm{su}_{r+1}}
, we learnt that a significant subset of Narain CFTs can be
classified by finite dimensional Lie algebras as NCFT
{}_{2}^{\text{{\@text@baccent{g}}}}
. In this classification, the NCFT
{}_{2}^{\mathrm{su}_{r+1}}
addressed
earlier constitutes the first class of Narain conformal field
theories. The remaining CFTs, based on the Lie algebras

\text{{g}}_{r}=B_{r},\quad C_{r},\quad D_{r},\quad F_{4},\quad G_{2},\quad E_{6,7,8}

(149)

are described in a manner analogous to the class
A_{r}=\mathrm{su}_{r+1}
. For all these NCFT
{}_{2}^{\text{{\@text@baccent{g}}}}
’s, the left
\boldsymbol{p}_{L}
and right
\boldsymbol{p}_{R}
momenta expand in
an equivalent way to (
125
) and (
128
). At
x_{i}=1
in the Narain
moduli space, we have

\boldsymbol{p}_{L}

=

\frac{1}{\sqrt{2}}\mathop{\displaystyle\sum}\limits_{i=1}^{r}\left(n^{i}\mathbf{K}_{ij}^{(g)}+w_{j}\right)\mathbf{{{}\mathchoice{\mbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle\mathchar 277$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\textstyle\mathchar 277$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle\mathchar 277$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle\mathchar 277$}}}}^{j}

\boldsymbol{p}_{R}

=

\frac{1}{\sqrt{2}}\mathop{\displaystyle\sum}\limits_{i=1}^{r}\left(n^{i}\mathbf{K}_{ij}^{(g)}-w_{j}\right)\mathbf{{{}\mathchoice{\mbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle\mathchar 277$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\textstyle\mathchar 277$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle\mathchar 277$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle\mathchar 277$}}}}^{j}

(150)

where
n^{i},

w_{j}
are integers. Moreover, the
\mathbf{K}_{ij}^{(g)}

is the intersection matrix
\mathbf{{{}\mathchoice{\mbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle\mathchar 267$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\textstyle\mathchar 267$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle\mathchar 267$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle\mathchar 267$}}}}_{i}.\mathbf{{{}\mathchoice{\mbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle\mathchar 267$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\textstyle\mathchar 267$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle\mathchar 267$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle\mathchar 267$}}}}_{j}
of
the cycles
\mathbf{{{}\mathchoice{\mbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle\mathchar 267$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\textstyle\mathchar 267$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle\mathchar 267$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle\mathchar 267$}}}}_{i}
and
\mathbf{{{}\mathchoice{\mbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle\mathchar 277$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\textstyle\mathchar 277$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle\mathchar 277$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle\mathchar 277$}}}}^{j}
’s are their
duals; i.e:
\mathbf{{{}\mathchoice{\mbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle\mathchar 267$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\textstyle\mathchar 267$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle\mathchar 267$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle\mathchar 267$}}}}_{i}.\mathbf{{{}\mathchoice{\mbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle\mathchar 277$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\textstyle\mathchar 277$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle\mathchar 277$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle\mathchar 277$}}}}^{j}={{}\mathchoice{\mbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle\mathchar 270$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\textstyle\mathchar 270$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle\mathchar 270$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle\mathchar 270$}}}_{i}^{j}.

It is also possible to use the following scaled quantities:
\left(\mathbf{i}\right)
the scaled
\mathbf{{{}\mathchoice{\mbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle\mathchar 268$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\textstyle\mathchar 268$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle\mathchar 268$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle\mathchar 268$}}}}_{i}=\sum_{k}x_{i}^{k}\mathbf{{{}\mathchoice{\mbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle\mathchar 267$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\textstyle\mathchar 267$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle\mathchar 267$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle\mathchar 267$}}}}_{k}
where
\mathbf{{{}\mathchoice{\mbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle\mathchar 267$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\textstyle\mathchar 267$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle\mathchar 267$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle\mathchar 267$}}}}_{k}
are the simple
roots of the Lie algebras (
149
), and
\left(\mathbf{ii}\right)
the
scaled
\mathbf{{{}\mathchoice{\mbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle\mathchar 287$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\textstyle\mathchar 287$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle\mathchar 287$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle\mathchar 287$}}}}^{i}=\sum_{l}y_{l}^{i}\mathbf{{{}\mathchoice{\mbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle\mathchar 277$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\textstyle\mathchar 277$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle\mathchar 277$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle\mathchar 277$}}}}^{l}
with the
constraint
\sum_{k}x_{i}^{k}y_{k}^{j}={{}\mathchoice{\mbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle\mathchar 270$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\textstyle\mathchar 270$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle\mathchar 270$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle\mathchar 270$}}}_{i}^{j}
(duality relation).
For the diagonal realisation, we have
\mathbf{{{}\mathchoice{\mbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle\mathchar 268$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\textstyle\mathchar 268$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle\mathchar 268$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle\mathchar 268$}}}}_{i}=x_{i}\mathbf{{{}\mathchoice{\mbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle\mathchar 267$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\textstyle\mathchar 267$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle\mathchar 267$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle\mathchar 267$}}}}_{i}
and
\mathbf{{{}\mathchoice{\mbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle\mathchar 287$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\textstyle\mathchar 287$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle\mathchar 287$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle\mathchar 287$}}}}^{i}=\frac{1}{x_{i}}\mathbf{{{}\mathchoice{\mbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle\mathchar 277$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\textstyle\mathchar 277$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle\mathchar 277$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle\mathchar 277$}}}}^{i}.

In this generic situation, the Cartan matrix
\mathbf{K}_{ij}^{(g)}
in (
150
) should be replaced by the scaled
\mathcal{K}_{ij}^{(g)}=\mathbf{{{}\mathchoice{\mbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle\mathchar 268$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\textstyle\mathchar 268$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle\mathchar 268$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle\mathchar 268$}}}}_{i}.\mathbf{{{}\mathchoice{\mbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle\mathchar 268$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\textstyle\mathchar 268$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle\mathchar 268$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle\mathchar 268$}}}}_{j}
and the
\mathbf{\tilde{K}}_{(g)}^{ij}
by
the scaled
\mathcal{\tilde{K}}_{(g)}^{ij}=\mathbf{{{}\mathchoice{\mbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle\mathchar 287$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\textstyle\mathchar 287$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle\mathchar 287$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle\mathchar 287$}}}}^{i}.\mathbf{{{}\mathchoice{\mbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle\mathchar 287$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\textstyle\mathchar 287$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle\mathchar 287$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle\mathchar 287$}}}}^{j}
. To clarify the procedure, we provide an illustration for the
rank 2 algebras. We have

\begin{tabular}[]{lll}$\mathbf{{{}\mathchoice{\mbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle\mathchar 268$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\textstyle\mathchar 268$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle\mathchar 268$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle\mathchar 268$}}}}_{1}$&$=$&$x_{1}\mathbf{{{}\mathchoice{\mbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle\mathchar 267$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\textstyle\mathchar 267$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle\mathchar 267$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle\mathchar 267$}}}}_{1}$\\
$\mathbf{{{}\mathchoice{\mbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle\mathchar 268$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\textstyle\mathchar 268$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle\mathchar 268$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle\mathchar 268$}}}}_{2}$&$=$&$x_{2}\mathbf{{{}\mathchoice{\mbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle\mathchar 267$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\textstyle\mathchar 267$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle\mathchar 267$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle\mathchar 267$}}}}_{2}$\end{tabular}\qquad,\qquad\begin{tabular}[]{lll}$\mathbf{{{}\mathchoice{\mbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle\mathchar 287$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\textstyle\mathchar 287$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle\mathchar 287$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle\mathchar 287$}}}}^{1}$&$=$&$\frac{1}{x_{1}}\mathbf{{{}\mathchoice{\mbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle\mathchar 277$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\textstyle\mathchar 277$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle\mathchar 277$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle\mathchar 277$}}}}^{1}$\\
$\mathbf{{{}\mathchoice{\mbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle\mathchar 287$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\textstyle\mathchar 287$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle\mathchar 287$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle\mathchar 287$}}}}^{2}$&$=$&$\frac{1}{x_{2}}\mathbf{{{}\mathchoice{\mbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle\mathchar 277$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\textstyle\mathchar 277$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle\mathchar 277$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle\mathchar 277$}}}}^{2}$\end{tabular}

(151)

where
x_{i}=1/\left(2R_{i}\right).
Here,
\mathbf{{{}\mathchoice{\mbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle\mathchar 267$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\textstyle\mathchar 267$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle\mathchar 267$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle\mathchar 267$}}}}_{1},\mathbf{{{}\mathchoice{\mbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle\mathchar 267$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\textstyle\mathchar 267$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle\mathchar 267$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle\mathchar 267$}}}}_{2}
are the simple roots and
\mathbf{{{}\mathchoice{\mbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle\mathchar 277$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\textstyle\mathchar 277$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle\mathchar 277$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle\mathchar 277$}}}}^{1},\mathbf{{{}\mathchoice{\mbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle\mathchar 277$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\textstyle\mathchar 277$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle\mathchar 277$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle\mathchar 277$}}}}^{2}
are the fundamental weights. For the su(3) example, the
intersection matrices
\mathcal{K}^{\mathrm{su}_{3}}
and
\mathcal{\tilde{K}}^{\mathrm{su}_{3}}
are given by eq(
104
); they can be represented by
the x
i
- dependent diagram in Figure
1
.

Figure 1:
Graphic representations of the intersection matrices
\mathbf{K}^{\mathrm{su}_{3}}
and
\mathbf{\tilde{K}}^{\mathrm{su}_{3}}
. The two nodes
of
\mathbf{K}^{\mathrm{su}_{3}}
and
\mathbf{\tilde{K}}^{\mathrm{su}_{3}}

represent the two circles used in the compactification. The
x_{i}=1/(2R_{i})
gives the real moduli of the 2-torus and its dual. For
x_{i}=1,
the
\mathbf{K}^{\mathrm{su}_{3}}
leads to the Dynkin
diagram of su(3).

Similar relations can be also written down for the other rank 2 Lie algebras

so(5)\simeq sp(4)
and G
2
.

Next, we give results for the Narain CFT
{}^{\mathbf{\text{\@text@baccent{g}}}}
’s classified by the simply laced ADE Lie algebras:

4.2.1
Linear su
\left(r+1\right)
class: NCFT
{}_{2}^{\mathbf{su}_{r+1}}

The class NCFT
{}^{\mathbf{su}_{r+1}}
is characterised by the intersection
matrix
\mathcal{K}_{\mathbf{su}_{r+1}}
(
127
) and its inverse
\mathcal{\tilde{K}}_{\mathbf{su}_{r+1}}
as depicted by the
following graphical representation in
Figure
2
.

Figure 2:
Graphic representation of the intersection matrix
\mathbf{K}_{su_{r+1}}
. For the cases of su(4) and su(5), they are explicitly given by
eqs(
152
-
156
).

For illustration, we consider examples of rank 3 Narain CFT
{}_{2}^{su_{4}}
and the rank 4 CFT
{}_{2}^{su_{5}}:

•

Rank 3 Narain CFT
{}_{2}^{su_{4}}
:

For this rank 3 unitary CFT, the intersection matrix
\mathcal{K}_{su_{4}}
,
between the cycles
\mathbf{{{}\mathchoice{\mbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle\mathchar 268$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\textstyle\mathchar 268$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle\mathchar 268$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle\mathchar 268$}}}}_{i}
as in (
151
), and the
\mathcal{\tilde{K}}_{su_{4}}^{ij}
, between its dual cycles
\mathbf{{{}\mathchoice{\mbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle\mathchar 287$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\textstyle\mathchar 287$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle\mathchar 287$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle\mathchar 287$}}}}^{i}
, are
given by:

\mathcal{K}_{su_{4}}=\left(\begin{array}[]{ccc}2x_{1}^{2}&-x_{1}x_{2}&0\\
-x_{1}x_{2}&2x_{2}^{2}&-x_{2}x_{3}\\
0&-x_{2}x_{3}&2x_{3}^{2}\end{array}\right)

(152)

and

\mathcal{\tilde{K}}_{su_{4}}=\left(\begin{array}[]{ccc}\frac{3}{4x_{1}^{2}}&\frac{1}{2x_{1}x_{2}}&\frac{1}{4x_{1}x_{3}}\\
\frac{1}{2x_{1}x_{2}}&\frac{1}{x_{2}^{2}}&\frac{1}{2x_{2}x_{3}}\\
\frac{1}{4x_{1}x_{3}}&\frac{1}{2x_{2}x_{3}}&\frac{3}{4x_{3}^{2}}\end{array}\right)

(153)

We also have
\det\mathcal{K}_{su_{4}}=4x_{1}^{2}x_{2}^{2}x_{3}^{2}.

Here, the condition
\mathfrak{p}^{\text{{\@text@baccent{m}}}}{{}\mathchoice{\mbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle\mathchar 273$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\textstyle\mathchar 273$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle\mathchar 273$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle\mathchar 273$}}}_{\text{{\@text@baccent{m}\@text@baccent{n}}}}\mathfrak{p}^{\text{{\@text@baccent{n}}}}\in 2\mathbb{Z}

is solved by the lattice

{\large\Lambda}_{\mathtt{su}_{4}}=\left\{\boldsymbol{m}\in\mathbb{Z}^{6},\quad\mathfrak{Q}_{\mathtt{su}_{4}}=\boldsymbol{m}^{\text{{a}}}{\large g}_{\text{{ab}}}\boldsymbol{m}^{\text{{b}}}\quad|\quad{\large g}_{\text{{ab}}}=\boldsymbol{Q}_{\text{{ab}}}^{\mathtt{su}_{4}}\right\}

(154)

with

\boldsymbol{Q}_{\text{{ab}}}^{\mathtt{su}_{4}}=\left(\begin{array}[]{cc}0&I_{3}\\
I_{3}&0\end{array}\right)\qquad,\qquad\det\boldsymbol{Q}_{\text{{ab}}}^{\mathtt{su}_{4}}=-1

(155)

•

Rank 4 Narain CFT
{}_{2}^{su_{5}}
:

For this rank 4 Narain conformal theory, we have

\mathcal{K}_{su_{5}}=\left(\begin{array}[]{cccc}2x_{1}^{2}&-x_{1}x_{2}&0&0\\
-x_{1}x_{2}&2x_{2}^{2}&-x_{2}x_{3}&0\\
0&-x_{2}x_{3}&2x_{3}^{2}&-x_{3}x_{4}\\
0&0&-x_{3}x_{4}&2x_{4}^{2}\end{array}\right)

(156)

with
\det\mathcal{K}_{su_{5}}=5x_{1}^{2}x_{2}^{2}x_{3}^{2}x_{4}^{2}
.

The associated
\mathcal{\tilde{K}}_{su_{5}}
is given by

\mathcal{\tilde{K}}_{su_{5}}=\left(\begin{array}[]{cccc}\frac{4}{5x_{1}^{2}}&\frac{3}{5x_{1}x_{2}}&\frac{2}{5x_{1}x_{3}}&\frac{1}{5x_{1}x_{4}}\\
\frac{3}{5x_{1}x_{2}}&\frac{6}{5x_{2}^{2}}&\frac{4}{5x_{2}x_{3}}&\frac{2}{5x_{2}x_{4}}\\
\frac{2}{5x_{1}x_{3}}&\frac{4}{5x_{2}x_{3}}&\frac{6}{5x_{3}^{2}}&\frac{3}{5x_{3}x_{4}}\\
\frac{1}{5x_{1}x_{4}}&\frac{2}{5x_{2}x_{4}}&\frac{3}{5x_{3}x_{4}}&\frac{4}{5x_{4}^{2}}\end{array}\right)

(157)

The condition
\mathfrak{p}^{\text{{\@text@baccent{m}}}}{{}\mathchoice{\mbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle\mathchar 273$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\textstyle\mathchar 273$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle\mathchar 273$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle\mathchar 273$}}}_{\text{{\@text@baccent{m}\@text@baccent{n}}}}\mathfrak{p}^{\text{{\@text@baccent{n}}}}\in 2\mathbb{Z}
is solved by
the lattice

{\large\Lambda}_{\mathtt{su}_{5}}=\left\{\boldsymbol{m}\in\mathbb{Z}^{8},\quad\mathfrak{Q}_{\mathtt{su}_{5}}=\boldsymbol{m}^{\text{{a}}}{\large g}_{\text{{ab}}}\boldsymbol{m}^{\text{{b}}}\quad|\quad{\large g}_{\text{{ab}}}=\boldsymbol{Q}_{\text{{ab}}}^{\mathtt{su}_{5}}\right\}

(158)

with

\boldsymbol{Q}_{\text{{ab}}}^{\mathtt{su}_{5}}=\left(\begin{array}[]{cc}0&I_{4}\\
I_{4}&0\end{array}\right)\qquad,\qquad\det\boldsymbol{Q}_{\text{{ab}}}^{\mathtt{su}_{5}}=+1

(159)

From equations (
152
-
156
), one also learn that for the higher rank
su(r+1) series, the determinant is of the form

\det\mathcal{K}_{su_{r+1}}=(r+1)\mathop{\displaystyle\prod}\limits_{i=1}^{r}x_{i}^{2}

(160)

which by setting
x_{i}=1
, one recovers the usual value
\det K_{su_{r+1}}=r+1
. For the general relationships of (
126
) namely
\mathcal{K}_{ij}^{\mathrm{su}_{\mathrm{r+1}}}=\sum_{k,l=1}^{r}x_{i}^{k}K_{kl}^{\mathrm{su}_{\mathrm{r+1}}}x_{j}^{l}
, we
have

\det\mathcal{K}_{su_{r+1}}=\det K_{\mathrm{su}_{\mathrm{r+1}}}\left(\det x_{i}^{k}\right)^{2}=(r+1)\left(\det x_{i}^{k}\right)^{2}

(161)

It is also worth mentioning that the genus-one partition function
of these NCFT
{}_{2}^{su_{r+1}}
is given by eqs(
146
-
147
).

The condition
\mathfrak{p}^{\text{{\@text@baccent{m}}}}{{}\mathchoice{\mbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle\mathchar 273$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\textstyle\mathchar 273$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle\mathchar 273$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle\mathchar 273$}}}_{\text{{\@text@baccent{m}\@text@baccent{n}}}}\mathfrak{p}^{\text{{\@text@baccent{n}}}}\in 2\mathbb{Z}
is solved by
the lattice

{\large\Lambda}_{\mathtt{su}_{r+1}}=\left\{\boldsymbol{m}\in\mathbb{Z}^{2r},\quad\mathfrak{Q}_{\mathtt{su}_{r+1}}=\boldsymbol{m}^{\text{{a}}}{\large g}_{\text{{ab}}}\boldsymbol{m}^{\text{{b}}}\quad|\quad{\large g}_{\text{{ab}}}=\boldsymbol{Q}_{\text{{ab}}}^{\mathtt{su}_{r+1}}\right\}

(162)

with

\boldsymbol{Q}_{\text{{ab}}}^{\mathtt{su}_{r+1}}=\left(\begin{array}[]{cc}0&I_{r}\\
I_{r}&0\end{array}\right)\qquad,\qquad\det\boldsymbol{Q}_{\text{{ab}}}^{\mathtt{su}_{r+1}}=\left(-\right)^{r}

(163)

4.2.2
Orthogonal so(2r) class: NCFT
{}_{2}^{\mathbf{so}_{2r}}

We illustrate the orthogonal class of NCFT
{}_{2}^{\mathbf{so}_{2r}}
s by studying the first two members of the family namely the rank 4 NCFT
{}_{2}^{\mathbf{so}_{8}}
and the rank 5 NCFT
{}_{2}^{\mathbf{so}_{10}}

represented by the graphics
3
.

Figure 3:
Graphic representations of the intersection matrices
\mathbf{K}^{\mathrm{so}_{8}}
and
\mathbf{K}^{\mathrm{so}_{10}}
.

•

Rank
4
Narain CFT
{}_{2}^{so_{8}}
:

For this rank 4 orthogonal Narain CFT
{}_{2}^{so_{8}}
, the intersection
matrix
\mathcal{K}_{ij}^{so_{8}}=\mathbf{{{}\mathchoice{\mbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle\mathchar 268$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\textstyle\mathchar 268$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle\mathchar 268$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle\mathchar 268$}}}}_{i}.\mathbf{{{}\mathchoice{\mbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle\mathchar 268$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\textstyle\mathchar 268$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle\mathchar 268$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle\mathchar 268$}}}}_{j},

and its dual
\mathcal{\tilde{K}}_{so_{8}}^{ij}=\mathbf{{{}\mathchoice{\mbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle\mathchar 287$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\textstyle\mathchar 287$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle\mathchar 287$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle\mathchar 287$}}}}^{i}.\mathbf{{{}\mathchoice{\mbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle\mathchar 287$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\textstyle\mathchar 287$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle\mathchar 287$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle\mathchar 287$}}}}^{j}
are given by

\mathcal{K}_{ij}^{so_{8}}=\left(\begin{array}[]{cccc}2x_{1}^{2}&-x_{1}x_{2}&0&0\\
-x_{1}x_{2}&2x_{2}^{2}&-x_{2}x_{3}&-x_{2}x_{4}\\
0&-x_{2}x_{3}&2x_{3}^{2}&0\\
0&-x_{2}x_{4}&0&2x_{4}^{2}\end{array}\right)

(164)

with determinant
4x_{1}^{2}x_{2}^{2}x_{3}^{2}x_{4}^{2}
; and

\mathcal{\tilde{K}}_{so_{8}}^{ij}=\left(\begin{array}[]{cccc}\frac{1}{x_{1}^{2}}&\frac{1}{x_{1}x_{2}}&\frac{1}{2x_{1}x_{3}}&\frac{1}{2x_{1}x_{4}}\\
\frac{1}{x_{1}x_{2}}&\frac{2}{x_{2}^{2}}&\frac{1}{x_{2}x_{3}}&\frac{1}{x_{2}x_{4}}\\
\frac{1}{2x_{1}x_{3}}&\frac{1}{x_{2}x_{3}}&\frac{1}{x_{3}^{2}}&\frac{1}{2x_{3}x_{4}}\\
\frac{1}{2x_{1}x_{4}}&\frac{1}{x_{2}x_{4}}&\frac{1}{2x_{3}x_{4}}&\frac{1}{x_{4}^{2}}\end{array}\right)

(165)

Using these matrices, the left and the right momenta follow
from (
150
) and they allow to calculate the
\mathfrak{Q}_{\mathrm{so}_{8}}
and
\mathcal{H}_{\mathrm{so}_{8}}
as well as the associated
relations such as the genus-one partition function.

•

Rank
5
Narain CFT
{}_{2}^{so_{10}}
:

For this case, we have

\mathcal{K}_{so_{10}}=\left(\begin{array}[]{ccccc}2x_{1}^{2}&-x_{1}x_{2}&0&0&0\\
-x_{1}x_{2}&2x_{2}^{2}&-x_{2}x_{3}&0&0\\
0&-x_{2}x_{3}&2x_{3}^{2}&-x_{3}x_{4}&-x_{3}x_{5}\\
0&0&-x_{3}x_{4}&2x_{4}^{2}&0\\
0&0&-x_{3}x_{5}&0&2x_{5}^{2}\end{array}\right)

(166)

with determinant
4x_{1}^{2}x_{2}^{2}x_{3}^{2}x_{4}^{2}x_{5}^{2}
; and

\mathcal{\tilde{K}}_{\mathrm{so}_{10}}=\left(\begin{array}[]{ccccc}\frac{1}{x_{1}^{2}}&\frac{1}{x_{1}x_{2}}&\frac{1}{x_{1}x_{3}}&\frac{1}{2x_{1}x_{4}}&\frac{1}{2x_{1}x_{5}}\\
\frac{1}{x_{1}x_{2}}&\frac{2}{x_{2}^{2}}&\frac{2}{x_{2}x_{3}}&\frac{1}{x_{2}x_{4}}&\frac{1}{x_{2}x_{5}}\\
\frac{1}{x_{1}x_{3}}&\frac{2}{x_{2}x_{3}}&\frac{3}{x_{3}^{2}}&\frac{3}{2x_{3}x_{4}}&\frac{3}{2x_{3}x_{5}}\\
\frac{1}{2x_{1}x_{4}}&\frac{1}{x_{2}x_{4}}&\frac{3}{2x_{3}x_{4}}&\frac{5}{4x_{4}^{2}}&\frac{3}{4x_{4}x_{5}}\\
\frac{1}{2x_{1}x_{5}}&\frac{1}{x_{2}x_{5}}&\frac{3}{2x_{3}x_{5}}&\frac{3}{4x_{4}x_{5}}&\frac{5}{4x_{5}^{2}}\end{array}\right)

(167)

From these two examples, we deduce that for higher rank r of the orthogonal
so(2r) series, the determinant is given by

\det\mathcal{K}_{so_{2r}}=4\mathop{\displaystyle\prod}\limits_{i=1}^{r}x_{i}^{2}

(168)

Analogously to the unitary series, the partition function for
orthogonal CFT’s reads as

Z_{so_{2r}}\left[{{}\mathchoice{\mbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle\mathchar 284$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\textstyle\mathchar 284$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle\mathchar 284$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle\mathchar 284$}}},\bar{{{}\mathchoice{\mbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle\mathchar 284$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\textstyle\mathchar 284$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle\mathchar 284$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle\mathchar 284$}}}};\mathbf{x}\right]=\frac{1}{\left|{{}\mathchoice{\mbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle\mathchar 273$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\textstyle\mathchar 273$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle\mathchar 273$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle\mathchar 273$}}}\left({{}\mathchoice{\mbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle\mathchar 284$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\textstyle\mathchar 284$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle\mathchar 284$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle\mathchar 284$}}}\right)\right|^{2r}}\mathop{\displaystyle\sum}\limits_{\boldsymbol{m}\in\mathbb{Z}^{2}}\exp\left(\boldsymbol{m}^{T}.\boldsymbol{D}_{so_{2r}}.\boldsymbol{m}\right)

(169)

with

\boldsymbol{D}_{so_{2r}}=i{{}\mathchoice{\mbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle\mathchar 281$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\textstyle\mathchar 281$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle\mathchar 281$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle\mathchar 281$}}}\left({{}\mathchoice{\mbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle\mathchar 284$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\textstyle\mathchar 284$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle\mathchar 284$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle\mathchar 284$}}}\boldsymbol{Q}_{L}^{\mathrm{so}_{2r}}-\bar{{{}\mathchoice{\mbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle\mathchar 284$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\textstyle\mathchar 284$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle\mathchar 284$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle\mathchar 284$}}}}\boldsymbol{Q}_{R}^{\mathrm{so}_{2r}}\right)

(170)

reading as follows

\boldsymbol{D}_{so_{2r}}=i{{}\mathchoice{\mbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle\mathchar 281$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\textstyle\mathchar 281$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle\mathchar 281$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle\mathchar 281$}}}{{}\mathchoice{\mbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle\mathchar 284$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\textstyle\mathchar 284$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle\mathchar 284$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle\mathchar 284$}}}_{1}\boldsymbol{Q}_{so_{2r}}-{{}\mathchoice{\mbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle\mathchar 281$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\textstyle\mathchar 281$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle\mathchar 281$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle\mathchar 281$}}}{{}\mathchoice{\mbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle\mathchar 284$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\textstyle\mathchar 284$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle\mathchar 284$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle\mathchar 284$}}}_{2}\boldsymbol{H}_{so_{2r}}

(171)

This analysis extends straightforwardly to the other members of the
orthogonal NCFT
{}_{2}^{so_{2r}}
family; report to appendix B for
useful properties.

For these orthogonal CFT’s, the condition
\mathfrak{p}^{\text{{\@text@baccent{m}}}}{{}\mathchoice{\mbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle\mathchar 273$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\textstyle\mathchar 273$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle\mathchar 273$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle\mathchar 273$}}}_{\text{{\@text@baccent{m}\@text@baccent{n}}}}\mathfrak{p}^{\text{{\@text@baccent{n}}}}\in 2\mathbb{Z}
is solved by the lattice

{\large\Lambda}_{so_{2r}}=\left\{\boldsymbol{m}\in\mathbb{Z}^{2r},\quad\mathfrak{Q}_{so_{2r}}=\boldsymbol{m}^{\text{{a}}}{\large g}_{\text{{ab}}}\boldsymbol{m}^{\text{{b}}}\quad|\quad{\large g}_{\text{{ab}}}=\boldsymbol{Q}_{\text{{ab}}}^{\mathtt{so}_{2r}}\right\}

(172)

with

\boldsymbol{Q}_{\text{{ab}}}^{\mathtt{so}_{2r}}=\left(\begin{array}[]{cc}0&I_{r}\\
I_{r}&0\end{array}\right)\qquad,\qquad\det\boldsymbol{Q}_{\text{{ab}}}^{\mathtt{so}_{2r}}=\left(-\right)^{r}

(173)

It should be noted that the difference between unitary and
orthogonal Narain CFTs lies in the matrix
\boldsymbol{H}_{\text{{ab}}}^{\mathtt{so}_{2r}}
which differs from
\boldsymbol{H}_{\text{{ab}}}^{su_{r+1}}
.

4.2.3
Exceptional class: NCFT
{}_{2}^{\mathbf{e}_{r}}

We illustrate the exceptional class containing three members by giving the
data of the rank 6 conformal theory NCFT
{}_{2}^{\mathbf{e}_{6}}
represented
by the graphic
4
.

Figure 4:
Graphic representation of the intersection matrix
\mathbf{K}^{\mathrm{E}_{6}}
.

For this E
6
conformal theory, the basic results are captured by the
intersection matrix
\mathcal{K}_{e_{6}}
of the
\mathbf{{{}\mathchoice{\mbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle\mathchar 268$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\textstyle\mathchar 268$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle\mathchar 268$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle\mathchar 268$}}}}_{i}
-cycles;

\mathcal{K}_{e_{6}}=\left(\begin{array}[]{cccccc}2x_{1}^{2}&-x_{1}x_{2}&0&0&0&0\\
-x_{1}x_{2}&2x_{2}^{2}&-x_{2}x_{3}&0&0&0\\
0&-x_{2}x_{3}&2x_{3}^{2}&-x_{3}x_{4}&0&-x_{3}x_{6}\\
0&0&-x_{3}x_{4}&2x_{4}^{2}&-x_{4}x_{5}&0\\
0&0&0&-x_{4}x_{5}&2x_{5}^{2}&0\\
0&0&-x_{3}x_{6}&0&0&2x_{6}^{2}\end{array}\right)

(174)

with determinant
3x_{1}^{2}x_{2}^{2}x_{3}^{2}x_{4}^{2}x_{5}^{2}x_{6}^{2}
.
Its inverse gives the intersection of the dual cycles; it reads as follows

\mathcal{\tilde{K}}_{e_{6}}=\left(\begin{array}[]{cccccc}\frac{4}{3x_{1}^{2}}&\frac{5}{3x_{1}x_{2}}&\frac{2}{x_{1}x_{3}}&\frac{4}{3x_{1}x_{4}}&\frac{2}{3x_{1}x_{5}}&\frac{1}{x_{1}x_{6}}\\
\frac{5}{3x_{1}x_{2}}&\frac{10}{3x_{2}^{2}}&\frac{4}{x_{2}x_{3}}&\frac{8}{3x_{2}x_{4}}&\frac{4}{3x_{2}x_{5}}&\frac{2}{x_{2}x_{6}}\\
\frac{2}{x_{1}x_{3}}&\frac{4}{x_{2}x_{3}}&\frac{6}{x_{3}^{2}}&\frac{4}{x_{3}x_{4}}&\frac{2}{x_{3}x_{5}}&\frac{3}{x_{3}x_{6}}\\
\frac{4}{3x_{1}x_{4}}&\frac{8}{3x_{2}x_{4}}&\frac{4}{x_{3}x_{4}}&\frac{10}{3x_{4}^{2}}&\frac{5}{3x_{4}x_{5}}&\frac{2}{x_{4}x_{6}}\\
\frac{2}{3x_{1}x_{5}}&\frac{4}{3x_{2}x_{5}}&\frac{2}{x_{3}x_{5}}&\frac{5}{3x_{4}x_{5}}&\frac{4}{3x_{5}^{2}}&\frac{1}{x_{5}x_{6}}\\
\frac{1}{x_{1}x_{6}}&\frac{2}{x_{2}x_{6}}&\frac{3}{x_{3}x_{6}}&\frac{2}{x_{4}x_{6}}&\frac{1}{x_{5}x_{6}}&\frac{2}{x_{6}^{2}}\end{array}\right)

(175)

For these exceptional CFT’s, the condition
\mathfrak{p}^{\text{{\@text@baccent{m}}}}{{}\mathchoice{\mbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle\mathchar 273$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\textstyle\mathchar 273$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle\mathchar 273$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle\mathchar 273$}}}_{\text{{\@text@baccent{m}\@text@baccent{n}}}}\mathfrak{p}^{\text{{\@text@baccent{n}}}}\in 2\mathbb{Z}
is solved by the lattice

{\large\Lambda}_{\mathtt{e}_{r}}=\left\{\boldsymbol{m}\in\mathbb{Z}^{2r},\quad\mathfrak{Q}_{\mathtt{e}_{r}}=\boldsymbol{m}^{\text{{a}}}{\large g}_{\text{{ab}}}\boldsymbol{m}^{\text{{b}}}\quad|\quad{\large g}_{\text{{ab}}}=\boldsymbol{Q}_{\text{{ab}}}^{\mathbf{e}_{r}}\right\}

(176)

with
r=6,7,8
and

\boldsymbol{Q}_{\text{{ab}}}^{\mathbf{e}_{r}}=\left(\begin{array}[]{cc}0&I_{r}\\
I_{r}&0\end{array}\right)\qquad,\qquad\det\boldsymbol{Q}_{\text{{ab}}}^{\mathbf{e}_{r}}=\left(-\right)^{r}

(177)

as well as moduli dependent matrix
\boldsymbol{H}_{\mathbf{e}_{r}}
.

Using the construction of previous section, the genus-one partition function

Z_{e_{6}}
for orthogonal Narain CFT
{}^{e_{6}}
reads as follows

Z_{e_{6}}\left[{{}\mathchoice{\mbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle\mathchar 284$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\textstyle\mathchar 284$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle\mathchar 284$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle\mathchar 284$}}},\bar{{{}\mathchoice{\mbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle\mathchar 284$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\textstyle\mathchar 284$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle\mathchar 284$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle\mathchar 284$}}}};x\right]=\frac{1}{\left|{{}\mathchoice{\mbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle\mathchar 273$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\textstyle\mathchar 273$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle\mathchar 273$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle\mathchar 273$}}}\left({{}\mathchoice{\mbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle\mathchar 284$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\textstyle\mathchar 284$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle\mathchar 284$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle\mathchar 284$}}}\right)\right|^{12}}\mathop{\displaystyle\sum}\limits_{\boldsymbol{m}\in\mathbb{Z}^{2}}\exp\left(\boldsymbol{m}^{T}.\boldsymbol{E}_{e_{6}}.\boldsymbol{m}\right)

(178)

with

\boldsymbol{E}_{e_{6}}=i{{}\mathchoice{\mbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle\mathchar 281$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\textstyle\mathchar 281$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle\mathchar 281$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle\mathchar 281$}}}\left({{}\mathchoice{\mbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle\mathchar 284$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\textstyle\mathchar 284$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle\mathchar 284$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle\mathchar 284$}}}\boldsymbol{Q}_{L}^{\mathrm{e}_{6}}-\bar{{{}\mathchoice{\mbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle\mathchar 284$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\textstyle\mathchar 284$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle\mathchar 284$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle\mathchar 284$}}}}\boldsymbol{Q}_{R}^{\mathrm{e}_{6}}\right)

(179)

reading also as

\boldsymbol{E}_{e_{6}}=i{{}\mathchoice{\mbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle\mathchar 281$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\textstyle\mathchar 281$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle\mathchar 281$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle\mathchar 281$}}}{{}\mathchoice{\mbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle\mathchar 284$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\textstyle\mathchar 284$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle\mathchar 284$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle\mathchar 284$}}}_{1}\boldsymbol{Q}_{e_{6}}-{{}\mathchoice{\mbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle\mathchar 281$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\textstyle\mathchar 281$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle\mathchar 281$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle\mathchar 281$}}}{{}\mathchoice{\mbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle\mathchar 284$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\textstyle\mathchar 284$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle\mathchar 284$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle\mathchar 284$}}}_{2}\boldsymbol{H}_{e_{6}}

(180)

Similar analysis can be replicated for the two other members of the
exceptional class namely E
7
and E
8
.

5
Conclusion and comments

In this paper, we developed a proposal with the aim of classifying a family of
Narain sigma-models with target torus
\mathbb{T}^{r}
describing CFT
2
’s with central charges
(\mathrm{c}_{L},\mathrm{c}_{R})=(\mathrm{r},\mathrm{r})
. Exploiting this result, we further provided a systematic
classification of the ensemble averaging of these CFTs over their moduli
space. Our findings revealed that this family of Narain CFTs can be indeed realised
in terms of the finite dimensional Lie algebras with rank r underlying the
level k=1 affine symmetry known to live at the boundary of the 3D bulk
gravity. This allowed us to give explicit realisations of the Zamolodchikov metric of the moduli space of these CFTs in terms of the associated Cartan matrix and its inverse.

To implement our proposal, we began by analysing the standard NCFT
on a circle
\mathbb{S}^{1}
with radius R and central charge
\mathrm{c}_{L/R}=1
and showed that it can be described using data on the
su(2) algebra and its representations. In this regard, we found
that this simple NCFT
{}_{2}^{\mathbf{su}_{2}}
can be concretely
constructed in terms of the SU(2) symmetry (concisely
\left.SU(2)_{k}\right|_{L/R}
with level
k=1
) and its representations
\mathcal{R}_{\mathbf{su}_{2}}.
The Kaluza-Klein modes {n} of the
compactified string are shown to reside in the root lattice
\Lambda_{r}^{\mathbf{su}_{2}}=\mathbb{Z}\mathbf{{{}\mathchoice{\mbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle\mathchar 267$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\textstyle\mathchar 267$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle\mathchar 267$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle\mathchar 267$}}}}
, generated by the
simple root
\textstyle\mathchar 267
of su(2); while its windings {w} are hosted
by the weight lattice
\Lambda_{w}^{\mathbf{su}_{2}}=\mathbb{Z}\mathbf{{{}\mathchoice{\mbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle\mathchar 277$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\textstyle\mathchar 277$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle\mathchar 277$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle\mathchar 277$}}}}
generated by the fundamental weight
\textstyle\mathchar 277
. Using
the fibration property
SU(2)\simeq\mathbb{S}^{2}\times\mathbb{S}^{1}
,
the coordinate variable of the one dimensional moduli space
\mathcal{M}_{\mathbf{su}_{2}}
of the NCFT
{}_{2}^{\mathbf{su}_{2}}
, describing the size
of the circle
\mathbb{S}^{1}
; has been also represented in terms
of the size of the base 2-cycle
\mathbb{S}^{2}
. As a consequence of this
su(2) based realisation, the Hamiltonian
\mathcal{H}_{\mathbf{su}_{2}}=p_{L}^{2}+p_{R}^{2}
is interpreted as a sum of self
intersection of 2-cycles namely
\mathcal{C}_{\mathbf{{{}\mathchoice{\mbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle\mathchar 268$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\textstyle\mathchar 268$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle\mathchar 268$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle\mathchar 268$}}}}}^{2}+\mathcal{C}_{\mathbf{{{}\mathchoice{\mbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle\mathchar 287$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\textstyle\mathchar 287$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle\mathchar 287$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle\mathchar 287$}}}}}^{2}
which turn out to be equal to
(2x^{2}n^{2}+\frac{w^{2}}{2x^{2}})
with real variable x as in the text. Similarly, the
angular momentum
\mathfrak{Q}_{\mathbf{su}_{2}}=p_{L}^{2}-p_{R}^{2}
is
expressed by the cycle-intersection
\mathcal{C}_{\mathbf{{{}\mathchoice{\mbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle\mathchar 268$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\textstyle\mathchar 268$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle\mathchar 268$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle\mathchar 268$}}}}}.\mathcal{C}_{\mathbf{{{}\mathchoice{\mbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle\mathchar 287$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\textstyle\mathchar 287$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle\mathchar 287$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle\mathchar 287$}}}}}
and is interpreted as the gap energy
between KK {n} and windings {w}. Moreover, the genus-one partition
function Z
{}_{\mathbf{su}_{2}}\left({{}\mathchoice{\mbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle\mathchar 284$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\textstyle\mathchar 284$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle\mathchar 284$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle\mathchar 284$}}},\bar{{{}\mathchoice{\mbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle\mathchar 284$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\textstyle\mathchar 284$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle\mathchar 284$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle\mathchar 284$}}}},R\right)
and the
underlying Siegel-Narain theta function
{{}\mathchoice{\mbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle\mathchar 291$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\textstyle\mathchar 291$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle\mathchar 291$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle\mathchar 291$}}}_{\mathbf{su}_{2}}\left({{}\mathchoice{\mbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle\mathchar 284$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\textstyle\mathchar 284$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle\mathchar 284$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle\mathchar 284$}}},\bar{{{}\mathchoice{\mbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle\mathchar 284$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\textstyle\mathchar 284$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle\mathchar 284$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle\mathchar 284$}}}},R\right)
are given by a discrete sum over the
U(1)_{L}\times U\left(1\right)_{R}
charges belonging to an even integer
lattice
\Lambda_{Q}^{\mathbf{su}_{2}}\subset\Lambda_{w}^{\mathbf{su}_{2}}
characterised by the quadratic form
Q=\sum\boldsymbol{m}^{\text{{a}}}\mathbf{Q}_{\text{{ab}}}\boldsymbol{m}^{\text{{b}}}
taking values in 2
\mathbb{Z}
.

Building on this NCFT
{}_{2}^{\mathbf{su}_{2}}
theory,
we proceeded to complete the classification of Narain theories
by incorporating higher values of
\mathrm{c}_{L/R}
by leveraging the fact that rank r finite Lie algebras
\mathbf{g}
can be
conceptualised as intersecting building blocks made of r copies of
su(2). Geometrically, this feature corresponds to r intersecting copies of
3-spheres
\mathbb{S}_{i}^{3}
and, due to the Hofp fibration, to
the intersection of r circles
\mathbb{S}_{i}^{1}
in the fiber and r
intersecting 2-spheres
\mathbb{S}_{i}^{2}
in the base. Within this
picture, we obtained a new way to parameterise the quantized left p
L

and right p
L
momentums of the NCFT as follows

p_{L}=\sum_{i,k=1}^{r}\left(n^{i}x_{i}^{k}\mathbf{{{}\mathchoice{\mbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle\mathchar 267$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\textstyle\mathchar 267$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle\mathchar 267$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle\mathchar 267$}}}}_{k}+w_{i}y_{k}^{i}\mathbf{{{}\mathchoice{\mbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle\mathchar 277$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\textstyle\mathchar 277$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle\mathchar 277$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle\mathchar 277$}}}}^{k}\right)\quad,\quad p_{R}=\sum_{i,k=1}^{r}\left(n^{i}x_{i}^{k}\mathbf{{{}\mathchoice{\mbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle\mathchar 267$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\textstyle\mathchar 267$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle\mathchar 267$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle\mathchar 267$}}}}_{k}-w_{i}y_{k}^{i}\mathbf{{{}\mathchoice{\mbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle\mathchar 277$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\textstyle\mathchar 277$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle\mathchar 277$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle\mathchar 277$}}}}^{k}\right)

(181)

where the
\mathbf{{{}\mathchoice{\mbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle\mathchar 267$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\textstyle\mathchar 267$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle\mathchar 267$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle\mathchar 267$}}}}_{k}
’s are the simple (co-) roots of the Lie
algebra
\mathbf{g}
and the
\mathbf{{{}\mathchoice{\mbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle\mathchar 277$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\textstyle\mathchar 277$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle\mathchar 277$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle\mathchar 277$}}}}^{k}
’s its fundamental
weights in addition to the integers
\left\{n^{i}\right\}
and
\left\{w_{i}\right\}
defining the KK and winding modes.
The variables
x_{i}^{k}
and their dual
y_{k}^{i}=\left(x^{-1}\right)_{k}^{i}
provide the coordinates of the moduli space
\mathcal{M}_{\mathbf{g}}
(with
\dim\mathcal{M}_{\mathbf{g}}=r^{2}
) of the NCFT
{}_{2}^{\mathbf{g}};
the constraint relation
x_{i}^{k}y_{k}^{j}={{}\mathchoice{\mbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle\mathchar 270$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\textstyle\mathchar 270$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle\mathchar 270$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle\mathchar 270$}}}_{j}^{j}

follows from the root/weight duality relation
\mathbf{{{}\mathchoice{\mbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle\mathchar 267$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\textstyle\mathchar 267$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle\mathchar 267$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle\mathchar 267$}}}}_{i}^{\vee}.\mathbf{{{}\mathchoice{\mbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle\mathchar 277$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\textstyle\mathchar 277$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle\mathchar 277$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle\mathchar 277$}}}}^{j}={{}\mathchoice{\mbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle\mathchar 270$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\textstyle\mathchar 270$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle\mathchar 270$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle\mathchar 270$}}}_{j}^{j}.
By using this coordinate system, we
have also given an explicit realisation of the Zamolodchikov metric of the
moduli space in terms of Cartan matrix
K_{\mathbf{g}}
of the Lie algebras
and its inverse
K_{\mathbf{g}}^{-1}
. In this regard, we showed that
\mathcal{H}_{\mathbf{g}}=p_{L}^{2}+p_{R}^{2}
is a function of the
coordinate
x_{i}^{k}
of the moduli space reading like

\mathcal{H}_{\mathbf{g}}=\sum n^{i}\left(x_{i}^{k}K_{kl}^{\mathbf{g}}x_{j}^{l}\right)n^{j}+\sum w_{i}\left(y_{k}^{i}\tilde{K}_{\mathbf{g}}^{kl}y_{l}^{j}\right)w_{j}

(182)

However, the even integer quadratic form
\mathfrak{Q}_{\mathbf{g}}=p_{L}^{2}-p_{R}^{2}
is independent of
x_{i}^{k};
it is given by
2\mathbf{\vec{n}.\vec{w}}
and it vanishes for
\mathbf{\vec{n}\bot\vec{w}}
. The Zamolodchikov metric
ds_{\mathbf{g}}^{2}
of the moduli space of NCFT
{}_{2}^{\mathbf{g}}
is given by the trace
(d{{}\mathchoice{\mbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle\mathchar 285$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\textstyle\mathchar 285$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle\mathchar 285$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle\mathchar 285$}}}_{\mathbf{g}})_{j}^{i}(d{{}\mathchoice{\mbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle\mathchar 285$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\textstyle\mathchar 285$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle\mathchar 285$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle\mathchar 285$}}}_{\mathbf{g}})_{i}^{j}
with matrix 1-form as follows

\left(d{{}\mathchoice{\mbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle\mathchar 285$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\textstyle\mathchar 285$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle\mathchar 285$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle\mathchar 285$}}}_{\mathbf{g}}\right)_{j}^{i}=\sum_{k=1}^{r}\left(\mathcal{K}_{\mathbf{g}}^{-1}\right)^{ik}\left(d\mathcal{K}_{\mathbf{g}}\right)_{kj}

(183)

As illustrations of our proposal, we presented several examples: we
first considered low dimensional Lie algebras
\mathbf{g}
with rank
r=1,2

(namely su
{}_{2},
su
{}_{3},
so
{}_{5},
G
2
); see sections 2 and
3. And then, we gave representatives of higher ranked symmetries
sitting within the families of ADE Lie algebras (su
{}_{r+1},
so
{}_{8},
so
{}_{10},
e
6
); see section 4. Along with this description, we
also provided results on the ensemble averaging of these
NCFT
{}_{2}^{\mathbf{g}}
s by studying the properties of the Siegel-Narain
theta function and averaging of the partition function
\mathcal{Z}_{\mathbf{g}}\left[{{}\mathchoice{\mbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle\mathchar 284$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\textstyle\mathchar 284$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle\mathchar 284$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle\mathchar 284$}}},\bar{{{}\mathchoice{\mbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle\mathchar 284$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\textstyle\mathchar 284$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle\mathchar 284$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle\mathchar 284$}}}}\right]
.

To conclude this study, we find it relevant to discuss the r
\times
s- dimensional moduli space
\mathcal{M}^{(s,r)}=\mathcal{O}(s,r;\mathbb{Z})\backslash\mathcal{O}(s,r;\mathbb{R})/\mathcal{O}(s;\mathbb{R})\times\mathcal{O}(r;\mathbb{R})
of the so-called generalised Narain CFTs and
their ensemble average. These are extended conformal field theories having
distinct central charges
\mathrm{c}_{L}=\mathrm{s}
and
\mathrm{c}_{R}=\mathrm{r};
say
\mathrm{s>r}
. This generalisation labeled as GNCFT
(s,r)
could, roughly speaking, be derived from NCFT
(r,r)
by
deforming the left
\boldsymbol{p}_{L}^{2}
by a positive quantity while
fixing
\boldsymbol{p}_{R}^{2}
as follows

\boldsymbol{p}_{L}^{2}\rightarrow\boldsymbol{p}_{L}^{2}+\mathbf{{{}\mathchoice{\mbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle\mathchar 281$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\textstyle\mathchar 281$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle\mathchar 281$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle\mathchar 281$}}}}_{L}^{2}+{{}\mathchoice{\mbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle\mathchar 273$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\textstyle\mathchar 273$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle\mathchar 273$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle\mathchar 273$}}}\boldsymbol{p}.\mathbf{{{}\mathchoice{\mbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle\mathchar 281$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\textstyle\mathchar 281$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle\mathchar 281$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle\mathchar 281$}}}}_{L}\qquad,\qquad\boldsymbol{p}_{R}^{2}\rightarrow\boldsymbol{p}_{R}^{2}

(184)

with some number
\textstyle\mathchar 273
that we set to zero in the rest of this
discussion. In this shift, the extra term
\mathbf{{{}\mathchoice{\mbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle\mathchar 281$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\textstyle\mathchar 281$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle\mathchar 281$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle\mathchar 281$}}}}_{L}^{2}
is required to be a quadratic form that can be thought of like
\sum_{\text{{\.{a}}},\text{{\.{b}}=1}}^{{{}\mathchoice{\mbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle\mathchar 279$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\textstyle\mathchar 279$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle\mathchar 279$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle\mathchar 279$}}}}\tilde{m}^{\text{{\.{a}}}}\mathfrak{M}_{\text{{\.{a}\.{b}}}}\tilde{m}^{\text{{\.{b}}}}
with
\textstyle\mathchar 279
integers {
\tilde{m}^{\text{{\.{a}}}}
}
{}_{1\leq\text{{\.{a}}}\leq{{}\mathchoice{\mbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle\mathchar 279$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\textstyle\mathchar 279$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle\mathchar 279$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle\mathchar 279$}}}}
and matrix
\mathfrak{M}_{\text{{\.{a}\.{b}}}}
encoding dissymmetric data in the left sector that violate the
left/right correspondence of Narain theories. Under this shift, the
quadratic form
\mathcal{H}_{\mathbf{g}}
gets modified like
\left(p_{L}^{2}+p_{R}^{2}\right)+\mathbf{{{}\mathchoice{\mbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle\mathchar 281$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\textstyle\mathchar 281$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle\mathchar 281$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle\mathchar 281$}}}}_{L}^{2}
and the
\mathfrak{Q}_{\mathbf{g}}
becomes
\mathfrak{\tilde{Q}}=\left(p_{L}^{2}-p_{R}^{2}\right)+\mathbf{{{}\mathchoice{\mbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle\mathchar 281$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\textstyle\mathchar 281$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle\mathchar 281$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle\mathchar 281$}}}}_{L}^{2}.
For
\mathfrak{\tilde{Q}}
to describe a
generalised NCFT, it must be
\left(\mathbf{i}\right)
moduli independent;
thus requiring
\mathfrak{M}_{\text{{\.{a}\.{b}}}}
to be a constant
matrix, and
\left(\mathbf{ii}\right)
an even integer form requiring
\mathbf{{{}\mathchoice{\mbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle\mathchar 281$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\textstyle\mathchar 281$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle\mathchar 281$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle\mathchar 281$}}}}_{L}^{2}\in 2\mathbb{Z}
. Typical examples of the
\left(2r+{{}\mathchoice{\mbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle\mathchar 279$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\textstyle\mathchar 279$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle\mathchar 279$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle\mathchar 279$}}}\right)\times 2\left(r+{{}\mathchoice{\mbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle\mathchar 279$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\textstyle\mathchar 279$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle\mathchar 279$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle\mathchar 279$}}}\right)
matrix of the deformed
\mathfrak{\tilde{Q}}
are given by

\mathbf{\tilde{Q}}=\left(\begin{array}[]{cc}\mathbf{Q}_{\text{{ab}}}&0\\
0&\mathfrak{M}_{\text{{\.{a}\.{b}}}}\end{array}\right)\qquad,\qquad\mathfrak{M}_{\text{{\.{a}\.{b}}}}=\left(\begin{array}[]{cc}0&I_{{{}\mathchoice{\mbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle\mathchar 279$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\textstyle\mathchar 279$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle\mathchar 279$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle\mathchar 279$}}}\times{{}\mathchoice{\mbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle\mathchar 279$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\textstyle\mathchar 279$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle\mathchar 279$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle\mathchar 279$}}}}\\
I_{{{}\mathchoice{\mbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle\mathchar 279$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\textstyle\mathchar 279$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle\mathchar 279$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle\mathchar 279$}}}\times{{}\mathchoice{\mbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle\mathchar 279$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\textstyle\mathchar 279$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle\mathchar 279$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle\mathchar 279$}}}}&0\end{array}\right)

(185)

with
\mathfrak{M}_{\text{{\.{a}\.{b}}}}
having analogous features
as
\mathbf{Q}_{\text{{ab}}}
. In this regard, one can write down
several examples of possible
\mathfrak{M}_{\text{{\.{a}\.{b}}}}
;
for instance the two simple following:
\left(a\right)
for generic
integer
{{}\mathchoice{\mbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle\mathchar 279$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\textstyle\mathchar 279$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle\mathchar 279$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle\mathchar 279$}}},
and
\left(b\right)
for even integer
{{}\mathchoice{\mbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle\mathchar 279$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\textstyle\mathchar 279$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle\mathchar 279$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle\mathchar 279$}}}=2{{}\mathchoice{\mbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle\mathchar 279$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\textstyle\mathchar 279$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle\mathchar 279$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle\mathchar 279$}}}^{\prime}
as follows;

\left(a\right):\mathfrak{M}_{\text{{\.{a}\.{b}}}}=\left(\begin{array}[]{ccc}2m_{1}&&\\
&\ddots&\\
&&2m_{{{}\mathchoice{\mbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle\mathchar 279$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\textstyle\mathchar 279$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle\mathchar 279$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle\mathchar 279$}}}}\end{array}\right)\qquad,\qquad\left(b\right):\mathfrak{M}_{\text{{\.{a}\.{b}}}}=\left(\begin{array}[]{cc}0&I_{{{}\mathchoice{\mbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle\mathchar 279$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\textstyle\mathchar 279$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle\mathchar 279$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle\mathchar 279$}}}^{\prime}\times{{}\mathchoice{\mbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle\mathchar 279$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\textstyle\mathchar 279$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle\mathchar 279$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle\mathchar 279$}}}^{\prime}}\\
I_{{{}\mathchoice{\mbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle\mathchar 279$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\textstyle\mathchar 279$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle\mathchar 279$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle\mathchar 279$}}}^{\prime}\times{{}\mathchoice{\mbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle\mathchar 279$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\textstyle\mathchar 279$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle\mathchar 279$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle\mathchar 279$}}}^{\prime}}&0\end{array}\right)

(186)

We suspect that these generalised NCFTs might be also classified by Lie
algebras, though we have not yet developed rigourous realisations
of such type of deformations nor their classification using Lie algebras

g
while insuring the covering of all moduli space
dimensions; i.e:
\dim\mathcal{M}^{(s,r)}=r\times s.
Progress in this
direction will be reported in a future occasion.

Appendices

We give three appendices: Appendix A discusses the ensemble average of
Narain conformal field theories and their bulk dual in relation with our
classification proposal. Appendix B examines useful properties of the root
\Lambda_{r}^{\mathbf{g}}
and the weight
\Lambda_{w}^{\mathbf{g}}

lattices of finite dimensional Lie algebras
\mathbf{g}
. In appendix C, we
derive the metric of the moduli space
\mathcal{M}_{\mathbf{g}}
and give
explicit calculations regarding the averaging of genus-one partition
function Z
{}_{\mathbf{g}}^{\text{{ncft}}}
to highlight the
footprint of
\mathbf{g}
and representations
\mathcal{R}_{\mathbf{g}}
.

Appendix A
Narain CFTs and gravitational dual

The genus-one partition functions Z
{}_{\mathbf{g}}^{\text{{cft}}}
of
the Narain CFTs have been considered in the main text in link with the
classification in terms of
\mathbf{g}
. In this appendix, we give
properties of its averaging over the moduli space,
